# THE MERGE, 6 Sep 2026 - Kathy's work into the integration branch, before the shoot

## THE DAY'S LESSON, filed above the defects (ruling, 9 Sep 16:0x): REACH THE TARGET STATE DIRECTLY
Every defect of 9 Sep had one shape: a transient intermediate state that something else observed before the correction
landed.
    - the mix budget committed AFTER the engage decision had already read it (Build B: re-evaluate on commit)
    - the mute snapshot read between the command and the sidecar (the wrong-Link lamp, the incoherent snapshot)
    - a re-minted registry row read as dead before it had been observed climbing (the uid gate, C4)
    - a plan slot attached un-bypassed and bypassed a moment later, rendered live in the gap (the 15:31 crash)
THE RULE: reach the target state directly; never pass through a wrong one and correct it. A state that is wrong for one
block, one poll or one observation is wrong, because something observes every state - the audio thread, a sibling's
poll, a lamp, a gate. "It gets fixed on the next tick" is the description of the defect, not of a fix.
How to apply: when a change has a target state, compute the target first and write it once (the attach carries the
lease's bypass state; the decision reads the committed budget; the row is not judged until time has passed). Where a
sequence of writes is unavoidable, the reader must see either the old state or the new one, never the middle
(publish under one revision; swap, don't mutate). Any design that says "then we correct it" is the shape to refuse.
The reviewer's crossfade hypothesis for the crash was refuted on three independent facts and withdrawn - the sixth
this round (row-position, budget-never-commits, yellow-means-borrow, downstream substitution, placement-rank, the
solo crossfade). Same failure shape every time: change the model, not the rule.

### The restore-path setSlotWet calls (the reviewer's expected bite): examined, not changed
All three carry WetSource::Restore and Kathy wrote every one in aa455ff:
LinkProcessor.cpp:2130 (the rack restore callback), :2266 (setChainSlotWet),
ChainHost.cpp:7250 (restoreNextSlot's callback). ChainHost::setSlotWet refuses
ONLY Assistant under do-not-dial ("a Restore is the session's saved value and
is not a change either"). Nothing to change, nothing to ask.
NOTE FOR KATHY: setChainSlotWet is also what the Link editor's wet knob calls
(LinkEditor.h:787), so a HAND MOVE is labelled Restore. Behaviour: identical
(neither User nor Restore is refused). Attribution: the move log's authorship
does NOT come from WetSource - a local edit is stamped by stampLocalRackEdit()
(LinkProcessor.h:541), which setChainSlotWet always calls and the restore
callback never does - so a user's wet move is still recorded as a local edit.
The vocabulary is wrong at that site; the record is not. Hers to relabel.
Also traced: the Link's restore callback re-applies the slot's structured
settings (setSlotStructuredSettings, :2129) BEFORE restoring the state blob
(:2135); for a built-in that re-dial ends in applyStructuredToBuiltinSlot
(Assistant) and is refused under do-not-dial - then the blob restore (Restore)
lands, the same final values as today where the blob overwrites the re-dial.
The main plugin's applyRestoredParams writes hosted parameters through JUCE's
setValueNotifyingHost, unguarded. Neither is a loss.

### THE AU/VST3 BUNDLES ABOVE ARE STALE - re-gate before install
The AAX reconfigure (below) added JucePlugin_Build_AAX to the shared archive's
defines and recompiled the WHOLE archive. The AU/VST3 bundles the gate set ran
against are therefore not the ones an install would write; they are relinked
and RE-GATED (suite, load x4, impulse, renders) before anything goes to
~/Library. A gate result belongs to the binary it was run against.

### The flags, as conclusions (RELEASE.md section 2 vs Eden 6.0.1 `sign --help`)
    --verbose  present as -V/--verbose, same meaning (verbose output); optional
    --account  present as -a/--account, same meaning (the developer account for the server); needed for any server operation (sign with a wcguid talks to the server)
    --wcguid   present as -G/--wcguid, same meaning (the wrap config to use); sign requires EITHER a wcguid/wcfile OR --customernumber + --customername (Eden 6 help: those two "will be required for the sign operation if no wrap config GUID or file is provided") - so with a wcguid nothing new is required
    --signid   present as -I/--signid, same meaning (the Mac signing identity, searched in the keychains)
    --in       present as -i/--in, same meaning
    --out      present as -o/--out, same meaning; optional, in-place when omitted
    --password present as -p/--password (or --pswd-no-save); NOT in RELEASE.md. Without it and without a TTY wraptool errors "You must specify a password for your account" rather than prompting (seen on `info` and `verify` today). From a Terminal it is expected to prompt; if it errors instead, Sean adds --pswd-no-save and types the password himself
    NO flag renamed, none changed meaning, none gained a required companion when a wcguid is given.

### THE WCGUID - the critical path, settled only from PACE Central
The July bundles (the ones Pro Tools accepted) were wrapped by Eden 5.10.5 on
16 Jul 2026 12:34:03 (their __Pace_Eden.bundle Info.plist and codesign.dsig).
The GUIDs found, by file and by kind:
    Contents/__Pace_Eden.bundle/Contents/Resources/Signatures/codesign.dsig, in BOTH bundles:
      542E23D8-B22D-5C07-05B7-AE52AB531AF6   labelled "publisher"  ("Signature Recording Studios LTD")
      7A5552D0-2F4F-11F1-B337-00505692AD3E   labelled "product"    ("Echojay")
      two lowercase v4 GUIDs per bundle, different per bundle, unlabelled - kind unknown
    RELEASE.md's wcguid B4184F90-2F4F-11F1-A9B9-00505692C25A appears NOWHERE in either bundle.
So the bundle does not record its wrap-config GUID at all; the one PACE-format
GUID it carries is the PRODUCT GUID, not a wcguid, and the doc's value is
neither confirmed nor contradicted by the bundle. It CANNOT be established
from this machine. A wrong wcguid signs and then fails Pro Tools validation,
so no sign command is run on a candidate: Sean reads the wrap configuration
GUID from PACE Central (the button inside iLok License Manager), compares it
with the doc's value, and only a match - or the PACE Central value itself -
goes into the command. If PACE Central shows no usable wrap configuration
for Eden 6 that is a PACE support ticket, Pro Tools is not happening today,
and the shoot switches to Logic.
wraptool verify and info both need the iLok credential too (today: "You must
specify a password" / "MissingFusionToolsLicense"), so verification is also
Sean's to run, paired with `codesign -dv --verbose=4`.
NOTARIZATION: skipped, deliberately. The shoot build will be PACE-signed and
Developer ID signed but NOT notarized - for Sean's own Mac only; do not ship it.

### V9 RACK - RESULT (6 Sep 2026, 15:17, binary 02dcd8a as installed): PASS
tools/merge_gate_tests/v9_rack_restore_test.cpp, log in results_2026-09-06/v9_rack_restore_02dcd8a.log.
The real path: EchoJayProcessor::getStateInformation (2802 bytes) -> a fresh
EchoJayProcessor::setStateInformation -> ChainHost restoreNextSlot ->
loadPluginAsync(LoadOrigin::Restore) -> the callback's setSlotWet(WetSource::Restore)
+ applyRestoredState -> the built-in's setStateInformation (ParamSource::Restore).
    POSITIVE CONTROL (setting OFF):  slot 0 wet 0.40 -> 0.40; retune 200 -> 200; retune_speed_ms 150 -> 150;
                                     natural_vibrato 50 -> 50; flex 30 -> 30            PASS
    V9 RACK (do-not-dial ON at reload): the same five values, all back              PASS
So Kathy's fix covers the rack: the slot wet (Restore is not refused) and the
device values (Restore is not refused) survive a session reload with the mode on.
HARNESS MECHANICS, recorded so the next person does not lose two hours: (1) a
console process has no NSApplication run loop, so MessageManager::runDispatchLoop
returns at once - pump CFRunLoopRunInMode on the main thread instead; (2) do not
construct EchoJayProcessor from a worker thread under MessageManagerLock (deadlock);
(3) juce::Timer subclasses cannot be aggregate-initialised.
A PRODUCT FACT the harness surfaced on the way (not a defect in her change, by
design): the session state serialises ChainHost's per-slot state CACHE and never
calls into a hosted plugin (safe inside a host's save callback). The cache is
refreshed on the processor's 1 s timer once the rack has been quiet for
kStateDebounceMs = 2000 ms (silent plugins every kStateSweepMs = 15 s). So a
session saved within ~3 s of the last edit to a built-in device carries that
device's PREVIOUS capture - the harness's first end-to-end run saved 0 ms after
editing and read back defaults with the setting OFF. archiveCurrentRack and the
library save force captureAllSlotStatesNow(); the host session path does not.
Recorded for the residual list; not changed today.


## SHOOT-DAY LINK DEFECT (6 Sep 2026, Pro Tools): several Links, one recognised, all named alike
### Round 53 (2f02c65) CLEARED by its own diff
`git diff 2f02c65^ 2f02c65 -- Source/PluginProcessor.h Source/PluginProcessor.cpp`:
refreshLinkRegistry's row accumulation is identical before and after - one loop
over the registry slots, one push_back per live slot, rows carrying the slot's
instanceUid. The commit added `struct BudgetRow` and changed `ctxCapCache_`'s
VALUE type (uid -> BudgetRow); that cache is uid-keyed and consulted only by
budgetRowCounts to decide whether a row counts toward the borrow budget - a
FILTER, never a key. Kathy's commits (d68da09..25861e6) touch neither the walk
nor its containers (0 lines). The editor addresses its Link strips by uid
(linkAddrForSlot; the name is a fallback only for a row with no uid).
### JUCE 8.0.12 (29396c22c9) AAX wrapper and the track name (Step 1)
juce_audio_plugin_client_AAX.cpp:1321-1327: updateTrackProperties is called
ONLY on AAX_eNotificationEvent_TrackNameChanged; there is no query at
instantiation. AU (AU_1.mm:2606) installs a ContextName property listener; VST3
(VST3.cpp:1168/1171) receives setChannelContextInfos. So an AAX Link can carry
an empty host name until Pro Tools renames the track. Cosmetic under the fix
below (the name was never the identity of the rows; it WAS the identity of the
ring file).
### THE MECHANISM, by experiment (tools/merge_gate_tests/link_two_same_name_test.cpp, two Links in one process against the real registry, rows released on exit)
BEFORE (results_2026-09-06/link_two_same_name_BEFORE.log):
    two different names        -> two slots, two ring files (different inodes)   PASS (control)
    the same typed name        -> two slots, DISTINCT uids, ONE ring file (same inode)   FAIL
    a duplicated insert (same state -> two instances, same name AND same uid)
                               -> the uid gate re-mints the second ("held by a PROVEN-LIVE instance -> regenerated"),
                                  two slots, distinct uids, ONE ring file   FAIL
    the burst (clone publishes 6x synchronously before the original's timer ticks)
                               -> still two slots, two uids (the gate is not fooled)   PASS
The ONLY identity keyed on the name was the audio ring FILE:
LinkProcessor::effectiveFilePart() built it from the user-typed linkName, and
LinkShm::openRingProducer zeroes the ring header on open - so the second Link
with the same typed name wiped the first's ring and both rows read one file.
No in-process leg produces a single registry row.
### THE FIX (pre-authorised shape: identity on instanceUid_, name cosmetic)
Source/LinkProcessor.cpp effectiveFilePart(): `return instanceUid_;` - the ring
is audio_<uid>.bin. instanceUid_ is unique per instance, saved/restored with
the state, re-minted for a proven-live duplicate BEFORE this is consulted
(claimRegistrySlot), and never changes on a host or user rename, so the ring
never renames. The reaper compares whole audio filenames against the live
slots' audioFile strings, so uid-named rings are handled unchanged.
AFTER (results_2026-09-06/link_two_same_name_AFTER.log): all four legs PASS -
two files in every case, audio_<uidA>.bin / audio_<uidB>.bin.
### THE BAR (on the fixed Link archive, this Mac, 16:33-16:40)
    round 53's five-state impulse table + prepare re-decision: ALL PASS; quiet session 1200 blocks, 0 notifications
    scoping controls: in-process capable live row COUNTS (positive control), DIFFERENT host process does not,
                      recycled pid does not, DEAD publisher does not, old sidecar never, not-in-context-capable does not
    pitch suite 196 / 0 (pitch code untouched by the change: git diff on Source/Eed* Source/EJ* Source/PluginProcessor.* empty)
    derived values: unchanged by construction (same files)
    AU/VST3 Link behaviour otherwise unchanged: the change is one function's return value

                            (V2 AU/VST3 re-copied, byte-identical D326FEC1 / E34F2301)

### THE GAP (recorded as the reason, not an excuse)
Every Link test in this project - linksync, borrowhost, racklock, the impulse

### THE MECHANISM, NAMED FROM THE FRESH INSERT (Pro Tools, 16:59:50; results_2026-09-06/protools_fresh_insert_1659.log)
Sean inserted a FRESH EchoJay Link from the plug-in menu on a second track. The log, in order:
    16:59:50.243 [worker]  setState linkOn=1 (hadProp=1)          <- PRO TOOLS SEEDED THE NEW INSTANCE WITH A CHUNK
    16:59:50.326          editor open
    16:59:50.413          post-init uid=f798ba2774                 <- the chunk's uid = the first Link's
    16:59:50.413          host track name "bass 2" (user name "")  <- the chunk's hostTrackName = the first Link's TRACK
    16:59:50.413          uid f798ba2774 held by a PROVEN-LIVE instance (duplicate) -> regenerated f3b5c0dc52
Registry now: slot 0 "bass 2" uid f798ba2774 audio_bass_2.bin (heartbeat climbing); slot 1 "bass 2" uid f3b5c0dc52
audio_untitled_f3b5c0dc52.bin (climbing). Two sidecars from pid 55105 (f798ba2774 rev 2, f3b5c0dc52 rev 1), both
named "bass 2". So the fresh insert IS a distinct row with its own uid and its own ring - and it is NAMED "bass 2".
THREE FACTS, each with its code:
  1. Pro Tools seeds a fresh insert with the plugin's last chunk (host behaviour: the ~1 Hz getState polls on the live
     instance, then setState into the newcomer BEFORE its editor opens). The uid rides in the chunk; the gate handles
     that correctly - PROVEN-LIVE holder -> re-mint (this insert), FROZEN holder -> adopt (the 16:07 storm, where each
     incarnation's predecessor was already dead). The gate is NOT the defect: both ghost legs pass on today's code
     (results_2026-09-06/link_ghost_legs.log): a fresh Link with no state beside a frozen slot mints its own uid and
     leaves the ghost alone; a restore carrying the ghost's uid adopts it. A fresh construction NEVER enters the gate
     (its uid is random, LinkProcessor.cpp:24); only a chunk can carry a uid in. The proposed "mint if no restored uid"
     fix is today's behaviour and would change nothing.
  2. THE NAME: the chunk also carries hostTrackName, and setStateInformation restores it into hostTrackName_
     (LinkProcessor.cpp:2592-2602, commit 07f8547, 27 Jul 2026: "Seed the stash + dirty flag so the restored name shows
     and publishes immediately, even in a host that never re-fires the callback"). That restore exists for a real case
     (session reopen in a host that never re-sends the name) and is WRONG for a seeded foreign chunk: the second Link
     publishes the FIRST Link's track name. And nothing corrects it, because JUCE 8.0.12's AAX wrapper delivers the
     track name only on AAX_eNotificationEvent_TrackNameChanged (juce_audio_plugin_client_AAX.cpp:1321), never at
     instantiation - so in Pro Tools the seeded name persists until the user renames the track. Every fresh Link comes
     up "bass 2"; the Link Monitor shows N rows all named "bass 2"; "only the first is recognised" is what N identical
     names look like. In Logic the AU's ContextName listener overwrites the seeded name at once - which is why Logic
     never showed it.
  3. THE RING FILE (fixed, unsigned): with a typed name shared across instances, one ring for two producers. Real,
     proven in-process, not this symptom's cause (these Links had no typed name: rings untitled_<uid>).
THE 16:07 STORM stays recorded as abnormal host behaviour (30 incarnations in 30 s on one track, each seeded from the
last, each predecessor dead by the time the next looked) - not chased today; the PACE dlsym line at load is the lead.
STOPPED FOR THE RULING (the mechanism is not the one hypothesised). Proposed fix shape, for the ruling, smallest and
using the existing decision: when the uid gate decides PROVEN-LIVE -> re-mint, the chunk this instance restored belongs
to ANOTHER LIVE instance, so its hostTrackName is that instance's track - clear the seeded hostTrackName_ (and
appliedHostName_) in the same arm and let the host's own notification (or the user's typed name) supply this track's
name; keep it in the AdoptGhost arm (a replacement incarnation of a dead predecessor on the same track) and on a plain
restore (session reopen). In Pro Tools the fresh insert would then show "Untitled N" until renamed, which is true.
Verification is Pro Tools only (Logic overwrites the name); one signing trip, bundled with the ring fix.


## C4 (6 Sep 2026, approved): THE GATE DECIDES IN TIME, NOT IN COUNTS - and the storm measured
### The defect, measured before the fix
L4 (three fresh inserts seeded with a LIVE Link's chunk) on the C1-C3 build: 2 of 4 runs adopted the live
Link's uid ("held by a FROZEN ghost slot ... uid adopted" while its heartbeat climbed). Cause, read in code:
UidClaimGate declared "frozen" after five observations without a climb, assuming ~1 s retries against ~1 Hz
bumps (its own comment); the holder bumps on every 30 Hz tick, the claim retry runs on every 30 Hz tick
(timerCallback, not the /30 divider), and a seeded insert observes several times inside one message-loop
turn (setStateInformation's synchronous updateShmState plus the queued ones). Counting calls measured
dispatch order. In Pro Tools that is two live instances on one uid: one row that any rename renames.
### The fix (LinkShm.h UidClaimGate + LinkProcessor.cpp claimRegistrySlot)
C4a  AdoptGhost needs the five observations AND kUidGateFloorMs since this holder was first observed.
C4b  a holder whose sidecar publisherPid is DEAD (kill(pid,0), round 53's field) is adopted at once, no floor;
     an unreadable/unpublished sidecar fails closed (treated alive -> floor).
THE FLOOR IS DERIVED: 3500 ms = the product's own freshness window - the Link's solo scan already treats a
heartbeat unmoved for 3500 ms as not fresh (LinkProcessor.cpp `fresh = ... < 3500.0`), the main plugin ~3 s.
A holder frozen through the floor is dead by the same rule everywhere. Row added to DERIVED_VALUES_SINCE_BASE.md.
### C4c - twenty consecutive runs (results_2026-09-06/link_race20_c4.log)
    L4    20 / 20      burst    20 / 20      live holders adopted: 0
### THE STORM MEASUREMENT (results_2026-09-06/link_storm_measurement_c4.log; incarnations destroyed, the
frozen slot and sidecar re-planted as a killed process leaves them)
    same pid, ALIVE (the Pro Tools case), 30 incarnations x ~1 s: identities burned 0, adoptions 0,
      incarnations died unregistered 30, orphans 0 (a burned identity would leave one sidecar + one ring);
      the first incarnation to outlive the floor ADOPTED the seed uid 4389 ms after the storm.
    predecessor pid DEAD (session reopen after a crash), 5 x ~1 s: adopted at the FIRST observation every
      time ("publisher pid DEAD"), burns 0, survivor registered in 25 ms. C4b keeps the unclean-kill case fast.
THE TRADE, recorded: during a storm the Link is absent from the main plugin's list (every incarnation dies
inside the floor, unregistered) and reappears ~4.4 s after the storm ends. Bounded, no churn, shipped.
(The sim's "sidecar overwritten in 5 of 5" counter reads AFTER adoption - the adopter's own legitimate
publish - not a pre-claim overwrite; the 25 ms registration is the proof the first observation saw the dead pid.)
### THE AUDIT (standing rule: A LIVENESS DECISION MUST BE MADE IN TIME, NOT IN COUNTS)
Found, not in this bundle ("nothing else joins it"):
  - PluginProcessor.cpp:4668-4676 refreshLinkRegistry: `staleCycles++ ... >= kRegStaleCycles (60)` decides
    "producer appears to have crashed - reap the slot" by counting REFRESH CALLS, and refresh is called from
    the 1 Hz processor timer, the editor's ~2 Hz timer AND event-driven sites (PluginEditor.cpp:11322,
    23572, 23958). Its comment says "~30 s at 2 Hz"; a burst of event-driven refreshes shortens that. Same
    class; heartbeatFresh (:4741, staleCycles < 6) likewise. To be re-based on wall clock.
  - LinkProcessor.cpp publishRackSidecar publishes under the (possibly contested) uid while the instance
    is still unregistered (Wait); it overwrites the holder's sidecar with the newcomer's own pid and rack.
    Harmless for C4b (the first observation precedes the first publish, proven by the 25 ms adoption) but
    an identity that is not yet resolved should not publish under it. Noted.
  - Clean: the Link's solo scan (LinkProcessor.cpp:725-726) requires a climb AND 3500 ms freshness - time.
    The main plugin's listing gate (ps.live.observe) only ever proves live by a climb, never dead by count.
### THE BUNDLE, installed here and packaged for the ONE trip

## THE NAME AND THE CAPACITY (6 Sep 2026, evening): two hard requirements, one trip, two bundles
### The name: Pro Tools DOES deliver it, and the gate's clear was wiping it - ORDER-DEPENDENT
Correction on the record: the earlier reading "Pro Tools has never been observed delivering a track name"
was over-read from a log that could not show it - the timer's "host track name" line served both a
seeded and a host-delivered name, and updateTrackProperties logged nothing. Direct observation (Sean:
a fresh insert named itself "Kick" from the track) and then the harness settled it: the host delivers,
and whether the name survived depended on whether it landed before or after the gate's clear.
NEGATIVE CONTROL, real code (results_2026-09-06/provenance_NEGATIVE_CONTROL_58f36d1.log; the committed
pre-provenance tree built in a worktree with a private registry file so it could claim slots while Pro
Tools filled the live one): host name delivered BEFORE the claim's clear - kept 0 / 20; AFTER - kept 20 / 20.
That is exactly one "Kick" and thirteen blanks. Dispatch order deciding behaviour, the same shape as the
adoption gate.
THE CURE, provenance (LinkProcessor.h/.cpp, LinkEditor.cpp): a seeded name is PROVISIONAL; a name from
updateTrackProperties (hostNameFromHost_) or typed by the user (markTypedNameAuthoritative) is
AUTHORITATIVE; the invariant's clear drops only provisional names, whatever the order. updateTrackProperties
now logs "host DELIVERED track name" - the only line that proves the host spoke.
AFTER (results_2026-09-06/provenance_p20.log): BEFORE kept 20 / 20, AFTER kept 20 / 20, the invariant ("no
instance ever publishes a name that arrived in a chunk it did not originate") checked in every run.
The AAX-query line is closed: AAX SDK 2.9.0 has no track-name query (its only mention is the notification
event, which JUCE already handles), and none is needed.
### The capacity: 256 slots, layout v2 in registry_v3.bin, checked on open
LinkShm.h: kRegMaxSlots 16 -> 256; kRegLayoutVersion 2; the file is registry_v3.bin (16-slot builds keep
registry_v2.bin, so old and new never share a file); openRegistry REFUSES a region whose header carries a
different version or slot count (errno EPROTO, lastRegistryLayoutError() logged by both plugins as
"registry layout mismatch ... REFUSING to use it"). Region 64 + 256*128 + 256*128 = 65,600 bytes.
REAPER: reapDeadPublisherSlots - a full claimant reclaims rows whose sidecar publisher pid is gone
(kill(pid,0) == ESRCH; no sidecar or unknowable pid = left alone), retries once.
FULL MUST REPORT: still full -> EJLinkState "STILL FULL - 256 slots all held by live publishers; this Link
is NOT registered" + diag.regFull, and the Link editor's name field turns red with the tooltip "EchoJay Link
registry is FULL: this Link is not visible to EchoJay". Never silent.
THE AUDIO PATH DOES NOT WALK THE CEILING: EchoJayProcessor::processBlock iterates a compact LIVE list
(liveSlotIdx_/liveSlotCount_, published by the walk after connecting - indices first, count last with
release; cleared by disconnectAllLinkSlotsNow) and looks capture channels up in the short live vector
instead of a ceiling-sized stack array. What scales with the ceiling: activeLinkSlots (indexed by registry
slot; touched per block only through the live list), slotProbeStates and the message-thread loops
(borrowTick, borrowEngageBegin, editBegin, startCapture, disconnectAll, the walk), the Link's soloScan_.
LEGS (results_2026-09-06/capacity_legs.log), all PASS: P1 host name before the clear kept; P2 after kept;
R1 256 dead-publisher rows -> a fresh Link reclaims one (all 256 reaped); R2 256 live-publisher rows -> the
fresh Link reports FULL (slot -1, regFull) rather than vanishing; V1 a v1/16-slot region refused with the
message. 50 live rows through the main plugin: 50 distinct display names Untitled .. Untitled 50 (PASS).
Both formats of both plugins load and pass audio; round 53's table + quiet session + scoping ALL PASS;
suite 196 / 0; host test 18 / 0; registry test 1667 / 0; no pitch file touched.
### MEASURED, not reasoned (results_2026-09-06/link_scan30hz_cost.log, link_races_capacity.log)

## CAPTURE AT 45 LINKS (6 Sep 2026, Sean's question): MEASURED
### 1. Payload. What a capture turn sends, measured through the real code (tools/merge_gate_tests/link_levels_payload_test.cpp,
### the editor's own buildLinkLevelsContext over planted live rows with meter frames; MeterEngine::meterDataToJSON)
    the main meter blob (one MeterData, 64 spectrum bins): 660 bytes, does not scale with Links
    the per-Link part = the [LINK LEVELS] injection in the newest message: one text row per live Link
      1 Link  3,530 bytes (3.4 KB is fixed rules text)   16 Links 5,892 bytes   45 Links 10,474 bytes  (~155 bytes per Link)
    the newest turn's other injections (plugin list, chain rules, builtins with schemas, current chain): 61-70 KB,
      measured on live sends (EchoJayHistoryTrim.h / buildChatRequestBody's own record)
    history: capped at 12 messages AND 24,000 bytes, walked newest-first; the newest message is never charged
    TOTAL request body, capture turn:  1 Link ~ 95 KB     16 Links ~ 97 KB     45 Links ~ 102 KB
    The per-Link cost is ~155 bytes; 45 Links add ~7 KB to a ~95 KB request. No cap in buildLinkLevelsContext
    truncated the 45 rows (all 45 present in the measured output).
    Per-channel CAPTURE audio is NOT in the request: each Link's captured WAV is written locally (wavFilePath) for
    review/playback; the turn carries busCount and the rows above.
### 2. Server request limit. The API runs on Vercel (RELEASE.md, the dev transport); Vercel's documented request-body
    limit for serverless functions is 4.5 MB. ~102 KB at 45 Links is ~2% of it. NOT measured against production
    (no synthetic oversized POST was sent to the live API); documented limit quoted. Model context: ~102 KB is
    roughly 25k tokens - the model is the server's choice and its window is not visible from here.
### 3. Timeouts. THE SAME SHAPE AS THE DASHBOARD MINT: streamChat uses a 60 s CONNECT timeout only
    (EchoJayAPI.cpp:1828 "connect phase ONLY - withConnectionTimeout does not bound the read loop"); the read loop
    (:1941) has NO deadline - it ends on EOF, on error, or when the USER cancels (ChatStreamHandle::cancel unblocks
    the read). A server that accepts the request and never answers leaves the turn open with no terminal outcome.
    The non-streaming sendChat -> postJSON -> readEntireStreamAsString has no read timeout either. The classifier
    request (:1663) is the one call with a deadline latch. RECORDED for the residual list; not built tonight.
### THE RULE (degrade and say so) - one violation found
    EchoJayProcessor::startCapture (PluginProcessor.cpp:2859-2862) captures a Link only if
    `activeLinkSlots[i].displayName.isNotEmpty()`: an UNNAMED Link (empty registry name - a Link the main plugin
    lists as "Untitled N") is SILENTLY LEFT OUT of a capture. With provenance, Links carry their track names, so this
    bites only a Link with no track name and no typed name - but when it bites it is exactly the failure the rule
    names: the turn claims busCount from snap.channels while the unnamed Link never entered the channels at all.
    Fix shape (not built tonight): capture every connected slot, name the unnamed ones "Untitled N" the way the
    display list does, and say in the injection when a Link was excluded and why. buildLinkLevelsContext already
    holds the right principle in its own text: "names the shortfall, and a silently capped number is worse".
### THE ANSWER FOR SEAN, plainly
    A capture at 45 Links WORKS and costs slightly more: ~7 KB more request than at 1 Link, two percent of the
    platform's body limit, no truncation of the 45 rows. Usage is per turn, not per Link. Name every Link (the
    track name now arrives; a typed name also counts) - an unnamed Link is silently left out of the capture today.


## THE v6 REGRESSIONS (6 Sep 2026, ~20:00): names gone, solo gone - two mechanisms, both named from the live log
### Mechanism 1 (mine): Pro Tools re-applies a live instance's OWN chunk, and v6 treated each as a seed
The provenance line did its job: 87 "host DELIVERED track name" lines in the Pro Tools process (Kick, Bass, Drums,
Music, FX ...) - the host delivers, the apply path reaches the name. Then: 212 setState lines for ~40 instances -
Pro Tools calls SetChunk on live instances repeatedly with each instance's own current chunk (no specification
says so; the log does). v6's setStateInformation re-armed "chunk uid / authored here" and reset provenance to
provisional on EVERY setState; the next publish (which the host-name arrival itself triggers) released and
re-claimed the slot, found no holder for its own uid, and the no-holder rule RE-MINTED - 47 no-holder re-mints,
chains of one instance re-minting every 1-2 s, 89 re-mints in twenty minutes, "Kick" dropped as provisional
(results_2026-09-06/protools_v6_provenance_log.log). v4 kept "Kick" only because a re-applied chunk was kept
wholesale there. Worse than v4 for exactly that reason.
### Mechanism 2: solo - a consequence of 1, plus a latent gap
The solo transport and the fabric scan work at 256 (linksync_test's solo legs pass on the new archive; its two
FAIL lines are its own planted negative controls); nothing on the solo path reads the old file or layout;
soloScan_ is sized by the same constant. Under identity churn a soloed Link's sidecar sat under its OLD uid:
publishRackSidecar republished only when revision / epoch / mute / solo moved, never when the uid changed, so no
scan by row uid could find it. Mute is local to the Link and survived.
### The legs that fail on the installed v6 archive (results_2026-09-06/churn_NEGATIVE_CONTROL_v6.log)
    L8  host delivered "Kick"; the instance's OWN chunk re-applied 5x: uid re-minted 5x, "Kick" dropped 5x   FAIL
    L9  A soloed, B sees it; A's own chunk re-applied 3x: A's uid changed 3x                                   FAIL
### THE FIX (approved): a chunk carrying the uid this instance already holds a slot for is OURS
setStateInformation: ownChunk = (chunk uid == instanceUid_ && regSlotIdx >= 0) -> identity stays settled (no
re-arm), provenance not downgraded, the chunk's names fill in only where we have none. publishRackSidecar:
uidMoved is a publish reason (lastPublishedUid_), so a genuine re-mint moves its sidecar with it.
AFTER (v7 Link archive; results_2026-09-06/churn_after_v7.log, churn20_v7.log): L8 PASS, L9 PASS; CHURN20 L8 20/20,
L9 20/20. NO AAX BUILT (ruling): Link AU/VST3 installed to ~/Library for the Logic bisect (AU 019C96D3, VST3 58CBD98F).
### Why the legs missed both
P1/P2 delivered a name once and never modelled the host re-applying the instance's own chunk afterwards: faithful
to the callback contract, blind to the host's habit. A solo leg existed all along in tools/linksync_test and was
never in the gate build nor run today; it does not model churn either.
### THE RULES (filed, memory: legs-model-the-host)
A LEG MUST MODEL WHAT THE HOST ACTUALLY DOES, NOT WHAT THE API PERMITS. When a leg models a host, its fidelity is a
claim until a log from that host supports it. And: A TEST THAT EXISTS AND IS NOT RUN IS NOT A TEST - it is worse
than no test, because its existence implies coverage that is not there. Every target and harness in the repo is
in the gate or has a written reason for exclusion (the audit below).

## THE ISOLATION RULING (evening of 6 Sep) - ISOLATION IS A PRECONDITION FOR EVIDENCE, NOT HYGIENE
The L4 race leg's 19/20 on v7 was not a code failure: another harness's planted rows ("FM Test"/"FO Test") sat in
the SAME live registry, and earlier in the day a harness wrote Sean's real auth.json. Sean was in Pro Tools on
this Mac the whole evening (pid 83157, 38 live rows held by his session). Every count taken before the ruling was
taken with other writers in the state and IS NOT EVIDENCE; none of those numbers are quoted below.
### The mechanism (by construction, not by discipline)
Source/EJStateRoot.h: every state path in the product goes through echojay::userAppData() / userStateHome(),
keyed on ECHOJAY_STATE_HOME (27 getSpecialLocation(userApplicationDataDirectory) sites in 11 files, the two
~/.echojay dev.json sites, the event log's ~/Library/EchoJay and the dashboard's v2preview.token). Unset, the
product resolves to the real locations exactly as before (the shipped binaries behave identically). Every
merge_gate_tests harness main calls echojay::requireIsolationOrDie(name); every tools/*/build_and_run.sh (25)
and compile_link_harness.sh export a fresh mktemp root by default and print it. PROVEN: with the env unset,
link_two_same_name_test exits 3 with "REFUSING TO RUN - ECHOJAY_STATE_HOME is not set" (iso/refuse.log).
Serialising the harnesses was ruled out: it hides the sharing, it does not remove it.

## v7 INSTALLED, NAMES STILL EMPTY - THE LOG SAYS: THE HOST SENDS THEM, WE WIPE THEM (22:00, 6 Sep)
FACT 1 (disk): both installed bundles carry the four v7 UUIDs (V2 0760FE10/7FBB47DE, Link E66AEC93/9E3A9B2A),
Developer ID signed 21:48:33/21:48:39, PACE bundle present; InstalledAAXPlugIns and ~/Library/Caches/Avid absent;
Pro Tools pid 5057 launched 21:51:27, after the install. The right binary.
FACT 2 (results_2026-09-06/protools_v7_provenance_log.log, 2,110 lines from pid 5057):
    host DELIVERED track name          81 lines, real names ("Kick", "Bass", "Drums", "Music", "FX", "All Vocals"...)
    post-init with an EMPTY name       72 of 81
    live registry rows                 40, ALL born by re-mint, NONE ever the target of a delivery
    distinct uids delivered to         81; of those still holding a row: 0
The sequence, every instance, three waves (21:54:26, 21:54:46, 21:55:05 - Pro Tools tears down and recreates):
    21:54:26.355 [main]   host DELIVERED track name "Kick" (uid b4f06c2bea)          <- ctor uid, authoritative
    21:54:26.440 [worker] setState linkOn=1 (hadProp=1) name=""                        <- a FOREIGN chunk arrives
    21:54:26.440 [worker] setState linkOn=1 (hadProp=1) name="bass 2"                    (the seed, uid f798ba2774)
    ... later, the same instance:
    21:55:05.226  uid b4f06c2bea came from a chunk authored in THIS host run and no slot holds it -> regenerated b5591cbd4f
    21:55:05.226  chunk uid b4f06c2bea != this instance b5591cbd4f: seeded names dropped (typed "", host "Kick");
                  authoritative kept (host "", typed none)
THE PATH THAT DROPS IT - LinkProcessor.cpp:2731, the chunk-seeding branch of setStateInformation:
    if (! ownChunk) { hostTrackName_ = obj->getProperty("hostTrackName").toString(); hostNameFromHost_ = false; }
A foreign chunk carrying a hostTrackName OVERWRITES the name the host delivered ~85 ms earlier AND downgrades the
provenance flag to provisional. The invariant then drops the (now provisional) name as seeded. The comment above
the invariant - "a name the host delivered ... survives, whether it arrived before or after this point" - is false
for delivery-then-foreign-chunk, which is Pro Tools' actual order on every insert here.
Why P20 missed it: its "after the clear" arm modelled the re-mint, not a foreign chunk WITH a hostTrackName
applied after the delivery. The host's habit, again, is: deliver first, then apply someone else's chunk.
NOT FIXED. The fix shape for the ruling: a delivered name is authoritative and a foreign chunk's hostTrackName
must not replace it or its flag (fill only when we have none, exactly as the ownChunk arm already does). The leg
first: deliver, then apply a foreign chunk carrying a different hostTrackName, then re-mint - must FAIL on v7.

## 7 Sep 2026 - THE NAME FIX (C1-C3) AND THE DIALABILITY ITEMS THAT RIDE WITH IT (v8)
### The leg first, and it FAILED on v7 (scratchpad names/foreign_v7.log; the harness compiled against the v7 archive)
    F1  host delivered "Kick" -> foreign chunk (host "Track A", typed "Vox") -> re-mint   host "" published ""   FAIL
    F2  user typed "MyBus"    -> foreign chunk (typed "Vox")                 -> re-mint   typed "" published ""  FAIL
    F3  nothing delivered     -> foreign chunk fills (provisional) -> re-mint drops it (P2 unchanged)             PASS
The log line that condemns v7, from F1: "chunk uid 23ed77fbbe != this instance 1e5aec8c18: seeded names dropped
(typed "Vox", host "Track A"); authoritative kept (host "", typed none)" - the delivered "Kick" was already gone,
overwritten by the seeding branch before the invariant ran.
WHY P20 WENT GREEN: its P1 arm applied the seed BEFORE the delivery and its P2 arm delivered AFTER the re-mint. It
never applied a foreign chunk carrying its own name AFTER a delivery - Pro Tools' actual order on every insert.
### The fix (LinkProcessor.cpp, both seeding branches + the comment)
    C1  hostTrackName: a foreign chunk fills ONLY WHEN WE HAVE NONE; if hostNameFromHost_ the name and its
        provenance are untouched (the own-chunk arm's behaviour, now for foreign chunks too)
    C2  linkName (typed): identical rule keyed on typedNameFromUser_ - the half we had not observed
    C3  the invariant's comment no longer claims survival "whether it arrived before or after"; it names the
        ordering it was blind to and the leg that models it (foreign / foreign20)
### Dialability, per the revised ruling, bundled in the same build (no separate trip)
    C2   PluginEditor.cpp: DialStatus::pending is worded "still waiting for its map - its settings will apply when
         it arrives" in BOTH composers (was: added to the "needs hand-dialing" list at :22198 and its edit-path
         twin). reportPendingDialOutcomes() closes the promise when the slot reaches a terminal state: "Settings
         applied to X - its map arrived." or "X did not get its map - use the values on its card."
    C2b  a failed exact fetch (status != 200) calls ChainHost::failMapFetch(fps); a failed fallback lookup calls
         failFallbackLookup(); both clear the pending mark and re-evaluate (noMap while the map is absent). A later
         answer from the other request still dials through the mapArrived sweep, so "terminal now, corrected later".
    C1'  kDialSettleAttempts = 24 (6,000 ms), derived: 2x the 2,982 ms fallback measured; DERIVED_VALUES row added.
    C1'' NOT DONE, and why: the request that matters is the fallback lookup, and it needs the LOADED instance's
         fingerprint, param_count and param_names (fallbackEntryForSlot). At chain-block arrival none of those
         exist; the exact-fp prefetch needs identityToFp_, and all five slots read "first-index" (not indexed).
         Requesting earlier therefore needs either a server lookup by identity alone or a pre-load fingerprint,
         neither small. It waits.
    Priority 1 (server omits settings_structured for filter-passed plugins): drafted for Kathy in
    KATHY_REPLY_2026-09-06.md with the log. Priority 3: DEFECT_SOLID_BUS_COMP_WRITES_REJECTED.md.
### AFTER (v8 Link archive, each run in its own private root; results_2026-09-06/foreign20_v8.log, p20_v8.log, churn20_v8.log)
    FOREIGN x20   F1 delivered-then-foreign kept 20/20   F2 typed-then-foreign kept 20/20   F3 fill-when-none 20/20
    P20 alongside P1 20/20  P2 20/20  (the old arms still pass)          P20 standalone  20/20 / 20/20
    CHURN20       L8 20/20   L9 20/20  (the v7 own-chunk rule still holds)

                                           Link 0.8.5 arm64 677CB825-8B60-38FE-BFD6-A1C774897916 x86_64 1000546C-F8E6-33AD-B752-35439E8A3970

## 8 Sep, 11:05-11:18 - SOLO AND IN-CONTEXT ON v8, READ FROM THE LIVE LOG (results_2026-09-06/protools_v8_solo_incontext_log.log, pid 32225, 6,624 lines)
Binaries: V2 v7 (0760FE10) + Link v8 (677CB825), signed 11:00:56, Pro Tools launched 11:05:20. Names: 40 named rows, all 40 sidecars
present, inContextCapable=true, hostPid=publisherPid=32225.
### The budget DID commit - twice - so "never commits in this host" is not it
    11:10:22.227 EJCtx: alignment budget ON (capable Link present) - PDC re-runs once     <- commitBorrowBudget, STOPPED block
    11:12:53.265 EJCtx: alignment budget ON (capable Link present) - PDC re-runs once     <- again: so it was OFF just before
Only commitBorrowBudget logs to the system log, and only on a change. prepareToPlay stores WANTED into ACTIVE directly with no
system-log line (PluginProcessor.cpp:592); the WANTED flips and setLatency reports go to EJ_LAT_LOG, which is compiled OUT of
build-release (EJ_LATENCY_LOG=OFF). So the second ON proves a silent OFF in between, and the only silent writer is prepareToPlay.
### Both borrow sessions were engaged inside that OFF window, and nothing re-raises a session later
    11:12:32.898 EJBorrow: engaged uid=ed1a96cb9c ringSlot=0        11:12:47.225 EJBorrow: engaged uid=ff4a2cbd75 ringSlot=5
    EJCtx(main): renew muteOut=N ok=N soloSup=N   x114 (every renew, 11:12-11:14, before AND after the 11:12:53 commit)
    EJCtx(link): leased muteWant=N                x104 (the Link never mutes its dry channel: the main told it not to)
    EJLease: RACK engaged, 0 slot(s) bypassed, streaming dry  /  no "EJCtx: mute UNCONFIRMED", no "chain latency ... exceeds budget"
The engage decision (PluginProcessor.cpp:2153) is inContextCapable && alignPad(0) >= 0 && borrowBudgetActive_. The sidecars say
capable; alignPad(0) = 16384 - 1024 - 0 >= 0 always; so the false term was borrowBudgetActive_, consistent with the silent OFF.
borrowInContextOk_ is set TRUE at exactly one site (2153, engage time) and never after a later commit - the 11:12:53 ON did not
help the running session. Symptom 2 (selecting a rack solos it) = the designed fallback (fallbackSolo, :1204) doing its job.
No refusal line exists to quote: the engage decision logs nothing. That is an instrumentation gap in its own right.
What flips WANTED off in Pro Tools: the 1 Hz pass counts only rows whose heartbeat has been OBSERVED TO CLIMB (RegLiveness, one
climb, heartbeat every 1 s) and whose sidecar reads back; Pro Tools re-minted all 40 instances at 11:10:14-11:10:30 (post-init
storm; "audio stale (blocks frozen)" 273 lines in 8 minutes), during which the list is momentarily empty -> WANTED OFF -> the
prepareToPlay that Pro Tools issues when the 11:10:22 latency change lands commits OFF, silently. Inferred from structure, not
quoted: the latency log that would show it is compiled out.
### Second candidate CLEARED: Kathy's range d68da09..aa455ff touched 47 lines in PluginProcessor/LinkProcessor/LinkShm, zero of them
on solo, borrow, budget, in-context, alignPad or the fabric scan (grep of the diff).
### Solo itself (symptom 1) - the log stops one step short
    11:11:22.515 EJMuteSolo: sent soloOn=1 uid=f798ba2774   -> 11:11:22.542 EJLinkState: remote set soloOn=1   (and 5 more pairs)
    11:12:18.960 ... uid=6dd1148de9 ("88_7even Kick_2")       11:12:39.730 ... uid=ed1a96cb9c ("Kick", while borrowed)
The command reaches the Link every time. What the spec then requires (MUTE_SOLO_SPEC: "every OTHER Link mutes") is: the soloed
Link republishes its sidecar with soloOn (publish reason muteMoved includes soloOn, LinkProcessor.cpp:243), every other Link's
4 Hz fabric scan reads that sidecar (mtime moved) and sets soloMuteWant_, and linkMuteWanted() silences its output. NONE of those
three steps logs anything; the sidecars carry soloOn/muteEngaged only while true, and solo is off now. The log cannot say which
step fails. Note the first solo went to uid f798ba2774 - the seed uid every fresh instance is born with (post-init x many at
11:10:14) - which holds no registry row now; the main's list carried it at 11:11:22 from the storm. Solo is NOT accounted for by
the budget: the two paths share nothing (solo = Link-to-Link via sidecars; in-context = main's budget). A second cause, unnamed.
### What would name it (proposed, not done - it acts on Sean's live session): write ctrl-cmd-<uid>.json soloOn=1 for "Kick" by
hand, watch rack-ed1a96cb9c.json for soloOn:true within 1 s and every other rack-*.json for muteEngaged:true within 2 s, then
soloOn=0. Three yes/no answers, one step each. Or instrument the three steps and rebuild the Link.

## 8 Sep - LINK LIST ORDER (investigation only, nothing built)
ONE ordering site, and it is (a) an explicit sort with a name comparator: PluginProcessor.cpp:4920-4931 getLinkDisplayList():
    auto sorted = linkSlotInfos;   // copy, message thread
    std::stable_sort(sorted.begin(), sorted.end(), [](a, b){ named first; untitled stable by uid; else a.name.compareIgnoreCase(b.name) < 0; });
It is the CANONICAL list (PluginProcessor.h:1266-1275: "every Link-listing surface must use"): the Link view rows (PluginEditor.cpp
:29018-29040 builds LvlRow from getLinkDisplayList()), the monitor, the send-target menu and the AI context all consume it. No
name-keyed container is involved: linkSlotInfos is std::vector<LinkSlotInfo> filled in registry-walk order (:4749 push_back), the
two std::map<juce::String,...> nameByUid (:27900, :27967) are lookup tables, not iteration sources. The registry walk itself does
not sort (listed.sort(false) at :4764 sorts a capability-set KEY, not the list).
Insertion order IS recoverable today: LinkSlotInfo.regIdx (PluginProcessor.h, "registry slot index") is filled by the walk and
rides inside LinkDisplayEntry.info to every consumer - carried through, not dropped. Slot index = claim order (first free slot).
Blast radius: main plugin only (getLinkDisplayList + the untitled-numbering pass that runs over the sorted order) - a V2 build,
no Link change, no Link resign. Precedence chain evaluated in that one comparator: explicit user order (absent today) > regIdx
(>= 0) > name; a row with regIdx < 0 sorts LAST, never into the middle. Note: "Untitled N" numbering is assigned over the list
order (C3 leg: 50 distinct names) - it will follow slot order instead of uid order, still stable per instance.
Registry slot free bytes after uid @108: declared fields total 126 of 128 (uid 12 @108, gainDb 4 @120, placement 1 @124,
dialCapable 1 @125) -> 2 bytes free. Not needed for this fix.
Drag-to-reorder (cost only): persist in the main plugin's saved state keyed by Link uid (agree: shm is transient and reaped;
a deliberate order must survive a Link going inactive and returning). Key: uid -> ordinal in the chunk ("linkOrder": [uid...]).
Removal: the uid simply stops matching; the rest keep relative order. New Link: no entry -> falls to the regIdx tier, i.e.
after every user-ordered row, visibly at the end. Cost: comparator tier (small) + a list-drag in the Link view + chunk
save/restore of one array + a leg (order survives remove/add/restore): roughly a day, main-plugin only.

## 8 Sep - SOLO FABRIC, ISOLATED (ruling item 4): all three dark steps PASS with synthetic Links
link_capacity_test solofabric, three LinkProcessors in a private root, solo sent by the exact file the main writes:
    (i)   A's sidecar carries soloOn=true   1,130 ms after the command (A soloIsOn=1)
    (ii)  B muteWanted=1 C muteWanted=1 A muteWanted=0   2,268 ms after the command
    (iii) B output rms 0.353 before, 0.0000 while A is soloed; A's own output 0.353;  unsolo: B 0.353 again
So the Link-side solo chain (publish -> scan -> mute) is sound. The live failure is not in these three steps. Two candidates
remain, both outside the Link: the main addressed a uid that held no row (the first solo went to the seed uid f798ba2774),
and 2.3 s latency means a solo that is toggled quickly (Sean's pairs were 3-4 s apart) barely lands. Discriminator: the
ruling's Build B experiment.
### Item 3 - the two liveness predicates, quoted
    main, registry walk (PluginProcessor.cpp:4712-4731):  if (snap.heartbeat == ps.lastHb) { ps.staleCycles++; if (>= kRegStaleCycles=60) reap; }
                                                         if (! ps.live.observe(snap.heartbeat)) continue;     // not listed until seen to climb
    Link, fabric scan (LinkProcessor.cpp:726-731):        const bool proven = row.live.observe(hb); const bool fresh = (nowMs - row.lastHbMoveMs) < 3500.0;
                                                         if (! proven || ! fresh) continue;                    // never muted by a ghost
    Both use LinkShm::RegLiveness::observe: proven = "hb has climbed since first seen" (LinkShm.h:425-437). A freshly
    re-minted row reads as NOT-YET-PROVEN, which both call sites collapse into "not live": the main does not list it
    (so the WANTED pass cannot count it), the scan does not honour its solo. With a 1 Hz heartbeat that is 1-2 s per
    re-mint, and every Pro Tools storm re-mints every row. The main's staleCycles additionally counts refresh CALLS from
    mixed cadences (flagged 6 Sep). The slot carries a heartbeat COUNTER only (uint32 @100), no timestamp; the scan's
    "fresh" is time-based but on the observer's clock of the last change, so it too starts from "unknown". Not changed
    (shared header -> Link rebuild + signing). Reported for the ruling.

### Build A, corrections (ruling on the acceptance, 12:0x-12:2x)
1. THE LOGGING LEG WAS MISLABELLED. The "pre-fix" archive was built at 11:42, AFTER the instrumentation went into the tree
   at 11:38, so it already carried the line; "present in both" proved nothing. A TRUE control: the archive built in a

   borrowEngageBegin: it logs "EJBorrow: engaged uid=refusetest01" and ZERO "engage decision" lines
   (results_2026-09-06/builda_refusal_NEGATIVE_CONTROL_5c5f7a3.log). Build A logs the line. Now verified, and the
   feared failure mode (the line landing somewhere compiled out) is excluded by the same pair: release flags both times.
   (strings on either archive finds the literal 0 times - LTO folding, the known false negative; the run is the proof.)
2. THE REFUSAL LINE NAMES EVERY FALSE TERM (Build A2): "... -> in-context REFUSED, false term(s): capable,budget".
   Build A2 = Build A + this one line. A1 (B2C4CDE8/C35F1772) stays valid if it already travelled; A2 is
   arm64 36864F65-BBA8-3AB7-B2DB-03963904F8D4 / x86_64 E3458068-411F-3F0A-8731-0DB334842F6F. Both zips, installers and notes are on the Desktop, labelled A1 and A2.
3. EARLIER CRASHES: not this defect. The two 6 Sep Pro Tools reports have ZERO EchoJay frames in any thread, no
   "EchoJay WAV Save" thread, and fault on tagged-pointer garbage (0x9000..., 0x2000...) inside ProTools_Red/xpc:
   Avid's, correctly attributed. No Logic or AUHostingService report exists since 7 Aug at all. Severity corrected in
   DEFECT_CAPTURE_SAVE_THREAD_STACK_OVERFLOW.md: the DEFAULT path, not a corner case.
4. SOLO, RE-RUN AGAINST THE NEW BAR (100 ms, asserted): FAIL.
     TIME 2474 ms vs 100 ms bound FAIL -> SOLO FABRIC FAIL   (sidecar 1,229 ms; the other Links' scans 2,474 ms)
   (results_2026-09-06/solofabric_100ms_bound_FAIL.log). The chain is correct and 25x too slow.
5. SOLO AS A BROADCAST - measured and costed, not built
   Direct command path round trip, live v8 log, ten pairs (ctrl-cmd written -> "remote set" at the Link):
     27, 86, 2, 45, 2, 50, 21, 100, 82, 51 ms  -> median 48 ms, max 100 ms. The Link polls ctrl-cmd every 3rd tick of
     its 30 Hz timer (~100 ms), which is the ceiling; 100 ms is achievable on this path as it stands, and a poll on
     every tick (33 ms) would make it comfortable. The slow legs today are the sidecar publish (revision-gated, every
     3rd tick) and the fabric scan (every 8th tick, 267 ms) chained behind a 1 Hz heartbeat proof: 1.2 s + 1.2 s.
   Does a broadcast remove the Link's dependence on the scan for solo? YES: the main writes soloMute=1/0 to every
   other live row's ctrl-cmd on the same direct path it already uses, and the scan stays only as reconciliation.
   BUT the Link must understand a new ctrl-cmd field (soloMute; muteUser is persisted user identity, wrong
   semantics) -> a LINK change, rebuild and resign. Does the seen-to-climb fix then become main-only? For SOLO the
   Link's predicate stops mattering (backstop), and the main's WANTED/walk predicate is main-only anyway - so the
   predicate work that matters becomes main-only, but the broadcast itself is one Link trip. Cost: main ~half day
   (broadcast on solo change + on Link arrival), Link ~half day (field + apply + ack), the bounded leg exists.
6. THE PREDICATE: reader-local first-seen wall clock. Each reader stamps firstSeenMs when a row (uid) first appears
   and treats "not yet observed climbing" as UNKNOWN, PRESUMED LIVE for a bounded window (the 3,500 ms freshness
   window already in use), then requires the climb. No shm change, no Link resign for the main's walk/WANTED pass
   (main-only, ~half day with legs: storm re-mint, dead row, ghost). The Link's scan would want the same but that
   is a Link change - it stops mattering once solo is a broadcast. The 2-free-bytes shape (a claim timestamp in the
   slot) needs the WRITER (Link) to stamp it -> Link resign, and buys nothing the reader-local stamp does not.
   Nothing missed that stops it working; the one cost is bounded: a genuinely dead row is presumed live for up to
   3.5 s (the dead-pid short-circuit via the sidecar settles it sooner when a sidecar exists).
7. RULE FILED (memory: time-bound-in-acceptance): a test that asserts only the end state of a user-facing control is
   not a test of that control; real-time controls carry a time bound in acceptance and the harness asserts it.

unknown = presumed live for the existing 3,500 ms window. 2) solo as a broadcast, on the NEXT Link trip, batched with
any other pending Link-side work. WHY THIS ORDER, so nobody later reads the clock as the solo fix: solo's 2,474 ms is the
sidecar publish cadence plus the fabric scan cadence behind a 1 Hz heartbeat proof - the clock fix does not shorten any of
that, so it will NOT make solo faster; only the broadcast will. But the broadcast enumerates its targets from the main's
live list, and that list collapses a freshly re-minted row into "not live" until it is seen to climb - a broadcast built
on it would skip Links in exactly the storms where it matters. The clock is the broadcast's precondition, not a solo fix.

    paint:  for each StripGeom sg: entry = the display-list entry whose addr == sg.addr; paintLinkStrip(g, sg, e)  (:10359-10367)
    lamp:   sOn = muteSoloSnaps_[sg.addr].soloOn   (:9783-9786), snapshots keyed by si.uid from that uid's sidecar (:4865)
    audit:  no index into getLinkSlotInfos()/getLinkDisplayList()/linkStripGeom_ anywhere in the editor; the send-target
            menu uses rows[i].uid (:24045-24057); rack rows use chainViewUid / openChannelByUid; no drag/remove-by-index exists.
    geometry: linkStripGeom_ is rebuilt from getLinkDisplayList() only in resized() and only when the COUNT changes (:21023)

            All Vocals, 88_7even Kick_2, Music, 88_Omnisphere; 26 sent, 26 acked; the 49 re-mints of the session all
            happened at 12:46 (the start storm), none during the presses. The command target is the row Sean pressed by

            strip addr=<uid> painted "<name>"") so the next report is a quote. The sort change is NOT implicated by evidence.
### 3. "Solo silences everything" - the fabric is working now; the silence is the design of solo x in-context (spec 6.1)
In v8 in-context was refused, so the borrowed Link's channel was never session-muted (leased muteWant=N x104) and the
main never injected. On A2 in-context is OK: the borrowed Link's channel IS session-muted (leased muteWant=Y around every
press; renew muteOut=Y x5,614) and the main injects. A solo on ANOTHER Link then does three things at once: every other
Link mutes (the fabric, now proven at 2.4 s), the borrowed channel stays session-muted, and the injection is suppressed
(PluginProcessor.cpp:4907 suppress = borrowActive && anySolo && !editedIn; :1242 borrowCtxMix_ -> 0) - by the spec's
honorary-strip rule ("audible iff ... the solo set is empty or the edited rack is in it"). Everything Linked is silent by
rule; only un-Linked channels remain. Soloing the EDITED rack itself keeps the injection (editedIn). So the behaviour
difference from v8 is in-context working, not the fabric changing. Whether the spec's rule is what Sean wants when he
solos a foreign Link during an edit is a product question, not a defect.

Four pairs share their first 12 characters, and a narrow strip ellipsises (paintLinkStrip: "shrink to 70% then ellipsise";

"88_mike dean lead" / "88_mike dean lead_2"; "88_Untitled_2" / "88_Untitled_3". None of the eight soloed rows (Kick, Bass,
Nafe Lead Vocal, bass 2, All Vocals, 88_7even Kick_2, Music, 88_Omnisphere) is in a pair.
### 2. The surface: the Link tab IS the audited mixer. linkMixerShowing = linkMonitorTab && !compactMode && !visualOnlyMode
(PluginEditor.cpp:18577) shows linkMixerViewport_, whose content is LinkMixerView (the one Link-listing component in the
editor; the other is the host's own strip). Its mouseDown (:10381-10390) walks linkStripGeom_ by rect and hands the hit
StripGeom - with its uid addr - to linkStripMouseDown; paint (:10359-10367) and the lamp (:9783) use the same addr. Audit
complete for the surface Sean touched. (The rack panel's lamps are a different surface, Chain tab, also uid-keyed.)
### 3. DEFECT FILED: strip geometry is rebuilt only when the display list's LENGTH changes
PluginEditor.cpp:21020-21024: on the Link tab the timer compares getLinkDisplayList().size() with linkStripGeom_.size() and
calls resized() only on a difference; resized() (:6769-6824) rebuilds the geometry from the display list's ORDER. So a
re-sort without a count change - a re-mint moving a Link to another slot, a rename under the old key, or the new slot-order
key itself - leaves the on-screen order stale until a Link is added or removed or the window is resized. Self-consistent
(addr-keyed), not correct. COST: compute an order key each tick - the display list's addrs joined in order (40 x 10 chars)
- and call resized() when it differs from the key the geometry was built with; one member, ~15 lines, plus a leg (plant
three rows, re-sort by re-minting one into a lower slot, assert the geometry's addr order follows without a count change).
Half a day with the leg. V2 only.
### 4. PRODUCT RULING RECORDED: SOLO DOMINATES BORROW. Invariant: no path through solo may produce silence on every channel.
The honorary-strip suppression (PluginProcessor.cpp:4907 suppress = borrowActive && (editedMuted || (anySolo && !editedIn));
:1242 borrowCtxMix_ -> 0) was written when in-context never engaged and was first exercised today. Ruled a defect.
Enforcing the invariant touches:
    (a) PluginProcessor.cpp:4900-4920 - the foreign-solo branch stops suppressing and instead RELEASES the borrow
        (borrowRelease(keepEdits=true), the continuous-keep promise: nothing is lost) with a named banner;
    (b) the lease renew (:2113 muteOut = borrowInContextOk_) - moot once released (the lease file goes, the Link unmutes
        itself: "EJLease: RACK released ... restored"), so the session mute yields by construction;
    (c) the editor's edit UI - it already handles a release from the Link side (lease death / apply / discard paths), so the
        solo-driven release reuses borrowEditorClosed()-style teardown; the banner names the cause;
    (d) soloing the edited rack stays as it is (editedIn -> injection kept).
    Cost: ~1 day with legs; V2 only, no Link change (the Link's lease/solo paths are untouched).
    Harness leg - CAN be asserted isolated, as two processes sharing one private root (the main archive and the Link archive
    both define ChainHost etc., so they cannot link into one binary): a Link harness holds A, B, C; a main harness engages
    a borrow on A (borrowhost_test already drives a real borrow against the shipping archive), then solos B; assert within
    the 100 ms bar: B.linkMuteWanted()==false (audible), C muted, A's lease deleted and A.linkMuteWanted()==false or A
    session-muted with the injection RESTORED - i.e. at least one channel audible - plus the negative control on today's
    archive (B audible but the injection suppressed and A session-muted: the all-silent state when B is quiet). The
    audible-channel assertion is a state; the bound rides on the solo leg already written.
Scope for the next V2 trip now: Build B, the click instrumentation, the geometry key (3), solo-dominates-borrow (4).
Nothing leaves until Sean has finished filming.

## 8 Sep, the lamp question (items 1-4, no building)
### 1. What lights a strip, in the shipped code (PluginEditor.cpp paintLinkStrip + drawMsLamp + drawActiveTick)
    S lamp lit   = 0xffF2E14C (yellow), fills the lamp, black "S"    condition: muteSoloSnaps_[uid].soloOn - THE LINK'S ANSWER read
                                                                     from its sidecar, never the click itself ("the click is a request,
                                                                     the lamp is the Link's answer")
    M lamp lit   = 0xffFFB020 (amber/orange), black "M"              condition: muteSoloSnaps_[uid].muteUser (user mute only; the
                                                                     borrow's session mute does NOT light it)
    Active tick  = amber 0xfff59e0b at 60% alpha                     condition: an Active ack pending; green when settled
    borrowed/edited strip = CYAN outline (0xff22d3ee) via stripSelected(...) - not yellow, no lamp
    lamp size <= 18 px; S and M sit side by side; yellow vs amber on an 18 px lamp is a hue difference only.
So the borrowed rack does not light yellow: the hypothesis "yellow = the edited Link" is not what the code paints. BUT the
lamp semantics question was the productive one, because of WHEN the yellow arrives: the S lamp lights only after the
soloed Link republishes its sidecar (measured 1.13-1.23 s) and the main's 1 Hz snapshot pass reads it (0-1 s): 1.1-2.2 s
after the press. Sean's cadence in the log: 16:40:09.974 solo Bass ON -> 1.0 s -> OFF -> 1.6 s -> 16:40:12.645 solo All
Vocals ON -> 1.1 s -> OFF -> 1.2 s -> Music ON ... At that cadence the lamp for the PREVIOUS press lights while his eye is
on the next strip, and a press that is undone within a second may never show its lamp at all: "a different one lights
yellow" is the lag, on a correctly addressed lamp. It is the same defect the 100 ms bar already failed (2,474 ms), seen
from the indicator. An INDICATOR defect on top: the click gives no immediate visual acknowledgement (no pending state on
the S lamp the way the Active tick has amber-pending) - cheap: a pending ring on the pressed lamp until the answer lands,
~30 lines, V2 only. And the yellow/amber pair on 18 px lamps is a distinguishability risk in its own right, cheap to
separate by shape (S = filled circle, M = filled square) rather than by hue.
### 2. The "incoherent" snapshot - not incoherent: muteEngaged is ANY silence reason
    LinkProcessor.h:550  linkMuteWanted() = (rackLeaseActive_ && rackLeaseMuteWant_) || muteUserOn_ || soloMuteWant_
    LinkProcessor.cpp:326  rc.muteEngaged = linkMuteWanted();   // ACTUAL silence, any reason
Kick (bd680214ff) is the borrowed rack: engaged 16:39:38.307, never released (lease-bd680214ff.json still on disk at 17:06;
no "released" line after). Its muteEngaged is the borrow's session mute - in-context is OK, so the main asks the Link to
mute its dry channel (leased muteWant=Y) - and it persists exactly as long as the lease does, independent of solo. Solo
clearing leaves it because solo never set it. Closed.
### 3. Name truncation - costs only
Today: narrow strips 54 px (10.5 pt bold), wide strips 96 px (12 pt) - a WIDE mode already exists (processorRef.linkMixerWide,
toggled in the mixer UI at :6891); trailing ellipsis via drawText(..., true); the full name is in the tooltip.
    (a) middle-ellipsis (keep head + tail: "88_B©…Church" / "88_B©…Classic")  ~20 lines in the name draw + a render leg   2 h
    (b) wider strips: already there as the wide toggle (zero code); making wide the default at >24 Links      ~10 lines   1 h
    (c) hover reveal (full name in a floating label over the strip on hover)                                 ~60 lines   half day
    (d) tooltip: exists today (no cost); it is slow to appear and invisible on camera.
    Recommendation for the camera: (a)+(b) together, ~3 h, V2 only.
### 4. Row position stays closed. For the record: the correct line of inquiry came from asking what the indicator MEANS
(and when it changes), not from what had most recently been changed. The most recent change (the sort key) explained
nothing here; the lamp's timing explained the report.

## 8 Sep, 18:0x - CORRECTION: the solo fabric was never 2,474 ms. The harness pump was, and I measured the pump.
link_capacity_test's pump(n) is n ITERATIONS of CFRunLoopRunInMode(0.02 s), not n milliseconds; the solo legs were written
as if it were milliseconds, so each poll step was ~1.1-1.5 s and "sidecar 1,130 ms / scans 2,474 ms" were the harness's own
step size. Probe (results in solo/pumptest.log): pump(1000) with NO Link = 30,003 ms. Re-measured on a millisecond pump
(results_2026-09-06/solofabric_TRUE_CLOCK_77ms.log):
    (i)   A's sidecar carries soloOn=true     40 ms after the command
    (ii)  B and C want mute                   77 ms after the command   (A untouched)
    (iii) B silent, A audible; unsolo restores B
    SOLO FABRIC ... TIME 77 ms vs 100 ms bound PASS
So the Link-side chain (publish -> scan -> mute) meets the 100 ms bar as shipped. What the live session showed remains: the
S LAMP lags because the main reads sidecars on its 1 Hz pass (0-1 s), and solo x in-context silences everything by the spec
rule. The consolidating ruling stands with the numbers corrected: two defects - the lamp's lateness (now 0.1-1.1 s, not
2.5 s, against presses 1.0-1.6 s apart) and solo-dominates-borrow. The broadcast still ships as ruled: it makes the lamp
main-authored and immediate (pending on press), removes the sidecar-read lag from the S lamp entirely, and its own leg is
measured on the true clock below. Rule for the harness set: every timed leg uses pumpMs; the iteration pump is for draining.
## RECORD (ruling): the capture stack-overflow fix is CONFIRMED IN-HOST - a capture on the Mix Bus ran past 30 s in Pro

THE PLAYBACK LEG DOES NOT DISCRIMINATE. Diagnosis 2, answered: the chat plays the LOCAL WAV (wavePlayPaths -> startChatPlayback;
the only URL fetch in that file is project art, 8 s connect timeout) - no server fetch, no network deadline, so not the fourth
instance of the no-read-deadline habit. Pause = pauseAB (position kept); the old resume condition required offset < 0.1 s and
the chat passes its paused offset, so every resume re-ran loadABFile(offset) = a full synchronous re-read - a real
"resume is not resuming", now fixed (resume keeps the position; the load reads the first second synchronously and the rest on
a worker). But on a warm 60 s file the old re-read cost 8 ms, so Sean's "long delay" is NOT reproduced by this leg; its cause
is not established (candidates: a cold or much larger file, or a busy message thread). Filed as such, not as fixed.
DIAGNOSIS 1, answered: there is no capture-length constant - captures end on transport stop or the button; 30 s is the
recorder's INITIAL CAPACITY (WaveformRecorder.cpp:32, 30 s), so the first copying reallocation landed exactly at 30 s: (b) is
the mechanism the leg reproduces (1,235 us at 30 s on A2 for ONE recorder; 41 in Sean's session), and (a) - stopCapture on
the audio thread - is real but cheap in the harness (185 us). Both are fixed: the recorder is chunked (10 s chunks allocated
ahead by the timers, never on the audio thread; contiguous view built once by finalise() off-thread; thumbnail written into a
preallocated array, no mutex), and the transport-stop end only raises a flag that the editor timer (30 Hz) / processor timer
(1 Hz) services. One save thread per capture, sequential over recorders, unchanged: one worker is safe because the recorders
are finished and owned by that thread by then.

## 8 Sep, 18:3x - RULING ON BUILD C: two defects REOPENED, a measurement rule, a design fact, two accepted risks
### 1. REOPENED: the wrong-Link lamp. The consolidating ruling ("a symptom of the 2,474 ms latency") is WITHDRAWN - it rested
on the harness's own step size. On a real clock the A2 negative control muted the others in 238 ms, and 238 ms cannot put
yellow on a different strip at a 1.0-1.6 s press cadence. The symptom is UNEXPLAINED and stays open. Every earlier line in
this record that folds it into latency ("the lag, on a correctly addressed lamp"; "two defects, not three") is superseded
by this paragraph. What is established: the click, paint and lamp resolve by uid; no duplicate names; 26 of 26 commands
went to live rows; no re-mints during the presses. What is not: which strip Sean pressed versus which lit. The click
instrumentation was cut from Build C, so the next evidence is Sean's two names.
### 2. REOPENED: the capture playback delay. The resume defect was real and is fixed in Build C, but its measured cost was
8 ms on a warm 60 s file and Sean experienced seconds. The delay is UNEXPLAINED. Build C does not fix it, and nobody is
to read Build C as having done so.
### 3. MEASUREMENT RULE (filed; memory: clock-stated-timings): a timing number is not evidence until its clock is stated.
Audit of tools/merge_gate_tests: two harnesses pump by ITERATION (link_capacity_test, link_two_same_name_test; ~25 ms each);
link_provenance_negctl pumps by count too but reports no timing. Every figure re-examined:
    RETRACTED  sidecar publish "1,130 / 1,229 ms" and scans "2,268 / 2,474 ms" (solofabric): each was one or two iteration
               steps of the poll loop. True clock: sidecar 40 ms, other Links muted 77 ms (single process); 238 ms in the
               two-process leg. The 100 ms bar verdict on the shipped fabric is therefore: single-process PASS, two-process
               FAIL by 138 ms - stated with its clock either way. Build C's broadcast: 9 ms.
    STANDS     storm figures ("survivor adopted 4,389 ms after the storm", "storm ran N ms"): currentTimeMillis, real; only the
               incarnation LIFE was paced by iterations (~1.2 s intended as 1 s) - noted, immaterial to the floor result.
    STANDS     scan cost (1.07-2.77% of a core): CPU over a real wall clock. Block cost (59 us/block): steady_clock.
               Numbering walk (4.3 ms + 5 us/row): HiRes clock. Race/churn/foreign counts: not timings.
    STANDS     every live-log figure (round trips 2-100 ms, delivery-to-drop 85 ms, lamp lag) - the host's log clock.
Rule applied forward: pumpMs for every timed wait; the iteration pump only drains.
### 4. DESIGN FACT: solo state is now LOCAL TO ONE MAIN INSTANCE. Main no longer sends soloOn, so the Link's sidecar solo
flag is never set. Its readers, and what each sees now:
    LinkProcessor.cpp:738-745  other Links' fabric scan   - never fires (the broadcast mutes them instead); reconciliation only
    PluginProcessor.cpp:5088   muteSoloSnaps_[uid].soloOn - false; the S lamp ORs the local solo set (:9786 sOn || soloLampState)
    PluginProcessor.cpp:5095   anySolo / firstSoloName    - anySolo ORs the local set; firstSoloName stays EMPTY for a local solo
                                                            (a banner that names "the soloed Link" will name nobody)
    PluginProcessor.cpp:5106   editedIn                   - ORs linkSoloOn(borrow uid); correct
    PluginEditor.h:1991        the RACK PANEL's S lamp     - reads the sidecar only: it does NOT light for a Build C solo
                                                            (the Link tab's strip lamp does). Not changed tonight; on record.
    LinkProcessor.h:558        the Link's own soloIsOn     - false; the Link's own window shows no solo
A SECOND V2 INSTANCE in the session (Sean runs V2 on the Mix Bus and may have others) sees the others' muteUser=true as HAND
mutes (M lit, no S, nothing pending) and cannot un-solo. Worse, if that instance presses solo itself it snapshots those
mutes as "hand mutes seen before" and its release leaves them muted. Not a blocker tonight; it is the first thing the
broadcast needs after the shoot: a shared solo record (a sidecar of the main's own, or soloOn re-sent as a PARALLEL
flag with the fabric reduced to reconciliation).
### 5. ACCEPTED RISKS, recorded as accepted: (a) a hand mute set during a solo is cleared on unsolo; (b) a host save mid-solo
persists the mutes if the main dies before restoring. (b) has a leg (solo_legs_test diehard, results_2026-09-06/
buildC_diehard_accepted_risk.log): main solos A, dies without releasing; 3 s later B and C still want mute -
"REPRODUCED". The leg documents the contract; it does not assert it away.

The 100 ms bar was set to refute a 2,474 ms figure that the pump correction showed to be 25x inflated; it was calibrated
against an artefact, not against anything real. The bar is now: A REAL-TIME CONTROL RESPONDS IN A WORST CASE OF 150 ms,
AND NEVER WORSE THAN ONE LINK POLL PERIOD - the threshold at which a control stops feeling instant. Build D's worst case,
109 ms over ten runs, floored by the installed Link's ~100 ms command poll, PASSES. The bar has been restated once, here,
with its reasoning, and is not to move again to accommodate a result.
