## 9 Sep - RULING ON THE TRACE (accepted): the substitution hypothesis is REFUTED - the fourth of the reviewer's this round
(row-position, budget-never-commits, yellow-means-borrow, downstream substitution). The log found three real mechanisms and
one of them - the quick re-press reading a stale sidecar snapshot - is the answer to Sean's report.
### THE BUG CLASS, fourth instance today: AUTHORITATIVE STATE INFERRED FROM A LAGGY SECONDARY SOURCE
    1. seen-to-climb liveness (a row is "not live" until a counter is observed to move)
    2. the count-vs-time gates (C4, staleCycles)
    3. sidecar solo discovery (the S lamp waiting for the Link's answer through a 1 Hz pass)
    4. the hand-mute snapshot: the main imposed 39 mutes, then rediscovered them 0.7 s later from the sidecar pass as
       "hand mutes" and refused to touch its own work
Rule: the main owns an authoritative local record of every command it issued, written at the moment it sends, and consults
THAT - never a shared surface that lags behind its own actions.
### Build E scope and shapes (nothing built yet)
E1  Clause 3: on every solo press X is EXPLICITLY un-muted (muteUser=0 sent to X regardless of what any record or snapshot
    says), whatever muted it - a previous main, a session mute, a stale snapshot. Omitting X from the mute set is not
    un-muting X.
E2  The authoritative mute record: the main keeps, per uid, the last command it sent (mute/unmute, seq, time) for the life
    of the instance. A uid counts as hand-muted only if the sidecar says muted AND this main has NEVER commanded it; for
    every uid it has commanded, its own record is the truth. The snapshot no longer classifies anything the main touched.
    (Accepted risk (a) stands: a hand mute applied after our un-mute is invisible until the next solo cycle.)
E3  NO SILENT SOLO ON RACK SELECTION (overruling the current design). Preferred shape: REMOVE the automatic fallback route
    - applyBorrowSoloMixOn's crossfade of the main's whole output to the borrowed ring runs only behind an explicit,
    labelled user act (none exists today, so it does not run). Selecting a rack whose in-context is REFUSED leaves the
    main's passthrough untouched and the Link's channel audible (the lease is renewed with muteOut=N), and the rack panel
    shows the reason as a waiting/held state: "Waiting for the mix budget - blends at the next stop" for the budget term
    (Build B then raises it on the commit, seconds), or "Cannot blend in context: <capable/pad/mute unconfirmed> - solo
    this rack to hear the edit" for the permanent terms. Why this shape over a visible fallback: the fallback replaced the
    user's whole mix with one rack without a press - a visible label would only explain a thing that should not happen;
    the waiting state matches Sean's model (selecting blends, soloing is a separate act), and Build B already delivers
    the blend seconds later without any new mechanism. Cost ~1.5 h + a leg (select with the budget uncommitted: main
    output == its passthrough, Link channel audible, label present; then a STOPPED block: injection starts).
E4  Mechanism 2 (spec 6.1 stands, ruled): soloing the edited rack keeps its channel session-muted and the injection is the
    audible copy. Cost of an indicator that the user is hearing the PROCESSED path: the rack panel's S lamp tooltip and
    the sticky banner gain "Hearing the processed edit in context - the raw channel is muted while this session is live",
    driven by (soloSet contains borrow uid && in-context OK): ~30 lines + a string leg, ~1 h. NOT built now.
E5  The rendered-audio leg replaces the flag leg: three Links, a borrow on A engaged IN-CONTEXT (real budget commit in the
    harness), the main's own output rendered with A's ring injected; solo B COLD, then release, then solo B again within
    ONE SECOND (Sean's cadence). Assert on rendered audio each time: B non-silent, the main's injection silent, C silent.
    Negative control on Build D: the rapid re-press must FAIL (the stale snapshot leaves C audible).
HOURS: rendered leg 2 h (the injection needs a committed budget inside the harness), E1+E2 1 h, E3 1.5 h, legs with
negative controls 1.5 h, build/verify/package 1.5 h -> about 7.5 h before the zip leaves, plus the signing trip.

## BUILD E (9 Sep, 10:5x) - built as approved, three conditions met
Condition 1  The fallback is UNREACHABLE, not deleted: applyBorrowSoloMixOn (the crossfade of the main's whole output to
             the borrowed ring, PluginProcessor.cpp:2046) keeps its mechanism; its one call site now passes `false` with a
             comment at the site stating it is reachable only from an explicit user act that does not exist yet.
Condition 2  The held states are STATUS, not error: "Waiting for the mix budget - <rack> blends at the next stop." is
             carried with borrowBannerIsStatus_=true and paints in the panel's calm text colour (the status painter colours
             only "Failed/Error" red and "Loading" blue); "Cannot blend <rack> in context - <reason>. Solo this rack to hear
             the edit." is a real limitation and reads firmly. On Build B's restore the banner reads "Blending <rack> in
             context."
Condition 3  ACCEPTED PRE-COMMIT BEHAVIOUR, in these words: with the fallback removed, selecting a rack before the budget
             commits leaves the Link's raw channel audible and the edit inaudible until Build B blends it seconds later.
             That is accepted, not a regression.

    classification for every Link this main has ever commanded; the sidecar pass classifies only Links it never touched.
    Accepted risk (a) stands as stated.

output with A's ring injected): render (in-context on A proven by the injection's rms, then solo B COLD, then release and
re-press B 0.7 s later - assert B audible, injection silent, C silent each time), select (engage A with the budget
uncommitted: passthrough intact by correlation with the input tone, A's raw channel audible, waiting banner; then a
STOPPED block: the injection starts), clause3 (main #1 mutes B and C and dies; main #2 solos B: B must be audible).
Each against Build D's kept archive and header as the negative control.
### The rendered-audio leg: what it renders and the one thing it cannot (stated, not hidden)
Two 1 Hz diagnostics went into the product for this (system log, audio-thread counters): "EJCtx(ring): consumed n/want
peak-in peak-after-rack" at the borrow consume, and "EJCtx(inj): ctxNow injPad padKey muteReady soloSup have mix injPeak
ringAge" at the injection mix. They showed the harness main consuming A's ring at full level (peak-in 0.500, unchanged
after the rack) while every injection gate was open, yet injPeak 0.000: the alignment pad key follows the ring age, and
between two independently wall-clock-paced processes the age jitters by hundreds of samples (1024 / 1302 / 1419 / 1768),
so the pad key changes and the code clears its delay line and resets the mix on every change (PluginProcessor.cpp
:1262-1266, by design). In a host both run inside one callback and the age is constant. The harness cannot reproduce
that lockstep. So the leg asserts, on rendered audio: B non-silent, C silent, the main's output silent after the press;
and for "the injection was live" it asserts the product's own consume peak (A's audio entering the injection path while
in-context was OK) rather than the injected audio at the output. Recorded as a harness limit, not a product finding.

## 9 Sep, the BUSSED-SOLO defect - mechanism confirmed, the corrected rule examined, failure cases, nothing built
MECHANISM (Build D/E session, pid 3795): the 10:28:05 press soloed "bass 2" and broadcast muteUser=1 to the other 39 of 40
rows - every bus Link in the session ("Bass", "Drums", "Music", "FX", "All Vocals" are Links too) included, by construction
of "every other Link mutes". A soloed track's audio reaches the mix through a bus that carries a muted Link: silence on the
way out. The specification described the Links as peers; Sean's session is in series. Confirmed.
### Item 2 - placement as the rank: THERE IS NO PLACEMENT DATA IN SEAN'S SESSION
Placement (0 unset / 1 bus / 2 insert / 3 send return) is USER-SET per Link, from the main's strip menu or a remote
command (LinkProcessor.cpp:1841 setPlacement; the restore path re-applies a saved value silently). Three whole-session
logs (v8, A2, Build D) contain ZERO "placement set to" lines. Nothing suggests Sean has ever set one; the default is
unset. So "rank by placement" has no ordering to rank on today - it fails as immediately as ranking by channel type would.
What placement could give if it were set: three ranks (insert < send return / bus). What it cannot distinguish: WHICH bus
a track feeds (membership), two buses at the same rank (siblings vs nested), a bus that is itself a source of another bus
(nested buses share the rank "bus"), and a send return's source track. Rank is not topology.
### Item 1 and item 3 - where the corrected rule ("mute at or below X's rank, leave above it alone") is wrong
The rule's reasoning is right for one direction only: a bus carries only what feeds it, so muting X's PEERS and leaving
X's DOWNSTREAM path open is safe and sufficient - that is the track-solo case, and the rule fixes it.
It is wrong in these cases:
  a) SOLOING A BUS. "Mute at or below X's rank" mutes the inserts - X's own sources - and X goes silent. Soloing a bus
     must keep its upstream sources audible. The host does exactly that (the sources feeding a soloed aux keep feeding
     it); the rule inverts it.
  b) NESTED BUSES. A bus feeding another bus shares the rank "bus": soloing the lower bus mutes the upper one (same rank)
     and kills the path out. Placement cannot see the nesting.
  c) TWO PARALLEL BUSES at the same rank where the user wants one heard: soloing bus P mutes sibling bus Q (same rank) -
     correct - but ALSO mutes every insert, including P's own sources (case a) - wrong. Siblings are right, sources wrong.
  d) A SEND RETURN fed from X's send: at rank "send return" (above insert) it stays audible - correct by accident; a send
     return fed from a MUTED track's send is silent anyway. Placement cannot tell whose send feeds it; the rule survives
     only because the bus carries silence by itself.
  e) THE MAIN NOT ON THE FINAL BUS: the rule mutes Links, not the main; Links downstream of the main are untouched (above
     every rank) - survives. The main's own injection follows the existing solo-dominates-borrow rule.
  f) TWO INDEPENDENT CHAINS both ranked "bus": soloing a track in chain 1 leaves chain 2's bus unmuted but mutes its
     sources - chain 2 passes silence - survives. Soloing chain 1's bus mutes chain 2's bus (same rank) and, again, its own
     sources (case a) - wrong.
THE RULE THAT SURVIVES is topological, not ranked: when X is soloed, leave audible everything ON X's PATH - its upstream
sources and its downstream buses - and mute every Link that is on NEITHER (the other branches). For a track that is
"mute the other inserts, leave every bus"; for a bus it is "mute the other branches' inserts and sibling buses, leave my
sources and my path out". Placement cannot supply the path. What can, today, without new data: NOTHING for the bus case.
The narrower rule that survives with the data we have: (1) soloing an INSERT/UNSET Link mutes only other insert/unset
Links and never a bus or send-return Link - but with every Link unset, "bus" is unknowable, so even this needs the user to
mark the buses; (2) soloing a bus Link is NOT supported until the topology exists - it does nothing rather than silence
the wrong thing, and says so. Topology must come from the session: a per-Link "feeds" declaration in the main (which
bus this Link's track feeds, persisted by uid), or inference from audio (a Link's signal present in a bus Link's ring),
which is a later, measured project.
### Item 5 - THE LESSON, recorded: every leg to date modelled the Links as peers because the specification described them
as peers. The specification was the defect and the harness inherited it. A harness built from a spec cannot catch an
error in that spec - the topology has to come from the user's actual session, not from the document.

## 9 Sep - placement-rank REFUTED and withdrawn (the reviewer's fifth this round). The failure had the same shape each
time: a mechanism was proposed, measurement killed it, and the productive move was to change the MODEL, not patch the rule.
## ADDITIVE SOLO - the design examined before anything is built (six answers, from the code)
On solo of X the main replaces its own output with X's ring; no Link is muted; the main sits at the end of the chain. This
is applyBorrowSoloMixOn (PluginProcessor.cpp:2046), made unreachable this morning on the condition that only an explicit
user act may call it. Solo is that act.
### 1. Does a Link publish its ring without a lease? YES.
    LinkProcessor.cpp:1719  if (linkOn || leaseActive_) { ... LinkShm::ringProduce(shmMap, chPtrs, 2, n); }
The ring is written whenever the Link is ON (the lease is the OR-ed alternative for a Link switched off). The ring carries
the Link's OUTPUT - `buffer` after the Link's own chain and gain, before its mute stage. The main already opens and
DRAINS every live ring every block (:1163-1170, ringConsume with null outputs), so X's audio is already arriving at the
main and being thrown away. No lease of any kind is needed; the borrow semantics never enter. Cost of the lightest
lease: zero, because none is taken.
### 2. Alignment: CONFIRMED - none of it applies.
The pad, the budget, the PDC report and the stamped ring seek exist to blend the injected ring against the main's LIVE
passthrough sample-accurately (spec 8.3). Solo REPLACES the output; there is nothing to blend against. The ring's backlog
becomes a fixed monitoring latency: seek to the existing cushion (kEditCushionFrames = 1024 frames, ~21 ms at 48 k) once at
engage and consume one block per block thereafter; if the backlog trips kEditReseekTrip (8192) re-seek, as the borrow
already does. The crossfade site sits BEFORE the main's own chain (:1318 vs chainHost.process :1324), so the soloed ring
still passes through the mix-bus processing - what the user would hear from the host's solo too. Explicitly: solo needs
no alignment, no budget, no PDC event; the largest source of complexity in the feature does not apply.
### 3. Multi-solo: cheap, and WRONG in series.
CPU: one ringConsume + one add per soloed Link per block (the drain already runs) - negligible. Leases: none. Correctness:
summing rings double-counts wherever the solo set spans levels: X feeding a bus that also has a Link puts X into the bus
Link's ring, so X + bus hears X twice (once dry, once through the bus processing). Siblings sum correctly; a set that
spans a feed relation does not, and without topology the main cannot tell which. Honest position: multi-solo of a set
that spans levels is not supportable additively without topology; single solo is exact; multi-solo can be offered as
"last press wins" or restricted to a set the user accepts as siblings.
### 4. What it does not cover.
Anything reaching the monitors without passing through the main's channel: tracks routed directly to the output or to a
parallel bus the main is not on, hardware inserts, a second monitor path. The plugin cannot see routing (AAX has no API
for it), so this is NOT detectable from inside; the guarantee is exactly "the main's output is X" and nothing more. If
the main is not on the final bus, everything on other branches keeps playing under the solo. Sean must know the
guarantee's shape: solo is only complete when the main is the last thing before the monitors.
### 5. What becomes dead if this lands (the size of the simplification)
    setLinkSolo's broadcast (sendLinkCtrlMute over the target list)          ~45 lines   dead
    the target enumeration over linkSlotInfos and reconcileSoloBroadcast          ~20 lines   dead
    the authoritative mute record (cmdRecord), mutedByUs, userMutedBefore         ~25 lines   dead
    the hand-mute snapshot classification and the restore on release              ~20 lines   dead
    the dead-main contract (persisted mutes) and its leg                            contract    gone - nothing is written to any Link
    the liveness dependency for targets (a Link appearing mid-solo)                 gone - only X's ring matters, and it is already drained
    the 100 ms command-poll floor and the pending S lamp (acks)                     gone - the effect is local: one block + a 30 ms fade
    solo-dominates-borrow's release                                                 unnecessary - the output is X regardless; borrow stays
    the two-process solo legs (mute/unmute timing, clause 3, dead main)             replaced by one rendered leg: the main's output IS X
  What stays: the S lamp (main-owned, immediate), spec 6.1 (when X is the borrowed rack the crossfade uses borrowBuf_, the
  PROCESSED ring - the existing code path), the Link's own fabric (unused). Net: roughly 150 lines of the main's solo path
  and every cross-process guarantee it needed go; what remains is one consume and one crossfade that already exist.
### 6. What we lose - where subtractive solo is better
    a) THE MIX POSITION OF X. The ring is X's insert-point signal: pre-fader, pre-pan, pre-automation, without any bus
       processing between X and the main (bus compressors, sends, the bus Link's own rack). The host's solo, and a
       subtractive solo, hear X where it sits in the mix; additive solo hears X at its Link. For a level-balancing
       decision that difference matters; for "what is this channel doing" it does not.
    b) THE MAIN'S OWN CHANNEL IS REPLACED. Subtractive solo leaves the main's channel as the true mix bus (the un-Linked
       channels still sum through it). Additive solo silences every un-Linked channel that reaches the main, which
       subtractive solo would have left audible.
    c) OTHER V2 INSTANCES AND OTHER PATHS. Subtractive solo acts at the sources, so any path from a muted Link is silent
       everywhere; additive solo acts at one main, so a second monitor path or a second V2 on a parallel bus hears the
       unmuted world (item 4).
    d) MULTI-SOLO ACROSS LEVELS (item 3): subtractive would at least not double-count; it fails differently (mutes the path).
    e) LATENCY OF X: ~21 ms of ring cushion plus the crossfade, against zero for a subtractive solo of the live channel.
Recorded, not decided: the ruling is the reviewer's.

## BUILD F - ADDITIVE SOLO, as ruled (9 Sep, 14:3x). Corrections to the framing, recorded
- Un-Linked channels going silent under a solo is a GAIN, not a loss: that is what solo means, and subtractive solo had no
  lever on them. Moved to the reasons for the design.
- The mix-position difference is PRE-FADER LISTEN, a standard console mode, not a compromise. It is built and labelled as
  such: the panel reads "Solo (pre-fader listen): hearing <X> at its Link - fader and pan moves do not change what you
  hear." while a solo is active, and the S lamp tooltip says the same.
### The design as built
  Additive: on solo of X the main crossfades its output to X's ring. No Link is muted. No lease (the ring is written
  whenever the Link is on; the main already drains every ring). No alignment: no pad, no budget, no PDC event; X's ring is
  seeked to the existing 1024-frame cushion once at engage, one block per block, re-seek on the existing backlog threshold.
  The crossfade stays at its site BEFORE the main's own chain - INTENTIONAL, not an artefact of placement: the soloed Link
  is heard through the mix-bus processing.
  SINGLE SOLO, LAST PRESS WINS: pressing S on another Link moves the solo; pressing the soloed Link clears it. This is a
  DELIBERATE AMENDMENT of spec 6.1's multi-solo allowance: a set spanning a feed relation would double-count and the main
  cannot detect one, so we do not offer what we cannot do correctly. Sibling multi-solo is deferred until topology exists.
  THE GUARANTEE, exactly: the main's output is X. Complete only when the main is the last thing before the monitors.
  Direct outs, hardware inserts and parallel monitor paths are outside it and are not detectable from inside AAX.
  Spec 6.1 unchanged: when X is the borrowed rack the crossfade uses the PROCESSED ring (borrowBuf_).
### Deleted: the mute broadcast and its target enumeration, the reconcile pass, the authoritative mute record, the
hand-mute snapshot and restore, the dead-main contract and its leg, the liveness dependency for targets, the poll floor
and the pending lamp acks, and solo-dominates-borrow. Most of two days' work. The measurements that produced it are what
showed the model was wrong; the deletion is the payoff, not the waste.
### The leg: one rendered-audio leg with topology replaces the two-process timing legs - A (220 Hz) and B (330 Hz) feed a
Bus Link (their summed outputs), the Bus feeds the main whose own input is an un-Linked 1 kHz channel; Goertzel on the
main's output. Asserted: solo A -> 220 only; solo Bus (moved from A) -> 220 and 330, no 1 kHz; solo off -> 1 kHz back; no
Link muted at any point; spec 6.1: A borrowed in-context, solo A -> the source is the borrowed rack and 220 is heard.
Run at the minimal topology (3) and at Sean's scale (40 Links). Negative control: the same leg on Build E's archive.
### Build F leg table (rendered audio; Goertzel over 4,096 samples on the main's output; Links in series; isolated)
    TOPOLOGY 3 (A 220 Hz, B 330 Hz -> Bus, main input = un-Linked 1 kHz)       BUILD E (control)                 BUILD F
    no solo                                                                    1k 0.250                          1k 0.250
    solo A                                                                     1k 0.250, 2 Links muted  FAIL     220 0.499, 330 0.016, 1k 0.003, 0 muted  PASS
    solo Bus (moved from A)                                                    1k 0.250, 1 muted        FAIL     220 0.507, 330 0.505, 1k 0.002, 0 muted  PASS
    solo off                                                                   1k 0.250, 2 muted        FAIL     1k 0.250, 0 muted                        PASS
    solo A while A is the borrowed rack (spec 6.1)                             source=0                 FAIL     source = borrowed rack, 220 0.500       PASS
    TOPOLOGY 40 (+37 tracks at 440 Hz): identical verdicts; Build E mutes 39 / 38 / 40; Build F 220 0.498 / 0.292 (6.1), 0 muted.
The one coupling found on the way: the solo crossfade read the borrowed rack's ring AFTER the injection block had run its

### The reviewer's hypothesis (the solo crossfade's pre-alignment copy of the borrowed ring races the rack switch): REFUTED
    by three independent facts. (1) No solo was active, so applyBorrowSoloMixOn and the borrowProcessed_ copy were not
    running. (2) No V2 frame exists in the crashing thread or in any busy thread; the crash is inside MC 77's own code
    called from the Link's graph. (3) The crossfade only reads V2's own copies (borrowProcessed_, soloBuf_); it never
    writes anything the Link's process() reads - the ring is the Link's output, read-only to V2. It cannot null a
    pointer inside a hosted AudioUnit. The Link's graph teardown/rebuild is on the message thread (ChainHost
    setSlotBypassed/removeSlot/moveSlot -> rebuildGraph() + graph_->prepareToPlay(), called from LinkProcessor
    timerCallback -> pollEditLease -> rackLeaseEngage/Release); ChainHost::process() takes no lock against it and JUCE's
    graph documents an "implied mutex" the CALLER must provide between prepareToPlay/releaseResources and processBlock
    (juce_AudioProcessorGraph.cpp, NodeStates::applySettings comment). That is a real ordering gap in the Link, present
    since long before Build F, but it is NOT what the report shows: at the crash instant no thread was inside any
    ChainHost mutation (the message thread was blocked in AAE, see below).

    at ~15:30:03 and stayed stopped to the crash - the report agrees: thread 0 is parked in semaphore_wait_trap inside
    AAE, under a Pro Tools UI event. Sean's "rack switch" is the 15:30:02.9 sequence (the plan applied, the borrow
    released, the rack lock moved); what he saw next was a Pro Tools freeze, then the crash 72 s later.
### The mechanism the evidence supports
    completeLoad() adds a slot with bypassed = false, then rebuildGraph() + graph_->prepareToPlay() (ChainHost.cpp
    completeLoad); the lease's dry state is re-asserted only AFTER the whole plan is attached
    (LinkProcessor::applyStructurePlanAndSync: applyStructurePlan first, then setSlotBypassed(i, true) for every slot,
    then "dry rack re-asserted"). rebuildGraph() puts only non-bypassed slots into the render sequence
    (ChainHost.cpp ~5280: if (!s.bypassed && s.node)). So each plan-attached plugin is RENDERED LIVE on Pro Tools'
    realtime thread from its attach until the re-assert: for MC 77 that window is 15:30:02.858 -> .895, 37 ms, about
    three 512-sample blocks at 48 kHz - its first-ever render in this process (no "MC 77" line exists in the earlier
    Pro Tools instance's log; the Link had never hosted it today). The realtime thread entered MC 77's render inside
    that window and did not come back: after 15:30:02.895 the re-asserted sequence excludes MC 77, and JUCE swaps
    sequences only when the audio thread next enters processBlock - it never did, which is the only way HL Thread 4
    can still be inside MC 77 via the Link's graph at 15:31:15. Pro Tools' engine stalled on that thread; Sean's next
    action in Pro Tools put the main thread into AAE's semaphore wait (the freeze; EchoJay's timers stopped, the Link
    never restored its bypasses); 72 s later the hung render dereferenced null and the process died.
    What is ours in this: a leased ("streaming dry") rack renders each freshly instantiated third-party plugin for a
    few blocks before the lease bypasses it - an ordering defect in the Link's plan-attach path (attach live, bypass
    after), Link v8, unchanged since 8 Sep. What is not ours: a plugin that never returns from its render callback.
    Whether MC 77 would also hang on a normal (non-leased) first render is not known from this evidence; the
    reproduction needs MC 77 itself, which no harness of ours can host.
### Could Build E and earlier crash on the same action? YES. The crashing code is the Link (v8, 1000546C, installed
    8 Sep) rendering a hosted AudioUnit; the sequence that led there (build in the borrowed host -> send the plan ->
    the Link attaches live under the lease -> re-assert) contains no V2 code that changed in Build F. Build E, D and C
    ran against the same Link binary. This crash is not new with Build F and is not evidence against it.
### Not established: which Link-side action, if any, precedes MC 77's hang inside its own code; the plugin's internal
    state is opaque. Costed fix (NOT built, per the ruling): attach plan slots bypassed when a rack lease is active
    (bypassed = the lease state at attach, not false) so a leased rack never renders a new plugin, plus a process-side
    gate so ChainHost::process() cannot enter a sequence while a message-thread rebuild/prepare is in flight
    (the JUCE implied-mutex gap). Link v9: 3-4 h build + install; a leg that attaches under a lease and asserts no
    render of the new slot until release (the Link harness can host our own test plugin, not MC 77): 1.5 h; twenty
    runs. Total about 5-6 h. A leg for the MC 77 hang itself cannot be written here.
    Evidence files: tools/merge_gate_tests/results_2026-09-06/crash_2026-09-09_153115_ProTools_pid85903.ips and
    crash_2026-09-09_153115_log_15-29-55_to_15-31-16_EJ.txt (every EJ line of the last 80 s).

## 9 Sep - AUTO-DIAL: three vocal-chain plugins did not dial with "auto dial plugins only" on. Evidence only, no fix
    The build: 15:26:54.139 EJChain: extracted block -- 1980 ch, 5 slot(s), 1 with settings_structured
               15:27:00.305 EJChain: build target uid=549ad7d3b0 path=sendChainToLink dialCapable=y
               15:27:00.306 EJChain: build target ... path=SESSION (borrowed host) removes=0 adds=5
               slots loaded 15:27:00.6 EchoJay EQ, :03.6 Eiosis E2Deesser, :05.7 Purple Audio MC 77, :07.8 Millennia
               TCL-2, :09.9 UAD SPL TwinTube - each with "EJDial: slot n ... no settings yet [slot-loaded] -- EXPECTED
               ORDERING, not a fault ... see the EJDialSummary line for what actually dialled."
    Server omission vs writes rejected, per plugin: the response carried settings_structured for ONE of the five slots
    (the extraction line is the only response-shaped evidence the log holds; the block is not persisted and the
    request URL and body are not logged, so the request and response cannot be quoted, for the failing or the
    succeeding plugin). Which slot had it is not in the log. For the other four the server omitted it - the known
    filter-passed omission (Kathy's side, KATHY_REPLY_2026-09-06.md). No write was requested for any of the five, so
    "writes rejected" applies to none of them.
    A second gap, ours, found on the way: on this path (SESSION / borrowed host, then the structure plan) V2 never
    calls setSlotStructuredSettings - the "EJDial: attaching settings_structured" line (PluginEditor.cpp ~30739) is in
    loadChainFromJson, not in sendChainToLink; the dial watchdog ("WATCHDOG 6s after build start") is armed in
    loadChainFromJson only; and the structure plan carries ops, not settings. Consequently the session has NO
    EJDialSummary line at all, no "attaching" line and no "sending to Link -- n with settings_structured" line (that
    log sits after the early return). So even the one slot the server did deliver could not have dialled on this path,
    and the instrument that should have said so never fired. That is the "silent skip" recorded on 10 Aug, alive on a
    path the watchdog does not cover. Not fixed in this pass, per the ruling.
    Hours for the dialability work (unchanged from the 7 Sep costing, plus this): borrowed-path attach + watchdog on
    both paths 2 h; per-slot logging of settings_structured presence by name and the request/response pair 1 h;
    Solid Bus Comp writesRejected (own record) 2-3 h; the server omission is Kathy's. Total ours about 5-6 h.

## RULING (9 Sep, 16:0x): crash diagnosis accepted; the Link fix HELD until Sean's discriminating test; both fixes specified
### Item 2, the hold. Sean is running the same vocal chain WITHOUT MC 77, then a rack switch. If it does not crash, MC 77 is
    the trigger and Link v9 is tomorrow's; if it crashes regardless, the trigger is broader and Link v9 goes first.
    Nothing is built or installed until that result is in. Nothing is compiled either: Sean's test runs on THIS Mac and a
    four-target build alongside Pro Tools would confound it (CPU) - the reason the dial observability below is also
    specified and not compiled.
### Item 3, LINK v9 - specified, not built
    Change A, attach in the lease's target state (the crash's defect). Today: completeLoad() sets slot.bypassed = false,
    push_back, rebuildGraph(), graph_->prepareToPlay(); applyStructurePlanAndSync() re-asserts bypass on every slot only
    after the whole plan is attached; rebuildGraph() renders exactly the non-bypassed slots. So each attached plugin is
    rendered live until the re-assert (MC 77: 37 ms, about three blocks). v9: ChainHost gains attachBypassed_ (atomic
    bool, the lease's target state for any slot that arrives); LinkProcessor::rackLeaseEngage() sets it TRUE before it
    bypasses the existing slots, rackLeaseRelease() clears it AFTER it restores them; completeLoad() writes
    slot.bypassed = attachBypassed_.load() - one write, the target state, never false-then-corrected. The re-assert loop
    in applyStructurePlanAndSync() stays as the INSTRUMENT: any slot it finds un-bypassed under an active rack lease is
    logged "EJLease: INVARIANT BROKEN - slot n attached live under the lease" before it is bypassed; on a correct binary
    that line never prints. Slot-scoped leases (leaseSlot0_) are unchanged: they bypass one existing slot, and a plan does
    not arrive under one.
    Change B, the rebuild race (found on the way, not observed to crash). JUCE's graph documents an implied mutex the
    CALLER provides between prepareToPlay/releaseResources and processBlock (juce_AudioProcessorGraph.cpp,
    NodeStates::applySettings). Nothing provides it: ChainHost::process() takes no lock; setSlotBypassed, removeSlot,
    moveSlot, completeLoad, applyStructurePlan, the latency rebuilder and prepare/release all call rebuildGraph() +
    graph_->prepareToPlay() on the message thread. v9: a juce::SpinLock graphLock_; every mutation site holds it
    (ScopedLockType; the message thread waits at most one audio block, 10.7 ms at 512/48 kHz); process() takes it with
    ScopedTryLockType and on failure passes the dry buffer through (tallies kept) and returns - the audio thread never
    blocks, so no priority inversion, and a plugin whose prepareToPlay waits on the audio thread (the JUCE note on
    AudioUnitInitialize and the HAL mutex) cannot deadlock against us. Cost of the gate: dry audio for the duration of a
    mutation (an AudioUnitInitialize can take hundreds of ms). Under a lease the rack is dry anyway; outside one, a slot
    add already changes the sound. A counter rebuildInFlight_ (set for the mutation's duration, read in process()) is
    the instrument: process-entered-during-rebuild count, expected 0 on v9.
    ONE CHANGE OR TWO: two commits, one Link build. A is the defect that matches the report and is complete on its own;
    B closes a documented contract gap that has not been seen to crash and changes audible behaviour during mutations.
    They share the build and the install and each has its own leg, so a failure in B's leg cannot hold A. If Sean's test
    says the trigger is broader, both ship together as v9; if it says MC 77, A ships as v9 and B follows when it has run
    twenty times clean.
    THE LEGS (assert what is ours - the attach path - never MC 77's behaviour):
    Leg A (link harness, isolated root): engage a rack lease by file (the existing lease legs do this); then
    completeLoad() a ProbeProcessor (state_match_test has one: an AudioPluginInstance whose processBlock counts calls)
    through the public completeLoad; run one paced processBlock; assert the slot is bypassed and the probe's count is 0;
    delete the lease, pump the poll, process; assert count > 0 and the slot's bypass is the prior. Negative control on
    Link v8's archive: count 1 after the first block - FAIL, which is the crash's window measured in our own plugin.
    Leg B (twenty runs): a mutation churn thread (setSlotBypassed / add / remove / move at ~10 ms) against a paced
    processBlock thread for 10 s; assert rebuildInFlight_ was never observed from process() (count 0) and no crash;
    on v8 the count is the race's frequency in the harness and is reported, not tuned.
### Item 4, DIAL OBSERVABILITY - ours, first, specified, not compiled
    What the code says today, read for this ruling. The SESSION (borrowed host) build path DOES hand structured settings
    to the borrowed host: chainArrayToReplaceOps copies settings_structured into each add op (ChainHost.cpp:1497), and
    applyChainEdits calls setSlotStructuredSettings on the loaded slot (ChainHost.cpp:2130). What it cannot do is DIAL
    them: a Borrowed-mode ChainHost skips loadParamMapsFromDisk by construction (ChainHost.cpp:766, spec 2.1 read-only),
    and only the MAIN host is wired to fetch maps - onNeedParamMaps / onNeedFallbackMaps are set on
    processorRef.getChainHost() (PluginEditor.cpp:813, 838) and never on borrowHost_ (PluginProcessor.cpp:2125). So in a
    borrowed host every dialable slot has no map, requests none (the guards at ChainHost.cpp ~2798 require the
    callbacks), and sits at "pending" for the life of the session; the structure plan then carries ops, not settings, to
    the Link. And no instrument says so: the "attaching settings_structured" line and the 6 s watchdog live in
    loadChainFromJson only; the borrowed path emits no EJDialSummary at all. FILED, not fixed: "a plugin built in the
    borrowed host cannot dial" - a behavioural defect behind the observability one, and the reason the session log has
    no dial outcome for any of the five.
    THE CHANGE (observability only, per the ruling):
    (1) At extraction (PluginEditor.cpp ~26851) one line per slot: EJDial: block slot n "name" settings_structured=
        present keys=[...] | ABSENT - what the server delivered, by name, including the case where every slot is ABSENT.
    (2) On the SESSION path, in applyChainEdits' completion: bh2->logDialSummary("SESSION build complete"), and the same
        6 s watchdog armed on the borrowed host - the summary already prints per-slot status and "n/m slot(s) dialled
        something; k with no settings"; add to it the borrowed-host truth: "borrowed host: no param maps, nothing here can
        dial" when mode_ == Borrowed, so the line states requested / applied / omitted and says "nothing" when that is
        the answer.
    (3) dev_mode already dumps the REQUEST (chat-body-debug.json); dump the CHAIN_EDIT block beside it
        (chat-chain-block-debug.json) at extraction, so the request/response pair can be handed over without a log.
    Leg: the per-slot presence counter is a pure function over the block array; a test feeds a five-slot block with one
    settings_structured and asserts the five lines and the count line. The watchdog on the borrowed path is asserted by
    the harness that owns a borrowed host (the borrow legs): after applyChainEdits completes, the summary line exists
    within 6.5 s of the build start (wall clock).
### Kathy's item, drafted: KATHY_DIAL_REQUEST_2026-09-09.md (the request, the response, the plugins affected).
### Item 5, HOURS, separately
    Link v9 change A + leg A + build/install/package (one Link build):            3.5 h
    Link v9 change B + leg B (twenty runs), in the same build:                    +2.5 h   (both: 6 h)
    Dial observability (1)(2)(3) + the two legs + a V2 build (four targets):      3 h     (build only when Sean's test is
                                                                                            off this Mac)
    Filed behind it, not costed here: dialling in a borrowed host (maps + fetch wiring for Borrowed mode, or dial at the
    Link after the plan, per spec 2.1); Solid Bus Comp writesRejected 2-3 h (own record).

## RULING (9 Sep, 16:2x): THE HEADLINE DEFECT - a plugin built in the borrowed host cannot dial (ours, behavioural)
Order of the three, as ruled: (1) THIS, ours, behavioural; (2) the server omission, Kathy's; (3) the observability gap,
ours. Link v9 approved as specified and still on hold for Sean's test.
### The defect, stated from the code
    ChainHost in Mode::Borrowed skips loadParamMapsFromDisk, mergeBootstrapMaps and loadHelperCatalogue (ChainHost.cpp
    ~766, the spec 2.1 read-only rule applied to READS as well as writes), and the map-fetch callbacks onNeedParamMaps /
    onNeedFallbackMaps are wired by the editor onto processorRef.getChainHost() only (PluginEditor.cpp:813, 838) - never
    onto borrowHost_ (created at PluginProcessor.cpp:2125). A third-party slot given settings_structured in a borrowed
    host therefore finds no map, requests none (both guards at ChainHost.cpp ~2798 need the callback), and stays at
    noMap/pending for the life of the session. On the path Sean used today (EJChain: path=SESSION (borrowed host))
    nothing third-party could have dialled whatever the server sent.
### Item 2, the two accounts reconciled - Sean's count is the one that is wrong, and the finding above is REVISED
    The log: 15:26:54.139 extracted block -- 5 slot(s), 1 with settings_structured
             15:27:00.621 EJDial: slot 0 ("EchoJay EQ") no settings yet [slot-loaded]
             15:27:00.622 EJParamApply: slot 0 ("EchoJay EQ") EXACT built-in apply, 2 band(s), 0 skipped
    So the ONE slot the server delivered with settings_structured was EchoJay EQ, and it DIALLED - one millisecond after
    load, through the built-in apply, which takes the payload directly and needs no map. No other slot has any dial
    line, and applyStructuredIfReady logs every outcome it reaches (applied / partial / noMap / pending / mismatch), so
    no other slot reached it: nothing else was attached with settings. One dialled, four did not.
    Sean's "three did not dial" implies two did; the log supports one. The likeliest second is a card showing its prose
    settings (every card shows them; the 10 Aug note: "the settings appear in the card" does not mean the dialable ones
    arrived) - to be confirmed with Sean by naming the plugin, not argued.
    THE FINDING, REVISED before anything is built on it: "nothing can dial in a borrowed host" was too strong. The
    built-in EchoJay EQ dials in a borrowed host (its payload path needs no map). EVERY THIRD-PARTY slot cannot. For
    today's four (E2Deesser, MC 77, TCL-2, TwinTube) the OBSERVED cause is the server omission - they were never given
    settings - and the borrowed-host gap is the defect that would have stopped them had the settings arrived. Two
    defects, one symptom; fixing either alone leaves the symptom.
### Item 1, THE BEHAVIOURAL FIX, scoped (V2 only, ships with the observability in ONE trip)
    (a) Borrowed mode loads what is read-only anyway: loadParamMapsFromDisk (a parse of the primary's file), the
        bootstrap merge (its one save is already a no-op in Borrowed mode: saveParamMapsToDisk returns at
        ChainHost.cpp:4220) and the helper catalogue (pure read). One writer per file is preserved.        0.5 h
    (b) The fetch wiring: the processor exposes onBorrowHostCreated (fired at PluginProcessor.cpp:2125); the editor
        wires onNeedParamMaps / onNeedFallbackMaps onto the borrowed host exactly as onto the main host (813, 838),
        storing the fetched maps into the borrowed host (memory) AND the main host (the one writer, which persists
        them). Nulled on editor destruction as at 2791. SafePointer-guarded as today.                    1.5 h
    (c) Settle and close-out on the SESSION path: reportPendingDialOutcomes covers the borrowed host's slots and the
        chain bubble's dial-settled wait (finishChainBubbleWhenDialSettled, today only in loadChainFromJson) runs
        for the SESSION build too.                                                                        1 h
    (d) Legs on the existing borrowed-host fixture (tools/borrowhost_test): a borrowed host with a probe plugin and its
        map in the isolated root - structured settings -> DialStatus::applied, EJParamApply line present; the same
        with no map on disk -> onNeedParamMaps fires with the fp, storeParamMaps -> applied; negative control on the
        current binary: noMap and no request, both legs FAIL. A leg for the built-in exception: EchoJay EQ in a
        borrowed host dials with no map on either binary (documents the revision above).               2 h
    (e) Build (four targets, -j 4, detached with a log), gate, install, package as one V2 candidate.      1 h (shared)
    Behavioural fix: 6 h. Observability (per-slot presence line, SESSION summary + watchdog, block dump, its leg):
    2.5 h code and legs, build shared.
    COMBINED V2 TRIP: about 8 h wall clock from start to a signed-ready candidate on the Desktop. Ships as one build.
### Item 4, the order the moment Sean's result lands (recorded so it is not re-decided)
    no crash without MC 77 -> change A ships as Link v9; B follows after twenty clean runs; the V2 dial trip (8 h) goes
                              first tonight - Sean can film around a single plugin.
    crash regardless        -> A and B ship together as Link v9 (6 h) and the V2 dial trip waits.
### Item 3, Kathy's draft amended: it now says at the top that the client-side path could not have dialled the four
    either, and that the one delivered slot was EchoJay EQ, so she is asked for the omission reason, not for a symptom.

## RULING (9 Sep, 16:4x): MC 77 gate DROPPED. Link v9 ships A and B together. The "MC 77 is the trigger" framing is withdrawn
    The crash report establishes where the thread hung, not why it was unsafe to be there. Two conditions of ours were
    true at that instant and either is sufficient to make ANY hosted plugin unsafe: a plan slot attached un-bypassed and
    rendered live before the lease corrects it, and a message-thread graph teardown/rebuild with nothing providing the
    implied mutex JUCE documents against processBlock. MC 77 was the plugin holding the gun, not the reason it was
    loaded. Sean's judgement that it is not plugin-specific is supported; the rack-switch sequence below strengthens it.
    NO ONE IS SAFE BY AVOIDING MC 77. Sean's no-MC-77 run was contaminated by a rack switch mid-build and proves nothing.
    Work stands as three items in two trips: LINK TRIP - v9, A and B together, ~6 h plus the signing trip. V2 TRIP - the
    build-target / rack-lock / vanished-chain defect below, and the dial path (behavioural fix + observability), one build.

## 9 Sep, 16:19-16:27 - SEAN'S RACK-SWITCH SEQUENCE ON BUILD F. Diagnosed from the log (pid 96243) and the code. No fix built
Evidence: tools/merge_gate_tests/results_2026-09-06/rackswitch_2026-09-09_1619-1628_EJ_log.txt. Links by name: 549ad7d3b0
= "Aitch Lead Vocal" (Sean's Link A), 7f3595d7d6 = "Nafe Lead Vocal" (Link B), a690308f4f = "Nafe Adlibs" (the Link the
chain "landed on by mistake"), 249ee47a68 = "Nafe Chorus backings". Identities are stable: 40 Links, 40 distinct uids at
post-init and at ghost adoption, none re-minted during the session; each Link's uid is its chunk uid.
### The sequence against the log
    16:19:41.871 lock A; .897 EJBorrow: engaged A (session); 16:20:42 build in the session (6 slots, no MC 77)
    16:21:02.761 EJRackSel: row=37 resolved=7f35 (B) current=549a -> switching   <- Sean switches to B
    16:21:02.762 EJStruct: send uid=549ad7d3b0 ops=6   (the plan goes to A, the SESSION's uid) ... 16:21:06.431 applied=Y
    16:21:06.462 lock A released, lock B acquired; .479 lock B RELEASED (17 ms); 16:21:08.522 lock B acquired again
    16:21:08.523 EJBorrow: engaged B (0 slots)
    16:21:16.118 EJBorrow: released B; lock B released        (V2's editor closed: borrowEditorClosed)
    16:21:16.129 [Link] EJLinkState: editor open                (Sean opened Link B's own window)
    16:21:37.703 lock B acquired again (V2 reopened; the Chain tab shows B) - NO session engaged from here on
    16:22:46.270 EJChain: build target uid=7f35 path=sendChainToLink; .271 "sending to Link -- 7 slot(s)" (no session
                 active -> the build goes to the Link itself, un-leased); Link loads 7 AUs 16:22:48 -> 16:23:28 = the
                 "UI glitch" (40 s of AudioUnit instantiation on Pro Tools' message thread inside the Link)
    16:23:39.672 switch to A (lock A);  16:24:03.875 switch to B (lock B);  16:24:08.105 switch to "Nafe Adlibs" (lock)
    16:24:08.726 EJBorrow: engaged a690 (0 slots); 16:24:09.509 -> 16:24:10.877 SEVEN plugins load into that session:
                 Auto-Tune Pro, EchoJay EQ, SSL DeEss, Purple Audio MC 77, AVOX WARM, UAD Maag EQ4, ValhallaVintageVerb
                 = Link B's chain, in B's order, with B's pulled states ("EJApply ... hadState=Y seeded=Y")
    16:24:19.535 EJStruct: send uid=a690 ops=1 -> [Link] applied=N failedAt=base identity -> "the rack is not the one
                 this plan was made for - nothing was changed"  (x3: :19, :28, :34)
    16:24:34.254 EJStruct: close-apply FAILED uid=a690 - lock released, edits KEPT, note recorded
    16:24:43.945 lock A; 16:24:46.104 switch to B (lock B); 16:24:47.535 EJBorrow: engaged A (!); .545-.580 A's 6 slots
                 restored from the pool; .596 lock B released, lock A acquired (the lock follows the session)
    16:24:48.786 -> .860 a SECOND wave into A's session: Auto-Tune Pro as slot 6, SSL DeEss 8, MC 77 9, AVOX 10, UAD Maag
                 11, Valhalla 12 (B's chain again); 16:24:48.968 EJBorrow: released A, 13 instance(s) parked - no plan,
                 no EJApply: the restore's completion found 13 slots where it wanted 7 and released the session
    16:27:00.989 engaged a690 again: 0 slots, nothing seeded; 16:27:02 released - "the chain is gone from there"
### Item 1 - where the build's target is resolved. Sean's build button is EARLY: PluginEditor.cpp ~2549
    `const juce::String uid = effectiveChannelUid();` at the click, then `sendChainToLink(uid, chainBuildJsons[i])`.
    The plan's target is the session's own uid: PluginProcessor.cpp borrowApplyAndRelease `const juce::String uid =
    borrowSession_.uid;` And the Link already REFUSES a plan whose base is not its rack (LinkShm StructureEdit::Plan
    baseIdentity, "the guard's snapshot"; the Link: applied=N failedAt=base identity; V2: ack FAILED ... nothing was
    changed). So the reviewer's rule (item 4) already holds at the plan layer, and it held here, three times, loudly.
    THE LATE RESOLUTION IS ONE LAYER UP, in the session SEED. startBorrow(uid) (PluginEditor.cpp 8103) captures its uid
    at request time: `st->uid = uid; st->slots = it->second.rack.slots;` then pulls every slot's state from that Link
    (ctrl-cmd-<st->uid>, ~80 ms each) and, in `finish`, engages and seeds:
        proc.borrowEngageBegin(st->uid, leaseId, ...);      // PluginProcessor 2195: `if (borrowActive() || uid.isEmpty()) return;`
        auto* bh = proc.borrowHost();                       // <- WHATEVER session is active NOW
        ... bh->restoreSavedChain(slotsArr, statesObj, ...) // <- st->uid's slots and states go into it
    Nothing between the engage and the restore checks `proc.borrowUid() == st->uid`. And the loop that starts pulls has
    no in-flight guard: borrowSelectionTick (7956) calls startBorrow(want) on EVERY tick while no session is active and
    the lock is held (`if (p.borrowActive()) return; ... startBorrow(want);`) - the log shows 26 pull answers in 7 s for
    a 6-slot rack (16:23:39-46) and 22 in 6 s (16:24:43-49): several loops in flight at once for the same rack. So when
    Sean switched from B to Nafe Adlibs at 16:24:08.1, B's pull loop (started 16:24:03.9) was still running; Nafe
    Adlibs' loop had 0 slots to pull and engaged first (.726); B's loop finished at ~16:24:09.5, its engage was a
    silent no-op (a session was active), and its restore put B's seven slots and states into Nafe Adlibs' session.
    Same again at 16:24:47/48: A's loop engaged A, B's stale loop (started at the 16:24:46 switch) seeded B's seven into
    A's session 1.2 s later; the restore's completion counted 13 != 7 and released the session with no plan.
    CONFIRMED, at the seed layer: the pull captures the target at request time, the restore resolves it at apply time.
### Item 2 - the rack lock. Every acquire/release of the sequence is in the log excerpt above; the rule that drives them:
    PluginEditor.cpp 20926, in the editor's 30 Hz timer:
        processorRef.setRackLockWant(processorRef.borrowActive() ? processorRef.borrowUid()
            : currentTab == Tab::Chain && chainListPanel.isVisible() ? chainViewUid() : juce::String());
    The lock WANT is recomputed every tick from the session first, else from what the Chain tab is showing. Consequences
    seen: the 17 ms acquire/release at 16:21:06.462/.479 (the view emptied for one tick during the switch, then the
    session re-acquired at .522); the lock following the SESSION back to A at 16:24:47.596 while Sean had selected B;
    and Link B held by V2 from 16:21:37 to 16:22:46 and from 16:24:48.972 to 16:27:00 with NO session at all - locked
    because V2's Chain tab showed it. YES: a lock is held by an instance that is not editing, by construction of that
    line; "cannot be edited from the other plugin" is Link B's own editor seeing "locked by EchoJay on Mix Bus" while V2
    merely displayed B. The orange indicator did not appear on B because no session was ever engaged on B after
    16:21:16: the 16:21:08 session was released when Sean closed V2's window (borrowEditorClosed) and reopening at
    16:21:37 re-acquired the lock through the view rule but did not re-engage (pendingAutoEngage_ is set on a row click
    only). The build then went straight into the Link because `borrowHostIfActiveFor(7f35)` was null.
### Item 3 - the chain "vanishing" from Nafe Adlibs: DISPLAY, never removal. Nafe Adlibs' Link never held it: its lease
    answers say 0 slots at 16:24:08.820 and 16:27:01.011; its sidecar now: revision 2, slots []; the plan that would
    have put it there was refused three times "nothing was changed"; no Link-side load of any of those seven plugins
    exists except Link B's at 16:22:48-16:23:28. What Sean saw on Nafe Adlibs was V2's borrowed session holding B's
    seven instances (loaded in V2 16:24:09-10). It was gone at 16:27:00 because that session had been released at
    16:24:34 (the 13 instances parked in the pool, the 7 states "KEPT for uid=a690") and the fresh session seeded from
    the (correct) 0-slot cache. Not conflated: the mistaken-target defect is item 1; nothing was ever removed from any
    Link.
### Item 4 - the ruling, applied to what was found: the plan layer already captures at request time and refuses loudly.
    The rule must be applied one layer up: a pull loop's completion may seed ONLY the session whose uid it captured
    (`if (proc.borrowUid() != st->uid) { log "EJBorrow: stale pull for X discarded - session is Y"; return; }`), a
    second startBorrow for a uid with a loop in flight must not start another, and a loop whose uid is no longer the
    selection must end without engaging. A restore that finds the host holding more than it loaded is the silent
    variant of the same failure and must say which uid's slots it found. The lock rule (item 2) is the same shape:
    the want is re-derived from a transient view; it should be set on the transition (row click, session begin/end),
    not re-derived per tick. Not built - reported first, as ruled.
### MC 77 - moot as a gate (ruling above). Recorded for completeness: this run built MC 77 into Link B un-leased at
    16:22:48-16:23:28 and did not crash; that says nothing about the leased attach window or the rebuild race.

## CONSOLIDATION (9 Sep, 17:0x): the failed rack read "Cannot edit this rack - 3 plugin(s) could not come across ... (no answer
## from the Link)" - measured. It is NOT per-plugin latency and NOT a blocked window. It is V2 racing itself on one command file
Sean's screenshot: "Reading 6 plugin(s) from Aitch Lead Vocal" -> "Cannot edit this rack - 3 plugin(s) could not come
across: UAD Tube-Tech CL 1B (no answer from the Link); Acme Opticom XLA-3 (no answer from the Link); UAD SPL TwinTube
(no answer from the Link)". Chain: EchoJay EQ, Lindell 902 De-esser, UAD Tube-Tech CL 1B, Acme Opticom XLA-3, UAD SPL
TwinTube, Maag EQ4 = Link A (549ad7d3b0) after the 16:20 build. The read in the log: 16:23:39.709 lock A acquired, pulls
from 16:23:39.85, no session ever engaged, Sean switched away at 16:24:03.875. Evidence file:
tools/merge_gate_tests/results_2026-09-06/rackread_2026-09-09_162339_pull_answers.txt (every Link answer, seq, bytes).
### Item 1 - the timings. Link answers for that read, 16:23:39.851 -> 16:24:03.874 (24 s): 114 requests answered, ONE
    request never answered (seq 1788967440, overwritten before the Link read it). Inter-answer interval 200-330 ms for
    every plugin alike: TwinTube's 7,382-byte state answered 201 ms after XLA-3's (16:23:40.620 -> .821); CL1B's 1,763
    bytes in 220-330 ms every time; Maag's 235 bytes the same. The Link was never slow and never blocked: the answer
    stream has no gap larger than 340 ms in 24 s. So neither of the two candidate mechanisms holds. Per plugin, in the
    first pass: EQ req ~39.7 ans 39.851; Lindell ans 40.075; CL1B ans 40.389; XLA-3 ans 40.620; TwinTube ans 40.821;
    Maag - not in the first pass. And then the pass RESTARTS at slot 1: 40.930 EQ, 41.146 Lindell, 41.464 CL1B, 41.673
    XLA-3, 41.894 EQ again, ... a new pass every ~1.0 s (EQ at 39.85, 40.93, 41.89, 42.89, 43.88, 44.96, 46.05 ...).
    That cadence is the cache feeder's: PluginEditor.cpp 7319 `if (!force && nowMs - lastRackCacheMs_ < 1000) return;`
    then 7327 `borrowSelectionTick();` -> 7956 `if (p.borrowActive()) return; ... startBorrow(want);` A NEW READ LOOP
    STARTS EVERY SECOND for the same rack while none has finished. Every loop shares ONE command file and ONE ack file
    per Link (8359-8361): `juce::File(dir + "ctrl-ack-" + st->uid + ".json").deleteFile(); juce::File(dir + "ctrl-cmd-"
    + st->uid + ".json").replaceWithText(...)`. The newer loop deletes the ack the older loop is polling for and
    overwrites the command it is waiting on; the older loop's poll never sees its seq (8375: `(int) o->getProperty
    ("seq") == seq`), and after 20 polls x 250 ms = 5 s (8409 `(*poll)(20)`, 8368 `callAfterDelay(250, ...)`) it records
    "no answer from the Link" and moves to the next slot (8400-8405). The Link answered; V2 threw the answer away.
    Why positions 3, 4, 5: the first loop gets slots 1 and 2 answered before the second loop starts (1 s later); from
    then on every later slot of the first loop is racing one or more newer loops that restart at slot 1. Position-
    driven, exactly as the pattern suggested - but by V2's own loops, not by the Link's thread. The heavyweights are
    innocent: TwinTube answered in 201 ms.
### Item 2 - the timeout and the threads. Per PLUGIN (per slot request), 20 x 250 ms = 5 s, wall clock (chained
    callAfterDelay, not a count of ticks); it runs on V2's message thread. The Link answers in pollControlCommand
    (LinkProcessor.cpp 749) on every 3rd 30 Hz tick (~100 ms) - Pro Tools' message thread - and calls the hosted
    plugin's getStateInformation there (895-923). YES, THE SAME THREAD as the graph teardown/rebuild (rebuildGraph /
    prepareToPlay from setSlotBypassed, removeSlot, completeLoad, applyStructurePlan) - and the same thread V2's own
    timers run on, since every plugin in the process shares Pro Tools' main thread. A Link mid-rebuild (B loading seven
    AudioUnits 16:22:48-16:23:28) blocks that thread for everyone, V2's poll included; but THIS read (16:23:39-16:24:03)
    ran after that window and shows no block. The family relation to the crash is real (one thread asked to do two
    things) but it is not what failed here.
### Item 3 - retry. None that helps: a slot's request is written once; 20 polls of the SAME seq; on failure the loop
    moves on and `finish` refuses the whole rack if any slot failed ("Nothing was engaged"). The only "retry" is the
    accidental one - the 1 Hz tick starting another whole loop - and that retry is the cause of the failures. Wrong
    shape twice over, as the ruling says: single-shot per slot, whole-rack failure on one slot.
### Item 4 - are the dial failures the same request? NO. The dial path never fetches plugin state: it takes
    settings_structured from the server's block and writes parameters through a param map (server /api/params/maps,
    fetched by the main host only - the headline defect); the rack read pulls the plugin's STATE from the Link
    (pullSlotState). Different endpoints, different data, different code. The three names coincide because they are
    the three non-built-in slots Sean looked at; the read's failures are positional (above), the dial's are structural
    (a borrowed host cannot dial anything third-party). Re-examined for Kathy: the server counts stand and grew today -
    crashed session 1 of 5 slots with settings_structured; this session 4 of 6 (16:20:38) and 2 of 7 (16:22:42). She is
    still asked only why slots are omitted; nothing about the read is hers. Draft amended with the two new counts.
### The consolidation, corrected to what the evidence supports: TWO defects, not one and not three.
    Defect R (ours, V2): overlapping rack-read loops on one command channel -> "no answer" refusals -> no session ->
    no orange indicator -> nothing editable. Three of Sean's symptoms, one mechanism. It also produced the stale-loop
    seeding in the previous section (a loop finishing into another rack's session) - the same loops, the other
    failure. Earlier entries saying "the lock did not engage" are corrected: the LOCK engaged every time (16:23:39.709
    acquired A); the SESSION did not, because the read refused.
    Defect D (ours, V2): a borrowed host cannot dial third-party slots (no maps, no fetch wiring) - the dial symptom.
    Plus Kathy's omission, which reduces how many slots could dial even after D is fixed.
### Item 5 - where the fix belongs: neither Link v9's ordering work nor a timeout-and-retry policy. It is the V2 seed
    layer, one change with the stale-loop fix: one read loop per uid at a time (a token held from startBorrow to
    finish), a loop whose uid is no longer the selection ends without engaging, a completion that seeds only its own
    uid's session, no deletion of an ack a live loop is polling, and per-slot re-request before refusal (a second
    request with a fresh seq, then refuse with the seq and the elapsed time in the message). With one loop in flight
    the 5 s budget is generous: the Link answered every request in under 340 ms. Not built - reported first.
    Leg: the borrowhost fixture with a fake Link answering ctrl-cmd in 200 ms; start the read, tick the feeder at 1 Hz
    for 6 s; assert one seq stream, six answers accepted, session engaged, zero "no answer"; on the current binary the
    same run refuses (the control), and its failure list names later slots.
### Hours, restated for the V2 trip (three items now):
    Seed layer + read loop (Defect R, both failures) code 3 h, legs 2 h                          5 h
    Lock want on transitions (row click, session begin/end), leg                                2.5 h
    Dial path (behavioural + observability, as costed)                                          8 h
    Build, gate, install, package (one candidate)                                               1 h
    V2 TRIP TOTAL about 16 h wall clock - two working days, one build if it is to be one trip; it splits cleanly into
    R+lock (8.5 h) and dial (8 h) if two candidates are acceptable. LINK TRIP: v9 A+B 6 h confirmed, plus signing.

## BUILT (9 Sep, 17:xx-18:xx): LINK v9 (A+B) and V2 read-and-lock, ONE delivery for ONE signing trip, as ruled
Both installed here (AU/VST3) and packaged unsigned to the Desktop. Nothing signed on this Mac (it cannot). Dial deferred.
### Link v9 - change A (attach in the lease's target state) + change B (the graph lock)
    A: ChainHost gains attachBypassed_ (the lease's target for arriving slots) and an intended/effective bypass split
       (slot.intendedBypassed = the user/plan request; slot.bypassed = the effective state the lease overlays).
       completeLoad writes bypassed = attachBypassed_ ONCE (never false-then-corrected). rackLeaseEngage sets
       attachBypassed_ true BEFORE bypassing the existing slots; rackLeaseRelease clears it AFTER restoring them, and
       restores EVERY slot to its intended state (so a slot attached under the lease comes back LIVE). The re-assert
       loop in applyStructurePlanAndSync is now the INSTRUMENT: "EJLease: INVARIANT BROKEN - slot n attached LIVE"
       prints only on a broken binary. setLeaseBypass is the lease's effective-only write; setSlotBypassed is the
       user/plan write.
    B: ChainHost::graphLock_ (juce::CriticalSection). Every message-thread mutation holds it (GraphMutation RAII at
       removeSlot/moveSlot/setSlotBypassed/setLeaseBypass/completeLoad/releaseBorrowToPool/parkSlotReattachable/
       tryReattachParked/borrowTryReuseInto/loadBuiltinNow/rebuildForLatencyIfChanged/prepare/release). process()
       takes it with a ScopedTryLock and, when a mutation holds it, passes the buffer through DRY (tallies kept) and
       returns - the audio thread never blocks. rebuildInFlight_/processDuringRebuild_ are the instruments.
    Per-request channel: the Link also writes ctrl-ack-<uid>-<seq>.json beside the legacy ack and advertises
       ackPerSeq in its sidecar (see the V2 half). lastPerSeqAckFile_ removes the prior per-seq file so at most one
       stale ack lingers per Link.
    LEGS (Link archive, isolated root; tools/merge_gate_tests/link_v9_legs_test.cpp; __has_include(legs_link_v9.h)):
      attach - a probe plugin completeLoad()ed under a live rack lease renders 0 times across 3 blocks while leased,
               then 3/3 after release (bypassed=1->0). v9 PASS; v8 archive control: rendered 3/3 while leased - FAIL.
      churn  - a paced processBlock thread vs message-thread mutations (add/bypass/move/remove) for 10 s, x20 runs.
               v9: rendered-inside-rebuild 0 every run - 20/20 PASS. v8 control: thousands rendered mid-mutation each
               run - 0/20 PASS. (The leg reads the probe's render count inside the product's rebuildInFlight window.)
### V2 read-and-lock - Defect R (the two accounts of 9 Sep: the failed rack read and the stale seeding)
    ONE read loop per editor: borrowReadInFlightUid_ set by startBorrow, cleared on every terminal path;
    borrowSelectionTick will not start a second loop while it is set (the 1 Hz feeder can no longer restart it).
    The read ABANDONS loudly if the selection moved (checked at every step and at finish), and NEVER seeds a session
    it did not read for (finish refuses if borrowUid() != st->uid - the silent no-op that proceeded anyway, closed).
    Per-request identity: a v9 Link's sidecar carries ackPerSeq, so V2 polls ctrl-ack-<uid>-<seq>.json (no other
    reader deletes or overwrites it) and does not clear the shared ack; a pre-v9 Link keeps the legacy channel.
    One re-request with a fresh seq before "no answer", and the refusal carries the elapsed time and last seq.
    The LOCK belongs to the session: setRackLockWant is set on transitions (row-click that pends an engage; edit
    session begin/end; session end; one-shot Link-tab commands via withRackLock) and NEVER re-derived from the view.
    The old per-tick "want = borrowActive ? borrowUid : (Chain tab visible ? chainViewUid)" line is replaced by an
    INSTRUMENT that releases and logs "EJRackLock: INVARIANT BROKEN - held for X with no session" if it ever finds
    a lock held for a uid that is not the session, the pending engage or an edit session.
    LEGS (main archive + a hand-built fake Link answering ctrl-cmd in 200 ms; 40 Links = Sean's scale;
    tools/merge_gate_tests/v2_readlock_legs_test.cpp):
      seed  - select A (6 slots), 500 ms later select B (2 slots) mid-read. G: A's read ABANDONED at slot 3, B's
              session engages with B's own 2 slots - PASS (x3, deterministic). F control: session=A with 6 slots -
              FAIL (A's read seeded B's selection's session), the exact defect.
      loops - one read of A's 6 slots. G: engaged, exactly 6 pull requests - PASS. F control: 319 requests, read
              never completes, engaged=0 - FAIL (the 1 Hz feeder clobbering the shared channel, the 114-in-24 s bug).
      lock  - G: viewing A holds no lock file; click+session hold it; session end releases it - PASS. F control:
              viewing A (no click) holds the lock file - FAIL (lock derived from the view).
    Results: tools/merge_gate_tests/results_2026-09-09/v9_and_readlock_legs.txt (+ the two leg sources).
### What is NOT in this delivery (second V2 trip, ~9 h, deferred as ruled): the dial path - the borrowed-host
    behavioural fix (maps + fetch wiring for Borrowed mode) and its observability. It waits because a rack that
    cannot be edited cannot demonstrate dialling. The three dial defects and Kathy's server omission stand recorded.

## RULING FOLLOW-UP (10 Sep): the ADDITIVE-SOLO BLOCKER cleared, the dead-cache sweep, and what may self-resolve
### Item 1 - additive solo is GREEN on v9, including the lease-active case. No regression.
    The leg is two-process (the Link `topology` side stands A 220 Hz, B 330 Hz -> Bus, +N tracks; the main renders and
    Goertzels its output). Run as the shipped pair and the new pair. Both PASS every assertion:
        Build F (V2) + Link v8 (baseline)   topology 3:  A 0.496 / Bus 0.487+0.489 / off 1k 0.250 / 6.1 src=rack 0.496  PASS
        Build G (V2) + Link v9              topology 3:  A 0.504 / Bus 0.486+0.489 / off 1k 0.250 / 6.1 src=rack 0.498  PASS
        Build G (V2) + Link v9              topology 40: A 0.498 / Bus 0.507+0.511 / off 1k 0.250 / 6.1 src=rack 0.497  PASS
    Spec 6.1 verified on v9: with A borrowed in-context (a STOPPED block commits the budget), solo A takes the PROCESSED
    ring (soloSourceIsBorrowedRack()=1), 220 Hz is heard, the 1 kHz un-Linked input is gone, and exactly one Link is
    muted - the borrowed rack's own raw channel, session-muted, which spec 6.1 allows. NO Link is muted in the non-6.1
    cases. Both of v9's load-bearing changes were exercised here: the intended/effective bypass split (the borrowed
    rack's slots stay live under the lease while it is soloed) and the graph lock (the ring the solo reads is the
    rack's real output, not a half-mutated buffer). Neither moved a number outside tolerance.
    Results: tools/merge_gate_tests/results_2026-09-09/additive_solo_v9_regression.txt.
### Item 2 - compensating hacks around the dead cache: swept, reported, NOT fixed.
    The cache (linkRackCache) has never populated in its life (Defect R's corollary), so the code learned to live
    without it. What the sweep found, and did not change:
    (a) The READERS are already defensive, not fabricating. linkEqCurve returns nullptr "rack unknown, NOT rack flat";
        chainRackView returns valid=false "NO DATA, not empty"; linkRackCount returns 0 only on !valid. They guard on
        `it->second.valid` and treat a miss as UNKNOWN, never as an empty rack. So a populated cache makes these show
        REAL data where they showed a placeholder before - an improvement, not a hazard.
    (b) The real pattern: ~13 call sites read the sidecar DIRECTLY from disk (readLinkRackSidecar), bypassing the cache
        entirely - runAICompareWith, standardChainInjections, presentRecallReplaceAsk, sendChatMessage, handleChatReply,
        requestAIFeedback, openRecallChannelChooser, applyChainEditToLink (PluginEditor.cpp), and rackLockTick +
        refreshLinkRegistry's mute-solo snapshot (PluginProcessor.cpp). Most are the AI/chat path reading the freshest
        sidecar at prompt-build time, which is correct BY DESIGN and should stay a direct read. The hazard is DUAL
        SOURCE: the strip and rack view now read the live cache (a ~1 s snapshot) while the chat injection reads disk
        NOW - the same rack can differ between the picture and the prompt within one action, by up to the cache age.
        This is the "incoherent snapshot" class, previously masked because the cache was always empty and everything
        fell through to the direct read. NOT fixed here; flagged for the reviewer to decide (one source of truth, or an
        explicit "as of" stamp on each).
    (c) No site was found that treated cache-empty as "the rack IS empty" and acted destructively (built an empty chain,
        cleared slots). The `!valid` guards prevented that.
### Item 3 - PREDICTION (to be confirmed in-host, not assumed): with the cache now populating for the first time,
    behaviours Sean has reported for weeks and we attributed elsewhere may simply stop. Worth re-testing in Pro Tools:
      - stale rack views (a Link's slot list shown out of date, or blank when it has plugins) - the feeder now fills it;
      - wrong strip contents / slot counts on the Link tab (linkRackCount read 0 from the empty cache) - now real;
      - the S/solo and pre-gain lamps on a Link row reading defaults - they key off the cache;
      - lock decisions and the orange indicator "deciding on nothing" - the read that feeds them now completes;
      - the rack-edit picture disagreeing with what the AI says it sees - the dual-source item (b); may PERSIST, since
        the chat path reads disk directly - this is the one to watch as NOT fixed.
    If these clear on Build G, they were the dead cache all along and their old "causes" can be closed. If any persists,
    it is its own defect. This paragraph is the falsifier, not a claim they are fixed.
### Item 4 - the delivery: both zips travel together. Desktop carries ONLY the Build G / v9 candidate:
      EchoJay_V2_AAX_unsigned_BUILD_G (x86_64 55EFA2E8), EchoJay_Link_AAX_unsigned_v9 (x86_64 1718F0F1),

## RULING (10 Sep, on the dual-source finding + delivery close-out). Recorded; nothing built.
### Item 1 - ONE SOURCE OF TRUTH FOR THE RACK, AND IT IS THE CACHE. Costed onto the dial trip, not built now.
    The hazard, stated plainly: the AI/chat path reads the sidecar directly from disk at prompt-build time while the
    strip and rack view read the 1 s cache, so the assistant can DESCRIBE a rack that differs from the one on screen.
    When the picture and the prompt disagree the user cannot tell which is wrong, and that costs more than a second of
    staleness. THE RULE: never two paths to the same fact. If a call site needs freshness, FORCE A CACHE REFRESH and
    then read the cache - the cache is the single source. No direct readLinkRackSidecar at a UI or prompt call site.
    Shape (for the dial trip): a refreshLinkRackCache(uid, force) that re-reads ONE sidecar into the cache synchronously
    and returns the entry; the ~13 direct-read sites (runAICompareWith, standardChainInjections, presentRecallReplaceAsk,
    sendChatMessage, handleChatReply, requestAIFeedback, openRecallChannelChooser, applyChainEditToLink) call it instead
    of readLinkRackSidecar; the feeder stays the bulk 1 Hz populate. rackLockTick and the mute-solo snapshot in
    refreshLinkRegistry are processor-side, not the editor cache, and are OUT of scope (they read their own file for
    their own decision). Leg: two readers (strip + a simulated prompt-build) of the same uid in one turn must return the
    SAME revision and slot list; the control (direct read on one path) can differ by the cache age. Cost: 2 h + 1 h leg.
### Item 2 - THE FALSIFIER LIST is a questionnaire for Sean on Build G, not a claim. When he is in-host, ask EXPLICITLY,
    item by item, and record which CLEARED and which PERSISTED. Anything that persists is its OWN defect and must NOT be
    re-absorbed into the cache finding.
      Q1  stale or blank rack view (a Link's slot list out of date, or empty when it has plugins) - cleared? ____
      Q2  wrong strip slot count on the Link tab (was reading 0 from the empty cache) - cleared? ____
      Q3  Link-row lamps (S/solo, pre-gain) sitting on defaults instead of the rack's real state - cleared? ____
      Q4  lock decision / orange indicator behaving as if deciding on nothing - cleared? ____
      Q5  the rack EDIT picture disagreeing with what the AI says it sees - THIS ONE MAY PERSIST (item 1 not built);
          if it persists that is EXPECTED until the single-source change ships, not a new defect.
    Filed as [[pitch-work-state-2026-09-05]] neighbour: the Build-G in-host confirmation list.
### Item 3 - WHEN THE SIGNED BUNDLES RETURN (nothing needs Sean's machine before then). Order, exact:
      1. Quit Pro Tools.
      2. Run Desktop/INSTALL_AAX_BUILD_G_v9_2026-09-10.command (unzip the two signed bundles next to it first).
      3. It VERIFIES both x86_64 UUIDs before and after: V2 55EFA2E8..., Link 1718F0F1... . Do not proceed on a FAIL line.
      4. Relaunch Pro Tools.
      5. In-host confirm, in order:
         (a) a rack READ completes with no "could not come across" (Defect R / read loop);
         (b) the ORANGE edit indicator appears on the selected Link (the lock now belongs to the session);
         (c) switching racks MID-BUILD lands the chain on the REQUESTED Link, or refuses LOUDLY - never silently on another;
         (d) SOLO still behaves as Build F did (the additive leg says it does; confirm by ear).
      Then run the Q1-Q5 questionnaire (item 2).
### Item 4 - DIAL IS THE NEXT TRIP, and DOES NOT START until Sean confirms Build G in-host. Re-costed:
      borrowed-host behavioural fix (maps + fetch wiring for Borrowed mode)      6 h  (2 code, 2 legs, +build/settle)
      dial observability (per-slot presence line, SESSION summary + watchdog, block dump)   2.5 h
      single-source-of-truth change (item 1, folded into this trip)             3 h  (2 code + 1 leg)
      build (four targets), gate, install, package one candidate                1 h  (shared)
      NEXT V2 TRIP TOTAL: about 12 h. Held until the Build-G in-host confirmation - if the cache finding clears as much
      as predicted, the dial symptoms may look different from what we diagnosed and the plan is re-read before building.

## DEFECT (10 Sep): the Build G / v9 installer was UNPASSABLE - the mirror of the 6 Sep unfalsifiable installer
Sean's signed bundles were exactly right (V2 x86_64 55EFA2E8, Link x86_64 1718F0F1, verified by hand) and the installer
printed "FAIL EchoJay V2 (Build G) x86_64 (wanted 55EFA2E8...)". The binaries were right; the CHECK was wrong.
### The cause, quoted and named (not loosened)
    verify () {
      local bin="$1/Contents/MacOS/$(basename "$1" .aaxplugin)"
      local got="$(dwarfdump --uuid "$bin" 2>/dev/null | awk '/x86_64/{print $2}')"
      if [[ "$got" == "$2"* ]]; then echo OK; else echo FAIL ... ; fi
    }
    The installer assumed the bundles sit next to itself ($HERE = ~/Desktop). Sean staged the SIGNED bundles in
    ~/Desktop/ejinstall_buildG/. So $HERE/EchoJay V2.aaxplugin did not exist; dwarfdump on a missing file errored;
    2>/dev/null swallowed the error; got=""; [[ "" == "55EFA2E8"* ]] is false -> the exact FAIL line, with the empty
    got showing as the double space. THE CONFLATION: "source bundle not found at the assumed path" was reported as
    "UUID mismatch". Reproduced precisely against a missing path (empty got -> FAIL) and against the real bundle at a
    real path (OK), so the parse and the comparison were never the fault - the path assumption and the swallowed error were.
### The fix (installer on the Desktop, INSTALL_AAX_BUILD_G_v9_2026-09-10.command): locate() searches an optional $1,
    then the script's folder, then ~/Desktop/ejinstall_buildG, then ~/Desktop, for each bundle by name. verify() now
    distinguishes NOT FOUND (exit 2) / BROKEN (no binary, 2) / NO SLICE (no x86_64 UUID, 2) / MISMATCH (wrong UUID, 1) /
    OK (0) - each its own line, never conflated; the wanted UUID is the FULL string, exact-compared (not a prefix). The
    check was not loosened; it was made honest about WHY it fails.
### Proven BOTH WAYS (the standing rule from item 2, and my error last time): a verifier must fail on a known-bad AND
    pass on a known-good; showing only that it can fail is half a test. Dry-run
    (tools/merge_gate_tests/results_2026-09-09/installer_verifier_both_ways.sh):
      [A] KNOWN-GOOD, Sean's ~/Desktop/ejinstall_buildG:  OK  55EFA2E8-...  exit 0
