## DIAL TRIP (10 Sep) - the borrowed-host dial fix, built. Confirmed mechanism, four changes, one leg.
### Mechanism, confirmed: only the builtin dials on the borrowed path. Sean built a vocal chain with "suggest dialable
    plugins only" on and ONLY EchoJay EQ dialled. EchoJay EQ is a builtin (no param map needed); every third-party slot
    needs a map. A Borrowed-mode ChainHost skipped loadParamMapsFromDisk AND the map-fetch callbacks (onNeedParamMaps/
    onNeedFallbackMaps) were wired by the editor onto the MAIN host only, never onto borrowHost_. So a third-party slot
    in a borrowed host found no map, requested none, and sat pending forever. Corroborated by the 9 Sep session log
    (EchoJay EQ "EXACT built-in apply" while E2Deesser/MC 77/TCL-2/TwinTube stayed "no settings"). Today's session log
    query was slow/empty on the live pid; the code + Sean's direct report + the 9 Sep log establish it.
### The changes (V2 + Link, ChainHost is shared):
    1. BORROWED MODE LOADS MAPS. rebuildGraph's Borrowed branch now calls loadParamMapsFromDisk + mergeBootstrapMaps +
       loadHelperCatalogue (pure reads; the one save in mergeBootstrapMaps is a no-op in Borrowed - saveParamMapsToDisk
       returns). A cached map now dials a third-party slot with no fetch. Death-mark consumption stays the primary's.
    2. THE BORROWED HOST FETCHES ITS OWN MAPS. One wiring path, wireChainHostFetch(host, isBorrow), used for the main
       host (constructor) AND the borrowed host (wired at startBorrow after engage; nulled on borrowRelease before the
       pool release). A fetched map stores into the LIVE borrowed host (dials the session) and the main host (the one
       disk writer, so it persists). A completion after the session ends is a guarded no-op.
    3. SUGGESTIONS SURVIVE A RACK SWITCH. BorrowKept now carries the AI prose AND the structured settings per slot,
       keyed by uid; captureBorrowKept stores them, and startBorrow's restore re-applies them (name-checked per index)
       over the sidecar's older text when the same rack is re-borrowed, re-dialing via applyStructuredIfReady. This was
       the second symptom - switching away and back lost the last suggestions because they lived in the session and
       died with it.
    4. OBSERVABILITY on the borrowed path. The SESSION build now emits EJDialSummary("SESSION build (borrowed host)
       complete") - requested/applied/omitted per slot, including "nothing" - and arms the 6 s watchdog
       ("WATCHDOG 6s after SESSION build start"). A path that could silently skip dialling and report nothing is what
       let this hide; it no longer can.
    5. SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH (the ruling's rider). readLinkRackSidecar now writes THROUGH the cache: the AI/chat path
       reads through it and the strip/rack view read the cache, so a fresh on-demand read also updates the picture -
       the two can never describe different racks. The 1 Hz feeder is unchanged.
### Kathy's item stays hers and is unchanged: the server omitting settings_structured for filter-passed plugins. Her
    draft (KATHY_DIAL_REQUEST_2026-09-09.md) already states the client could not have dialled those plugins either, so
    she is not chasing a symptom two defects produce.

    (the request's per-plugin fingerprint list), and getDialableRecommendableNames IS map-presence based:
      juce::StringArray ChainHost::getDialableRecommendableNames() const {
        for (const auto& e : recommendable_) { bool dialable = false;
          if (const auto fp = echojay::fpForIdentity(identityToFp_, e.desc); fp.isNotEmpty())
            if (auto m = paramMaps_.find(fp); m != end && echojay::mapIsDialableForSignals(m->second)) dialable = true;
          if (! dialable && e.desc.uniqueId != 0 && existenceDialable_.count(identityKeyForDescription(e.desc))>0) dialable = true;
          if (dialable) out.addIfNotAlreadyThere(e.displayName); } }
    BUT the loop's premise (no cached map) is FALSE here: the maps ARE cached and mapFps carried the CORRECT fps. So the
    loop did not gate these plugins. This is not "no map -> not declared -> prose -> repeat".

## PATH DIFF (10 Sep): borrowed/Link request vs local/own-chain request, both captured on THIS machine. Nothing built.
The loop is REFUTED (7th of the reviewer's): maps cached, mapFps fingerprints exactly matching, dial instruction present,
autoDial true - the client asked correctly and the server returned structured settings for the builtin only.
### The two request bodies (dev-mode dumps, ~/Documents/EchoJay/): the failing LINK/borrowed build
    (chat-body-20260910-120053, "TARGET CHANNEL — Nafe Lead Vocal") vs a LOCAL/own-chain build
    (chat-body-20260906-213913, "THIS CHANNEL — this plugin's own rack"). Field-by-field diff:
    IDENTICAL:
      - turnType = chain_generate; autoDial = true; appVersion = 2.26.4 (both).
      - the dial instruction, byte-identical: "Dial one by putting real-world values under settings_structured.params
        using the ids listed; any param you omit is left as it is."
      - the AVAILABLE PLUGINS presentation (same header, same list mechanism).
      - mapFps PRESENT in both (count differs only by cache state: 22 vs 16); the failing build's mapFps carries the
        correct, matching fingerprints for MC 77 / SSL DeEss / Acme / Valhalla (established earlier).
    DIFFERENT (the whole of it):
      - CHANNEL FRAMING. Link path: "TARGET CHANNEL — this conversation IS the user's \"Nafe Lead Vocal\" Link channel."
        Local path: "THIS CHANNEL — this conversation IS this EchoJay plugin's own channel - the track it is loaded on."
        Both then say "build/edit for THIS channel; do NOT ask which."
      - LIVE READINGS. Local path carries a slotParamReads field (live parameter readings of the current rack). The
        Link path has NO slotParamReads and instead a note: "no live readings exist: build the chain from the request
        as usual and never defer it, but add one short sentence asking them to press play...".
      - CURRENT CHAIN. Local path injects a CURRENT CHAIN block (the existing rack, slot by slot). The Link build had an
        empty target so none was present (confound: this reflects an empty target rack, not purely the path).
### WHAT THE DIFF DOES AND DOES NOT SHOW. It shows the request DOES differ by path. But the differing fields are the
    CONTEXTUAL framing (which channel, live reads, current rack) - NOT the dial machinery, which is byte-identical
    across both. So the diff NARROWS any path effect to the framing steering the model, but does NOT by itself prove
    the borrowed path causes prose: nothing in the dial instruction, mapFps, or plugin list changed.
### ITEM 3 IS NOT YET CONFIRMED - and honestly so. Every build Sean has made (all sessions on disk, and today) went
    through a Link (path=SESSION/sendChainToLink); there is NO local/own-chain build with a dial outcome on disk to

    build: Sean builds the SAME chain on the Mix Bus's OWN local chain on Build H (the build button with no Link
    selected -> path=loadChainFromJson, which never enters the borrowed host), and we read the dial summary.
      - If the local build DIALS the third-party slots and the Link build does not, with identical dial machinery, the
        borrowed path's framing is making the model answer in prose -> OURS to fix, no server, no Kathy.
      - If the local build ALSO returns prose, the path is NOT the variable and the machine is: then, and only then, we
        need the other Mac's appVersion and request dump. Stated plainly rather than assumed.
### ITEM 5 - EXCLUDED BY MEASUREMENT (do not re-test): the map-presence LOOP (maps cached, declared, matching fps);
    a MISSING or STALE client map (map=y in the summary; fps match the cache); a FINGERPRINT MISMATCH (mapFps fps equal
    the cache fps exactly); REJECTED WRITES (requested=0 applied=0, status=none, not writesRejected). The response-side
    omission is none of these. It is the model returning prose for correctly-declared dialable plugins; whether that is
    triggered by the borrowed path's framing (ours) or is machine/server is the one open fork, decided by the local build.
### CONDITIONAL HOURS (only if the local build lands item 3): the fix is to make the borrowed/Link chain_generate request

## IDLE CRASH DIAGNOSIS (10 Sep) - NOT ours, and the v9 hypotheses are refuted by measurement. Nothing built.
Pro Tools crashed while idle in the background (Build H loaded). THREE crashes today, all THIRD-PARTY plugins on Pro
Tools' own runloop, NONE with any EchoJay frame:
    10:39:34  EXC_BAD_ACCESS  thr88   ProTools_Red -> Foundation -> libxpc -> libdispatch   (Pro Tools' own XPC/dispatch)
    11:52:34  EXC_BAD_ACCESS  Main    "Black Box Analog Design" -> libc++abi (C++ exception unwind) via CFRunLoop
    12:29:28  EXC_BAD_ACCESS  Main    "Purple Audio MC 77" -> CFRunLoop -> HIToolbox -> AppKit -> DFW (the idle crash)
### Item 1 - THE IDLE CRASH (12:29:28), classified. EXC_BAD_ACCESS / SIGSEGV, KERN_INVALID_ADDRESS at 0x18 (a null-
    object member access; vmRegionInfo: "0x18 is not in any region" - a NULL dereference, NOT a huge/tagged address).
    So it is NOT memory exhaustion (that is EXC_RESOURCE or a vast address), NOT a file-descriptor limit (no EXC_RESOURCE/
    EMFILE), NOT disk-full (no I/O errno). It is a null-pointer dereference inside Purple Audio MC 77's own code, on the
    MAIN thread, dispatched by a CoreFoundation runloop source through HIToolbox/AppKit (a plugin-WINDOW timer/event) into
    Digidesign's framework. EchoJay V2 and Link are LOADED in the process but have ZERO frames on any thread - EchoJay is
    not in the crash. Same unstable plugin as the 9 Sep realtime crash (MC 77), a different failure of the same plugin.
### Item 2 - THE v9 ACK FILES: REFUTED by measurement. State dir ~/Library/Application Support/EchoJay/link RIGHT NOW:
    ctrl-ack-* = 0 files, ctrl-cmd-* = 0 files, lease-* = 0 files. They do NOT accumulate. A reaper EXISTS: the v9
    per-seq write deletes the PREVIOUS ctrl-ack-<uid>-<seq> on each new answer (LinkProcessor.cpp:1073 lastPerSeqAckFile_),
    and the legacy ctrl-ack-<uid> is deleted at every stale-ack site (PluginEditor.cpp 8038/8453/8697/8948/9040/28828/
    28964). At most ~one stale per-seq file per Link ever lingers; zero do now. The per-request channel I ruled in is
    bounded. NOT the cause, and not unbounded.
### Item 2b - what DID accumulate: rack-<uid>.json sidecars, 721 files / 200,845 bytes total (~278 B each). No reaper
    deletes them (grep found none). BUT: oldest is 25 Aug, they accrue ~45/day as Link uids churn - slow, PRE-v9, and
    200 KB is trivial. NOT the crash cause (a 200 KB dir does not null-deref MC 77's window timer). It IS genuinely
    unbounded and worth a bound (item 5), low priority.
### Item 3 - THE CACHE: REFUTED by measurement. Pro Tools RSS sampled at idle over 60 s: 2981 -> 3132 -> 3110 MB - FLAT,
    not growing. linkRackCache is std::map<uid, entry> (keyed - entries are REPLACED per uid, not appended), bounded by
    the number of distinct uids. No unbounded memory growth at idle. Not the cause.
### Item 4 - PREDATES v9? The failure CLASS (third-party plugins / ProTools_Red faulting on the runloop) appears on 6 Sep
    (2026-09-06-175328 and -215224, both ProTools_Red, before Build G/v9). MC 77-specific crashes appear only from 9 Sep
    on - because MC 77 ENTERED Sean's sessions on 9 Sep, not because of v9 (a scan of all Aug / 1-8 Sep reports found no
    MC 77 crash). So this is not a v9 regression; it is third-party plugin instability that surfaces whenever those
    plugins are loaded, on any build.
### Item 5 - THE BOUND (the only thing here that is ours to bound, and it is NOT the crash): a reaper for rack-<uid>.json.
    Shape: on registry ghost-reap (a uid whose publisher pid is dead and adopted), delete its rack-<uid>.json; plus a
    startup sweep of rack files whose uid is not a live registry slot. Ceiling = live Links, not sessions-ever. ~1.5 h,
    next trip. The ack channel and the cache already have bounds (measured above). No fix ships for the crash itself
    because the crash is a third-party plugin's null deref, not ours to patch.
### WHAT TO TELL SEAN (the actionable part, for the shoot): the crashes are third-party PLUGIN instability - Purple Audio
    MC 77 and Black Box Analog Design firing runloop/window timers into bad state while idle. Both crashed today with NO
    EchoJay involvement; MC 77 also caused the 9 Sep realtime crash. For a crash-free shoot, REMOVE Purple Audio MC 77 and
    Black Box Analog Design from the session (or at minimum close their plugin windows). EchoJay cannot patch a third-party
    plugin's timer. This is independent of Build H and of the dial work.

## DECISIVE (10 Sep, dump+code read): the "only suggest dialable" constraint is SENT but NOT ENFORCED - specified, never built
This changes the acceptance bar and kills the variance hunt before it starts (the reviewer's item 5).
### Item 1 - IS THE TOGGLE IN THE REQUEST? YES as a boolean, NO as an operative constraint.
    - The flag IS present: the body carries "autoDial": true (getAutoDialMode), the "Only suggest plugins EchoJay can
      auto-dial" toggle. So the server IS told the toggle is on.
    - BUT the SPECIFIED enforcement is ABSENT. There is NO "[AUTO-DIAL MODE ON]" note in the request (searched the whole
      body: AUTO-DIAL MODE / only offer / one-click dialable / unmappable all ABSENT), and the client injects NOTHING
      tied to getAutoDialMode() beyond the raw boolean. The system prompt DESCRIBES the toggle generically but never
      states it is ACTIVE for this turn or instructs the model to obey it.
    - The server side is report-only, and the code says so in as many words (EchoJayAPI.h, 25 Aug 2026): "NOTHING
      RESTRICTS ANYTHING TODAY. api/_auto-dial-check.js checkAutoDial is report-only ... The reply is not changed and no
      suggestion is withheld. Enforcement is SPECIFIED, not built (CONTRACT 11e/11f; Kathy's 25 Aug ruling: prevention
      via the [AUTO-DIAL MODE ON] note, then a backstop that refuses an op naming an unmappable plugin)."
    SO: the toggle is effectively DECORATIVE - sent, understood by nobody as a constraint, enforced by neither side. The
    reviewer's instinct was right; a dump read settles it. This is the whole of the "apparent randomness": the model is
    never firmly told to only offer dialable plugins or to dial them, so it does so ad hoc.
### Item 2 - THE NEOLD WUNDERLICH VIOLATION (offered with map=n under "dialable only"): NEITHER side filtered. The client
    sends the FULL plugin feed (EJFeed: split MODE=OFF, ~1078 names) - it does NOT pre-filter to the dialable set. The
    server check is report-only and withholds nothing. Both failed because the enforcement that would make either filter
    was never built. Not a bug in a filter; the filter does not exist.
### THE LOCAL BUILD (2 of 4) - and it is NOT a map question and NOT a clean path A/B. Slots:
    slot 0 EchoJay EQ (builtin)              settings_structured=y  status=applied
    slot 1 AMEK Mastering Compressor         settings_structured=n  (map CACHED) -> NO settings
    slot 2 UAD Ampex ATR-102                 settings_structured=n  (map CACHED) -> NO settings
    slot 3 bx_limiter True Peak              settings_structured=y  status=applied
    2/4 dialled. THE TWO FAILURES BOTH HAVE CACHED MAPS. So on the LOCAL path too, mapped third-party plugins get no
    settings - exactly the Link-path pattern. The local (2/4) and Link (0/5) builds are DIFFERENT CHAINS, not the same
    chain on two paths, so "2/4 vs 0/5" is not a controlled A/B - it is two draws of different chains. On BOTH paths the
    model omits settings_structured for mapped plugins, non-deterministically. This points at ELICITATION VARIANCE, not
    the path, and certainly not the map.
### ITEM 5 HONOURED - DO NOT MEASURE VARIANCE OF THIS REQUEST. The operative constraint (the [AUTO-DIAL MODE ON]
    instruction) is absent, so replaying the current body N times would measure a request that never carried the
    constraint - the wrong thing, exactly as the reviewer warned. The fix comes first: make the request CARRY and the
    responder HONOUR the constraint, THEN measure whether any variance remains. No variance replay run.
### THE ACCEPTANCE BAR, restated: with "only suggest dialable" ON, the bar is ZERO VIOLATIONS - no suggested slot may
    return settings_structured=n, and no suggested plugin may lack a map. A ratio (2/4, 4/6) is not a pass. The leg must
    COUNT VIOLATIONS and require zero across repeated runs (which also folds in the variance measurement, once the
    constraint is actually operative).
### THE FIX SHAPE (report before building, per standing practice): build the specified enforcement (11e/11f).
    (1) When autoDial is on, the request carries an explicit active-constraint instruction - the [AUTO-DIAL MODE ON]
        note: "Only offer plugins in the dialable set (the mapFps names). For EVERY suggested slot, provide
        settings_structured.params using the ids for that plugin. Do not offer a plugin you cannot dial."
    (2) The candidate feed is FILTERED to the dialable set when the toggle is on (getDialableRecommendableNames is
        already the set), so an unmappable plugin like NEOLD WUNDERLICH is never offered.
    (3) A backstop: a build op naming an unmappable plugin under the toggle is refused with a card message, not applied.
    WHERE IT LIVES: the note + feed filter can be CLIENT-side (request builder, EchoJayAPI.cpp / PluginEditor.cpp) - a
    client change, so a build+trip - OR, since "autoDial":true is ALREADY sent, SERVER-side (Kathy owns this per the 25
    Aug ruling), which needs NO client signing trip. Recommend the server route as primary (cheapest, already specified,
    already receiving the flag); the client note-injection is the fallback if it must be client-owned.
    This REOPENS Kathy on the SPECIFIED ENFORCEMENT (11e/11f) - distinct from the withdrawn "server omits settings"
    claim. The evidence that it is needed is this session: autoDial=true sent, constraint honoured by nothing.
    HOURS: client note + feed filter + backstop + a violations-counting leg: ~4 h and a build. Server route (Kathy):
    her estimate; no client trip. The variance A/B (item 2/3 of the second block) is DEFERRED until (1)-(3) are live -
    measuring it before is measuring the wrong request.
### The idle crash (prior section) is closed: third-party plugin (MC 77 / Black Box) instability, not ours.

## RE-OPENED & RE-CLASSIFIED (10 Sep): the idle crash. My earlier "NOT ours" was drawn from a missing frame - WITHDRAWN.
The reviewer is right: a null deref in a plugin's window timer names the thread that TOUCHED freed/invalid state, not the
one that invalidated it; absence of an EchoJay frame does not exclude EchoJay. Re-investigated with that in mind.
### What EchoJay did to MC 77's node in the 60 s before the 12:29:28 crash: NOTHING structural. The only EchoJay line in
    12:27:00-12:29:30 is 12:28:25.695 "EJApply: slot 0 EchoJay EQ CREATED" (a borrow commit, 63 s prior, a DIFFERENT
    slot). ZERO setSlotBypassed, ZERO rebuildGraph, ZERO removeNode, ZERO prepare/release, ZERO lease re-assert, ZERO
    INVARIANT lines in the window. The reviewer's mechanism (a message-thread graph mutation invalidating an open hosted
    editor) had NO TRIGGER here - no mutation occurred.
### Was MC 77's editor EchoJay-hosted and open? Evidence says NO for this crash: (a) no EJPane (editor-open) line for MC
    77 in the window; (b) the crash stack is Pro Tools' OWN plugin-window path (DFW = Digidesign framework -> AppKit ->
    HIToolbox -> CFRunLoop -> MC 77) - that is Pro Tools hosting MC 77's editor WINDOW as a direct AAX insert, NOT
    EchoJay's inline holder (which would show a JUCE component inside EchoJay's own window, not route through DFW). The
    Link is HEADLESS for hosted plugins (createEditorForSlot is called ONLY from V2's inline chain view, never by the
    Link). So the crashing MC 77 editor was Pro Tools', not ours, on this evidence.
### What ordering EXISTS between a message-thread graph mutation and an open hosted editor (quoted): PARTIAL, and it holds
    for the paths EchoJay controls.
    - PROCESSOR LIFETIME is protected: removeSlot moves the node to graveyard_ and thence leakedNodeStore
      (std::vector<Node::Ptr>, refcounted) - the processor is NEVER destroyed, so an open editor's processor pointer
      cannot dangle on removal (ChainHost.cpp 798-837, 2551). This satisfies JUCE's only hard editor contract (the
      editor must not outlive its processor).
    - STRUCTURAL BUILDS close editors first: chainListPanel.closeAllEditors() runs before applyChainEdits /
      loadChainFromJson / sendChainToLink and on tab change (PluginEditor.cpp 7771, 9008, 11283, 23465, 28435, 30504,
      30676). So a build does not mutate the graph under an open inline editor.
    THE RESIDUAL GAP (real, whether or not it caused these crashes): a graph mutation NOT initiated by the editor -
    specifically the LEASE-driven setSlotBypassed / rebuildGraph / prepareToPlay from the Link's 30 Hz poll - is NOT
    wrapped in closeAllEditors, and v9 change B ordered those against processBlock ONLY, not against an open editor.
    Today that gap is LATENT because the Link is headless (no inline editors on the leased graph); it would become live
    if any host that CAN show an inline editor took a lease-driven mutation while an editor was open. JUCE does NOT
    forbid prepareToPlay/releaseResources while an editor is open (unlike the explicit processBlock implied mutex), so
    there is no contract to quote here - only the plugin's own tolerance, which varies.
### RE-CLASSIFICATION, honest: NOT DETERMINABLE to be ours, with the positive evidence LEANING to the plugin as a direct
    Pro Tools insert - no EchoJay mutation in the window, no EchoJay editor-open for MC 77, the crash on Pro Tools' own
    plugin-window path, and EchoJay's own inline-editor paths guarded (graveyard + closeAllEditors). I am NOT recording
    "not ours"; I am recording "not shown to be ours, evidence points at the plugin/Pro Tools, one latent ordering gap
    of ours exists that did not fire here." WHAT WOULD SETTLE IT: (1) confirm from Sean's screenshot whether the open MC
    77 window was EchoJay's inline editor or Pro Tools' insert window (the stack says Pro Tools); (2) a controlled repro
    - EchoJay hosting a plugin's inline editor while a lease-driven mutation fires, vs the same plugin as a direct
    insert. The 11:52:34 crash (Black Box Analog Design, same Pro Tools main-thread window-timer shape, no EchoJay
    frame) is the same class.
### THE FIX regardless (the latent gap is worth closing): extend v9 change B's discipline to the editor - any host that
    can show an inline hosted editor closes it (or defers the mutation) before a lease-driven setSlotBypassed /
    rebuild / prepare, the same closeAllEditors gate that builds already use. Small, and it removes the class the
    reviewer named even though it did not fire here. ~2 h + a build; folds into the next Link/V2 trip.
### ITEM 6 - the 721 unreaped rack sidecars: real, unbounded, ours. Reaper costed 1.5 h (prior section), next trip.

    about ~1000 plugins cannot be refuted by 4 cached ones. The sampling error is recorded. BUT the loop as re-stated
    ("dialability resolved against the 22-entry client mapFps") is ALSO not what the server does - see below. The truth

    today). exit 0. It is a Vercel project (vercel.json, @anthropic-ai/sdk).
### THE CHAT HANDLER RESOLVES DIALABILITY AGAINST THE DATABASE, NOT THE CLIENT CACHE. In api/_auto-dial.js
    buildAutoDialSet keys on NAME (key:'name') against `mappedNames` = api/_chat-assembly.js getMappedNamesSet() ->
    lib/params-lib.js mappedPluginNames() -> redis().smembers("index:mapped-names") - the FULL server mapped-name index
    (the 2000+ set), read at request time (cached, TTL, fail-open). mapFps is used ONLY for stats (fpMiss/wouldRefuseByFp),
    NOT to gate dialability. So the client's 22-entry cache has NO say in the server's dialable set.
### AND THE ENFORCEMENT EXISTS AND IS HARD (api/chat.js 1263-1315, "AUTO-DIAL ENFORCEMENT (8 Sep 2026)"): when
    autoDial===true on chain_generate/chain_edit, the server (1) INJECTS a [AUTO-DIAL MODE ON] note listing the
    database-dialable subset of the feed (api/_chat-assembly.js buildAutoDialNote at the autoDial===true branch ~3557),
    and (2) HARD-ENFORCES: enforceAutoDial() REWRITES the reply to strip undialable suggestions (reply = enf.reply). It
    fails OPEN on any error ("an outage costs enforcement, never the turn"). So the reviewer's specified fix - resolve
    against the DB, restrict to dialable, note + backstop - IS BUILT in HEAD.

## ITEMS 1 & 2 (10 Sep, live reads, authorised, non-destructive): the index is NOT empty, enforcement IS live, and the
## bar still is not met - but the reason is model ELICITATION, not data. Did NOT deploy; did NOT touch the owner token/env.
### Item 1 - the live index:mapped-names is NOT empty. Established indirectly (I have no Redis creds locally; @upstash/redis
    Redis.fromEnv() needs UPSTASH_* env I did not pull): the live replay below RETURNED settings_structured for third-party
    plugins whose maps live in that index (SSL Fusion Vintage Drive, Newfangled Elevate, ...), which is impossible if the
    index were empty (the autoDial branch guards `if (mappedE.size > 0)` and enforceAutoDial returns unchanged on an empty
    allowed set). So the leading "empty index -> fail open" hypothesis is REFUTED by the server's own behaviour.
### Item 2 - replayed Sean's captured body verbatim against https://www.echojay.ai/api/chat (his account, his token,
    autoDialMode=true, nothing changed), 6 times. The server enforces and the model DOES emit third-party settings, but
    at a HIGHLY VARIABLE rate:
      run 0: 2/3 third-party dialled (Bettermaker prose-only)
      run 1: 0/3 (UAD Manley Variable Mu, Ozone 12 Vintage Tape, Ozone 12 Maximizer prose-only)
      run 2: 2/3 (SSL Fusion Transformer prose-only)
      run 3: 2/3 (UAD Ampex ATR-102 prose-only)
      run 4: 1/3 (Vertigo VSC-2, Ozone 12 Vintage Tape prose-only)
      run 5: 0/3 (Drawmer 1973, Looptrotter SA2RATE2, Invisible_Limiter_G3 prose-only)
    ~7/18 third-party dialled (~39%), 0/3 to 2/3, DIFFERENT chains each run (chain_generate re-samples the model).
### WHAT enforceAutoDial DOES, quoted (api/_auto-dial-check.js:132): it STRIPS undialable suggestions - `bad(name) =
    !allowed.has(name) && !isBuiltin(name)`, allowed = the DB-resolved dialable set - and returns unchanged if the
    allowed set is empty. So every plugin surviving in the replied chain is DIALABLE (in the DB) or a builtin. Therefore
    the prose-only slots above are DIALABLE plugins the model chose to describe in PROSE instead of emitting
    settings_structured. enforceAutoDial cannot fix that: it removes undialable NAMES, it does not force settings onto a
    dialable slot.
### WHICH OF THE THREE: none cleanly - it is a FOURTH thing. NOT "index empty" (refuted). NOT "HEAD undeployed" (the live
    server clearly runs the enforcement - it strips and it dials). NOT "deployed and working" to the bar (violations every
    run). The truth: deployed, index populated, undialable plugins correctly excluded, but the MODEL emits
    settings_structured for dialable plugins only ~39% of the time, stochastically. This is the ELICITATION VARIANCE the
    earlier ruling wanted measured - now measurable because the constraint is operative on live - and it is the whole of
    the remaining defect. Sean's original 1/6 was a bad draw of the same process, not a categorically broken server.
### CONSEQUENCE FOR THE BAR: "zero violations, repeated runs" is NOT achievable against the current live server - not for
    a data or deploy reason, but because the model's settings emission for dialable plugins is unreliable. A retest in
    host might land 2/3 on a good draw and 0/3 on a bad one. So a same-day seed/deploy does NOT fix it; the fix is
    ELICITATION RELIABILITY.
### THE FIX (server-side, no client build, no signing trip; iterable in minutes): make the model emit settings_structured
    for EVERY dialable slot reliably. Where it lives: api/_chat-assembly.js (the [AUTO-DIAL MODE ON] note and the chain-
    block format instruction) and the reply-path. Options, in order of strength:
      (1) STRENGTHEN THE NOTE/SCHEMA: state that under the toggle EVERY suggested slot MUST carry settings_structured.params,
          with a worked per-plugin example, and make the chain-block format require it (schema-level, not prose).
      (2) A SERVER BACKSTOP THAT FILLS, not just strips: for a dialable slot the model returned prose-only, the server has
          the map (registryByFp) and could attach a default/derived settings_structured rather than leaving prose - or
          RE-ASK the model for that slot's values. This makes zero-violations structural, not probabilistic.
      (3) Measure the rate under each change (the same replay-N harness used here) and require zero across runs before it
          ships - the acceptance bar, now runnable server-side with no build.
    This is Kathy's tree (or a SANCTIONED deploy with an owner); the reviewer/Sean authorises knowing what rides along.
### Cost of the reads: 6 chat turns on Sean's account (the authorised replay). Nothing written, nothing deployed, owner
    token untouched, no vercel env pull, no commit to that tree.

### ITEM 1 - THE PER-SLOT REPAIR PASS, shape:
    WHERE: a new server module (api/_dial-repair.js) called from api/chat.js AFTER enforceAutoDial, before the reply is
    returned. Uses registryByFp (already loaded) and the same LLM client. NO client change, NO plugin build.
    WHAT: parse the chain block; a REPAIR CANDIDATE is any slot whose plugin name is in the `allowed` (DB-dialable) set
    but arrived with no/empty settings_structured. For each candidate, issue a NARROW follow-up completion scoped to that
    ONE plugin: input = the plugin name + its parameter ids/ranges from the map + the model's own prose for that slot as
    the intent; instruction = "return ONLY settings_structured.params as {id: value} using these ids and real-world values
    matching the description; no prose, no other plugins." Merge the returned params into that slot. Retry up to N per slot
    (fresh completion each); take the first that returns valid structured params.
    RETRY COUNT N: the 39% is the per-attempt rate for the FULL multi-plugin generate. The SCOPED per-slot task (one
    plugin, ids given, values only) is far easier and should succeed much higher per attempt - but that MUST BE MEASURED,
    not assumed. Start N=3; the acceptance harness reports the scoped per-attempt success; if it is ~90% then N=3 gives
    <0.1% per-slot failure (effectively zero); if the scoped rate is lower, raise N until 20 runs are clean. Report both
    the scoped rate and the N that clears the bar.
    TOKEN COST on a six-slot chain: worst case all six dialable and ~4 arrive prose -> 4 candidates x up to 3 scoped calls;
    each scoped call is small (one plugin's id list + short prose in, a values object out) ~1-2k in / ~100-300 out. Worst
    case ~24k tokens; typical ~8-12k (most succeed on attempt 1). Modest beside the ~100k primary, and cheap (no big
    system prompt on the scoped calls).
    FAILURE HANDLING (explicit, per the ruling): if all N attempts fail for a slot, it is REPORTED as a violation - a field
    in the reply and a server log line - and left as prose, NEVER silently prose. The client's EJDialSummary already shows
    settings_structured=n; the server additionally names it so a clean run is provable and a failure is visible.
### ACCEPTANCE (item 3), runnable by me against the preview URL: the same replay, 20 runs, toggle on; ZERO violations
    (every dialable slot carries settings_structured), the PER-RUN rate reported not just the total, control = the current
    live 39%. I can run this by curl the moment Sean gives me the preview URL + protection-bypass.

## ITEM 2 - IS THE REPAIR REMOVING/OVERWRITING PRIMARY SETTINGS? Investigated at the DEPLOYED commit
   (3b270e5, re-cloned from github.com/studioseand-cyber/echojay-saas). ANSWER: NO. The suspected mechanism
   (merge replaces settings_structured with only the range-validated controls) is REFUTED by the code.
### _dial-repair.js line 119: `if (hasStructured(slot)) continue;`. hasStructured is comprehensive - it is
   TRUE if settings_structured has any of params | controls | bands | eq_bands non-empty. So ANY slot the
   primary already filled is SKIPPED and never touched. The merge (line 130,
   `Object.assign({}, slot.settings_structured, {controls: filled})`) runs ONLY on a slot that reached it
   WITHOUT structured settings - a prose-only slot. On such a slot the assign is purely additive; it also
   preserves any pre-existing settings_structured keys and writes only `.controls`.
### valueOk / range-rejection applies ONLY inside askOneSlot, to the REPAIR's OWN scoped-call output. It can
   only shrink what the repair ADDS. It never sees or filters a primary value.
### The other reply-rewrite on the path, enforceAutoDial (_auto-dial-check.js), also does NOT strip kept
   slots: its chain branch is `block.chain.filter(sl => keep?)` - kept slots retain their FULL object incl.
   the primary's settings_structured; only UNdialable slots are removed wholesale, by design.
### So a pure-addition pass it is, and it CANNOT lower the dialled rate. The 51 violations are dialable slots
   the PRIMARY left prose-only (~61% of dialable slots miss at baseline) that the repair also failed to
   rescue - now REPORTED as violations instead of left silent. They are surfaced misses, not removed settings.
   ~2.5 unrescued prose slots/run over 20 runs is consistent with the 39% baseline, not a regression from it.
### On a run where the repair rescued NOTHING, `changed` stays false and reply is byte-IDENTICAL to
   post-enforce - so the repair cannot have caused "only EchoJay EQ dialled" either. That symptom is the
   BASELINE elicitation miss (primary emits third-party settings ~39% of the time), unchanged by an additive
   pass that mostly failed. CONCLUSION: the repair was WASTEFUL (3-4 mostly-failing model calls) but NOT
   destructive. Rollback still correct (net-negative cost for ~zero benefit); it was not undoing output damage.

## ITEM 3 - PROVIDER STRUCTURED-OUTPUT SUPPORT IN THIS STACK. The scoped call hits api.anthropic.com/v1/messages
   directly (chat.js callModel), free-form text + `text.match(/\{[\s\S]*\}/)` + JSON.parse, max_tokens:500.
   That is the unreliable primitive. Anthropic's structured-output primitive is FORCED TOOL USE (there is no
   OpenAI-style response_format/JSON-mode). The scoped call under it: define ONE tool `dial` with
   input_schema = JSON Schema built FROM the registry entry (mode controls -> enum of their labels; anchored
   controls -> number with minimum/maximum from range), set tool_choice:{type:'tool',name:'dial'}, read the
   tool block's `.input`. Prose becomes structurally impossible, AND item-3's valueOk folds INTO the schema
   (the model cannot emit an unknown control or an out-of-range value). Retries then cover only API errors.
   NOT WRITTEN/DEPLOYED yet, per the ruling.

## ITEM 5 - MEASUREMENT PATH. Cloudflare 1010 = browser-signature ban on the python-urllib client, not Sean's
   access. The plugin (EchoJayAPI.cpp) sets no custom User-Agent - it uses JUCE createInputStream's default
   (curl/NSURLSession), which Cloudflare passes. FIX before next measurement: send a realistic UA on the
   harness request (browser-like or the JUCE default) and verify one request clears before running a batch.

## SUSPICION WITHDRAWN + FRAMING CORRECTED (10 Sep, per ruling). The destructive-merge suspicion is WITHDRAWN:
   hasStructured (_dial-repair.js:119) skips any slot the primary filled, so the merge only ever touches a
   prose-only slot and is additive. Framing corrected across this record: the repair was WASTEFUL (3-4 mostly-
   failing scoped calls per build, 0/20 benefit), NOT harmful to output. Rollback stands as the right call on
   cost, not as damage control.

## RULING (10 Sep): the schema goes on the PRIMARY chain call, not only the scoped repair. Report before building.
   Findings (nothing built/deployed):
### CLIENT IMPACT = NONE, IF the server preserves the wire format. The client's entire dependency is
   EJReplyBlocks::extractChainBlock (EchoJayAPI.cpp:3675 -> the delimiters <<<ECHOJAY_CHAIN>>> / <<<END_CHAIN>>>)
   which pulls the JSON out and leaves the prose; ChainHost reads settings from settings_structured.{controls|
   params|bands} (ChainHost.cpp:1527, 3596, 4093). It does NOT care whether the JSON came from freeform text
   or a forced tool's `.input`. So if the server keeps emitting prose + the same delimiters + the same chain
   JSON shape, NO plugin change and NO plugin build reopens. (No streaming on this path: sendChat -> /api/chat,
   full reply, then extractChainBlock.)
### PRIMARY UNDER FORCED TOOL = REAL TENSION. Anthropic tool_choice:{type:'tool',name} returns a tool_use block
   and NO text; forced tool use SUPPRESSES prose. The current primary emits prose + the embedded block in ONE
   text completion (chat.js:1120-1136, no tools today). So a single completion CANNOT produce both the user
   prose AND a forced structured chain. Resolution = TWO-CALL primary: call 1 prose (conversational reply),
   call 2 forced tool `dial_chain` whose input_schema is the whole chain block; server assembles prose +
   delimiters + JSON.stringify(tool.input). Cost: 2 primary calls, but deletes the repair's 3-4 scoped calls.
### WHOLE-CHAIN SCHEMA LIMIT (flagged honestly): one input_schema over the whole chain CANNOT enforce per-plugin
   ranges/enums, because JSON Schema can't make an array item's `controls` sub-schema depend on that item's
   sibling `name` (no clean per-item cross-field conditional). It GUARANTEES structure (every slot carries a
   controls object, valid JSON, no prose) - which fixes the actual bottleneck, the ~61% prose miss - but not
   per-control validity. Two ways to get validity too: (a) keep valueOk as a post-filter after the whole-chain
   tool, or (b) make call 2 PER-SLOT forced tools, each with that plugin's schema (enums for mode controls,
   min/max for anchored) - item-3 validation folds INTO the schema. RECOMMENDATION: whole-chain forced tool on
   the primary + valueOk post-filter; escalate to per-slot schemas only if measurement shows range violations
   surviving. Retries become error-handling only, never the mechanism.
### MEASUREMENT BAR (unchanged): fix harness UA, confirm ONE request clears Cloudflare 1010, THEN twenty runs to
   ZERO violations; report the number regardless. Nothing deploys tonight; Sean authorises tomorrow with numbers.

## ================= FINALIZED BUILD SPEC (10 Sep, approved; BUILD TOMORROW, nothing deploys tonight) =================
## R1 - PROSE AND SETTINGS MUST NOT BE ABLE TO DISAGREE (the load-bearing requirement).
   Two independent calls WILL drift - Call 1 says "4:1, 4-5 dB GR", Call 2 invents its own numbers, and the
   plugin says one thing and does another. That is WORSE than an undialled slot: it reads as the plugin lying,
   not falling short, and it is plainly visible on camera. Therefore:
   - Call 2 TRANSCRIBES a decision already made. Its user message CONTAINS Call 1's prose, and its instruction
     is to encode EXACTLY what the prose stated, never to decide independently.
   - Where the prose is genuinely ambiguous for a control, the tool OMITS that control rather than inventing a
     value. An omission is honest; a contradiction is not.
   - NEW VIOLATION CLASS: prose/settings DISAGREEMENT, reported separately from missing settings.
## R2 - ARCHITECTURE (as proposed): two-call primary; Call 2 = whole-chain forced `dial_chain` tool
   (tool_choice pinned); server reassembles prose + <<<ECHOJAY_CHAIN>>> delimiters + JSON.stringify(tool.input)
   so the WIRE FORMAT IS UNCHANGED and NO plugin build reopens. valueOk stays as a POST-FILTER. Per-slot schemas
   ONLY if measurement shows range violations surviving. Retries for API errors ONLY, never as the mechanism.
## R3 - valueOk REJECTS THE OFFENDING CONTROL, NEVER THE SLOT. A dropped slot recreates the exact failure we
   spent this evening ruling out. Reject control -> leave the slot with its other valid controls, count an
   out-of-range violation for that control; never remove the slot.
## R4 - MEASUREMENT BEFORE ANY DEPLOY: (a) fix the harness UA, (b) confirm ONE request clears Cloudflare 1010,
   (c) then TWENTY runs. Report violations BY CLASS - missing-settings | out-of-range | prose/settings-
   disagreement - AND the total. Report the number whether or not it flatters the change. The disagreement leg:
   for a SAMPLE of runs, parse the prose's stated values (ratios, dB, Hz, times) and check the emitted control
   values match; a mismatch is a disagreement violation.
## R5 - NOTHING DEPLOYS until Sean has the numbers. Production STAYS on the rolled-back build (7sewsns69,
   dpl_32tjzuTMj82cd93tHHbLgVy3kLnp) overnight. Sean authorises the deploy tomorrow with the numbers in front.
## (Open items unchanged: 721 rack sidecars; editor-ordering gap under lease-driven mutation; idle-crash
##  re-classification; brief-questions separation from the toggle.)

## THE ERROR (mine, load-bearing all afternoon): I asserted "the editor builds via sendChat ->
   /api/chat (3 sites, zero streaming), which is where the repair is wired." WRONG. The plugin routes a
   CHAIN BUILD to /api/chat-stream whenever ~/Library/EchoJay/stream_chains exists at send time
   (PluginEditor.cpp:27278 streamChainBuildsEnabled, choke point :27305, read PER SEND). ALL the auto-dial
   machinery - the note, the DB-resolved allowed set, enforceAutoDial, and the repair - lives ONLY on
   /api/chat. The streaming path has NONE of it. The server was correct; Sean's requests were going elsewhere.
### COST: the entire afternoon - the repair, the resolver fix, the 0/20, three production deploys and a
   rollback - was built on a path Sean's chain builds were not using. It also made me DISMISS Kathy's
   cae83fb chat-stream gate as "not touching Sean" when it governs the EXACT path he was on (see item 5, open).
### HOW IT HAPPENED: I read code and inferred the endpoint instead of OBSERVING a live request - while the
   dev-mode dump and the unified-log EJStream routing line were on disk the whole time.
### STANDING RULE, EXTENDED: which endpoint a request reaches is an OBSERVATION, not an inference. Confirm it
   from a captured request or a server log BEFORE building anything on it. (Same one-fact-two-sources class as
   the four timing/liveness defects already filed today.)

## ================= METERS-DRIVE-SETTINGS: own channel vs Link (10 Sep, diagnosis, NO build) =================
## Sean's narrowing: meters drive the settings when he builds on the main's OWN channel (Mix Bus); they do not
   when he builds on a Link (the Link request carries "no live readings exist").
## ITEM 1 - MECHANISM + AVAILABILITY = PLUMBING GAP, CONTAINED.
   - Mechanism: the chat meter context is assembled CLIENT-SIDE from the OWN channel's engine -
     buildMeterSnapshotInjection(getMeterEngine().getMeterDataJSON(), reduceSpectrumWindow(), reduceMacroWindow())
     at PluginEditor.cpp:25104. On a Link target this instance's engine measured nothing for that strip
     (specFrameCount<=0), so the snapshot is omitted and the note fires:
     EchoJayAPI.cpp:2688 - "NOTHING HAS PLAYED THROUGH THIS CHANNEL YET, so no live readings exist ..."
     (condition askToPlay = the own spectrum ring is empty).
   - Availability: the Link's readings DO exist and the main ALREADY holds them. Each Link publishes a
     LinkMeterFrame into shared memory ~10Hz (LinkShm.h:199+); the main reads it via readLinkMeterFrame
     (PluginEditor.cpp:6882) to paint MOM/SHORT/INT/PSR/PLR per strip. readLinkMeterFrame is called ONLY for
     mixer paint + Link UI (6882/29213/29531), NEVER by the request builder. So the data is in SHM/registry,
     simply not assembled into the request for a Link target. Contained: plumbing, not measurement.
## ITEM 2 - MEASURED FIELDS, field by field.
   Own-channel build sends [METER SNAPSHOT v2/v3]: LUFS (momentary/short/integrated), crest, true peak, width,
     correlation, PSR, PLR, oversCount, macroBands, bandCrest, FINE spectrum window + macro window.
   Link build sends NONE of these measured meter fields. The ONE measured field that already crosses from a
     Link today is [DETECTED KEY] (PluginEditor.cpp:25127; a Link publishes key fields from its channel).
   Absent from the Link request but PRESENT/measured in the Link's own SHM frame (LinkShm.h): momentary/short/
     integrated LUFS, rmsL/R, peakL/R, truePeakMax/Cur, peakFastL/R, crest, correlation, width, bandRel[6]
     (macro sub..air), lra, shortTermTP (=> PSR/PLR). All published, none assembled into the request.
## ITEM 3 - WHERE THE FIX LANDS (the question with teeth): CLIENT-SIDE => REOPENS A PLUGIN BUILD + SIGNING TRIP.
   The meter context is assembled client-side and the Link readings are NOT in the request today (only key
   rides). Server cannot fix it alone because it never receives the Link's readings. The client must read
   readLinkMeterFrame for the TARGET Link and assemble a snapshot into the request. Precedent exists (key is
   already plumbed Link->request through the same frame), so the change is well-scoped - but it is a client
   change and an AAX re-sign, not a server-only ship. State this plainly, do not discover it later.
## ITEM 4 - GENUINELY UNAVAILABLE: the FINE spectrum only. The Link frame carries macro 6-band spectral
   (bandRel[6], sub..air, dB rel to band mean), so a Link build CAN be loudness-aware AND macro-band-aware.
   It does NOT carry the fine spectrum ring the own channel has (reduceSpectrumWindow, the full FFT reduction).
   So: a Link build can be meter-aware and macro-spectrum-aware, but NOT fine-spectrum-aware. Record honestly;
   do not promise fine-spectrum parity for a Link.

## ITEM 1 - FRESHNESS GATE ADDED (was missing). readMeterFrame is seqlock-CONSISTENT but NOT fresh: a stopped
   or idle Link returns its last frozen frame with seq settled; a just-claimed slot returns a -100/0 blank.
   The prior guard only checked integrated>-70, so a stopped Link's last-programme integrated (~-14) would have
   passed and emitted the persistent group - inviting the model to cite a FROZEN reading with live confidence.
   FIX (PluginEditor.cpp buildTargetLinkMeterSnapshot): emit ONLY when
        f.audioStale == 0  AND  f.integrated > -70.0f
   else return {} and the honest no-live-readings note stands. Residual (publisher DIED mid-playback, frozen
   audioStale==0) is owned by the registry liveness getLinkDisplayList reflects, same as [LINK LEVELS].

   and sub sit above..." with nothing measured. That is the plugin ASSERTING THINGS ABOUT THE USER'S MIX THAT
   IT HAS NO BASIS FOR. The Link-meter fix replaces this with CITED measurements on the Link path, but the
   confabulation remains wherever data is absent (own channel with nothing played, an idle Link, a failed
   capture). It undercuts the plugin's core claim - that it responds to THIS audio. HIGH on the defect list;
   the empty-branch prompt says "do not characterise loudness, dynamics, stereo image or any metric" yet the

## ITEM 1 REVISED (11 Sep): RECENCY WINDOW replaces the too-strict audioStale==0 gate.
   The first cut gated on audioStale==0 (audio flowing THIS INSTANT). The own channel gates on
   specFrameCount>0 ("played this session"), so play/stop/ask answered on the main and not the Link - an
   asymmetry that reads as a bug. FIX: a bounded RECENCY WINDOW.
   - N = 60s. Why: a play->stop->build gesture finishes well inside a minute, so the Link answers whenever the
     main would; a minute-plus-old reading is likely a different section/take, so it hard-rejects to the honest
     note. And the block STATES the age ("measured moments ago" / "measured about N s ago"), so a 40s reading
     is never implied to be live. Cleanly excludes "stopped for minutes". Named constant kLinkMeterRecencyMs.
   - The frame carries no wall-clock and the Link is UNCHANGED, so recency is tracked CLIENT-SIDE: the
     processor's 1 Hz timer (timerCallback -> updateLinkAudioRecency) stamps linkLastFlowingMs_[uid] whenever
     it samples audioStale==0. buildTargetLinkMeterSnapshot emits iff flowMs!=0 && (now-flowMs)<=60000 &&
     integrated>-70. Both touch the map on the message thread only (no lock).
   - BOUNDARY TEST extended, 7/7 PASS: flowing-now EMIT; 30s EMIT; 59s (just inside) EMIT; 61s (just outside)
     NO BLOCK; 5min NO BLOCK; never-seen NO BLOCK; recent-but-blank(-100) NO BLOCK.

## ITEM 6 - STATED LIMITATION (a Link build vs a main build): a Link publishes six MACRO bands (bandRel[6]),
   not the fine per-bin spectrum window, so a Link build carries loudness + macro-band balance but NOT:
     - fine spectrum (the 64-bin windowed FFT the own channel's reduceSpectrumWindow provides);
     - oversCount (true-peak over count - not in the LinkMeterFrame);
     - bandCrest (per-band crest lo120/mid120_5k/hi5k - not in the LinkMeterFrame).
   These three are DECLARED gaps, not bugs and not fixed: the Link does not publish them. Everything else the
   own channel's chat injections carry, the Link now carries. A Link build is meter-aware and macro-spectrum-
   aware; it is not fine-spectrum-aware, by the publisher's design.

## RULING "heard 0s" on the target-Link [CHAIN LEVELS] line (15 Sep 2026). NOT BUILT YET - deferred behind the retest.
   DIAGNOSIS (observed, PluginEditor.cpp:29239): the Link path fills inLevelDb/inPeakDb/inCrestDb from the frame but
   never assigns d.inHeardS, so it prints the struct default 0.0f. LinkMeterFrame carries no gated-heard-seconds
   field (only audioBlocks = a processBlock counter, and audioStale). The own path prints a real duration
   (inHeardS = heardHops_ * kHopSeconds, EchoJayLevelTally.h:331). So "heard 0s" beside a valid BS.1770 integrated
   -22.3 LUFS is a guaranteed contradiction on every Link build - a MISSING source, not the pre-gain bug's wrong source.
   RULING: OMIT THE CLAUSE on the Link path - do not print "heard ..." at all there, matching the block's existing
   honest-absence pattern ("p10/p90 not available from a Link", "post-chain output not measured on a Link"). Zero new
   machinery.
   EXPLICITLY FORBIDDEN: (a) deriving heard from audioBlocks - that is elapsed processing time, not gated-heard time;
   a Link open and silent 10 min would falsely report 10 min "heard" (one word, two definitions - the exact defect
   class eliminated this week). (b) adding a new LinkMeterFrame field now - a shm schema change means rebuilding and
   re-signing BOTH plugins days before the shoot for a cosmetic line.
   PROPER FIX (AFTER FILMING): carry a real gated-heard-seconds value for the Link (its own BS.1770 heard duration),
   so the Link line can state a true "heard" like the own channel does.
   SEQUENCE GATING THE BUILD: retest first on installed FE27F78E, flag deleted. If the reply cites -22.3 LUFS and sets
   +4.3 dB pre-gain, "heard 0s" is cosmetic and rides the NEXT build with the Finding 1 fix (one trip, everything).
   If the reply is vague again, "heard 0s" becomes a live suspect and earns its own attention - and its retest dump is
   to be diffed against the 10:10:07 dump first to establish what changed. ONE signing trip, carrying everything,
   once we know what is actually broken.

## ESCALATED: macroBands + momentary DROPPED live (10:46) but present at 10:10 - required-set guard GREEN. UNDERSTOOD, NOT PATCHED.
   MECHANISM (read from the emit + publish path, not inferred):
   - Emit omit conditions (PluginEditor.cpp:29245,29253-54): momentary omitted iff f.momentary <= -70; macroBands
     omitted iff every |bandRel[i]| <= 0.01 (anyBand false).
   - Publisher (LinkProcessor.cpp:375-450): momentary/shortTerm come from MeterEngine short windows; bandRel is
     written ONLY if n>0 bands have absolute dB > -119 (else the fresh local frame's bandRel stays all-zero). An
     audioStale (no blocks ~1s) hard-floors momentary/shortTerm/shortTermTP to -100; but at 10:46 shortTerm was
     -37.5 (not -100), so audioStale was FALSE - not the stale override.
   - MeterEngine (MeterEngine.cpp:910-926): momentary is a 400ms window with fast attack, 20 dB/s release; integrated
     is the separate cumulative gated value.
   SHARED CAUSE of the field drop AND the crest jump (points 1 & 3): the 10:46 frame was sampled during the
   POST-PLAYBACK DECAY/near-silence. momentary (400ms) had decayed below -70 -> omitted; macroBandDb fell below the
   -119 floor -> bandRel all zero -> macroBands omitted; shortTerm (3s) lagged at -37.5; crest inflated to 29.8 dB
   (latched peak -4.5 vs the now-quiet recent RMS - an ARTEFACT of measuring during decay, NOT a real vocal dynamic).
   Meanwhile integrated/truePeakMax/PLR (cumulative/latched) held: input -22.2 (was -22.3), peak -4.5 (=), PLR 17.7
   (was 17.9). The freshness gate (recent-flow + integrated>-70) passed a decaying snapshot as a live reading.
   "heard 0s" does NOT share this cause: it was 0s at 10:10 TOO, with audio playing and all fields healthy - proof it
   is the unpopulated inHeardS field (never assigned on the Link path), independent of measurement state. So 2 of the
   3 share a cause; heard-0s is separate but converges on the same remedy.
   HARNESS UNFALSIFIABLE for exactly these two fields (point 2, THIRD instance of seeded-frame != real-frame):
   harness.cpp:85,90-92 seed f.momentary=-18.5 (always >-70) and all six bandRel nonzero (min 0.3, all >0.01),
   audioStale=0. The momentary-omit, macroBands-omit, and audioStale branches can NEVER execute in the harness, so
   the required-set check asserting mom+macroBands present is unfalsifiable there - it proves "healthy frame emits
   all" and never constructs a decaying/silent frame. Same class as the unreachable call site and the replayed body.
   FIX IMPLICATION (for the next build, per the reviewer): the levels block must DECLARE a partial/decayed window
   honestly ("measurement window just ended / partial - momentary and bands not currently available") instead of
   printing a confident integrated beside a zero heard and two silently-missing fields; and the harness must seed a
   SECOND frame in the decay/silent state and assert the honest declaration BOTH directions (Build G rule).

## CATCH 1 RESOLVED — slot 0 EchoJay EQ req=4 applied=2 is an ACCOUNTING artifact, and a REPORTING-BLINDNESS defect, EQ-specific.
   The payload was params{phase_mode, auto_gain} + eq_bands[2]. SurgicalEqProcessor::applyStructured (SurgicalEqProcessor.cpp:517)
   threads the count pointers ONLY into applyEqBands (line 566); the params branch calls applyParams(...) WITHOUT them
   (line 560), and the header documents "appliedOut/skippedOut count bands only." So applied=2 = the 2 bands; the 2
   params are UNCOUNTED. phase_mode and auto_gain ARE valid EQ schema ids (schema() lines ~9,13; setParamValue handles
   both), so they most likely LANDED - this is NOT a proven value loss. BUT the params-group outcome is not reported at
   all: a dropped id, or a string like phase_mode="linear" coerced to 0.0 by the numeric applyParams (wrong value), would
   be INVISIBLE and status=applied prints regardless. So the applier is structurally BLIND on the params group.
   EQ-SPECIFIC: root cause is the EQ's dual-shape (params + eq_bands) counting bands only. Third-party slots use the
   map/applySettings path, which counts and reports every param (Lindell 2/2). "Larger payloads lose values" is NOT a
   general defect - it is the EQ's accounting. IMPLICATION FOR BAR 1: the applied COUNT is untrustworthy for the EQ;
   Bar 1 must measure applied by READBACK of actual param values, never by the applier's self-count.

## CATCH 2 RESOLVED — A2 INVERTS. The dialable-only toggle filters SERVER-SIDE, not against the 37-entry local cache.
   fetchDialableIdentities (EchoJayAPI.cpp:557) POSTs /api/params/lookup {mode:"exists", plugins:[{ik,manufacturer}]}
   and marks dialable = the server row's mapped_versions is non-empty. HARDENING: any non-200 (or 200 without a results
   array) sets anyFail and KEEPS THE FULL LIST - the toggle FAILS OPEN. fpAttempted in param_maps.json is literally null,
   so the local cache cannot even tell attempted-empty from never-attempted. Therefore local absence of Millennia TCL-2 /
   SSL Native FlexVerb proves NOTHING about server dialability, and my A2 ("toggle broken, offered unmapped plugins") is
   REFUTED. Two live possibilities, indistinguishable without observing the lookup response for the turn (no lookup line
   was logged at 10:46):
     (i)  they ARE server-dialable -> toggle correctly offered them -> model produced no settings -> A1 extends to ALL
          FOUR undialled slots (a pure model defect with the server map capable).
     (ii) the lookup failed-open (non-200/timeout) -> the full list incl. undialable was offered -> a degraded-network
          condition, not a logic bug.
   Distinguishing (i) from (ii) needs ONE observation of /api/params/lookup for a build - server traffic, to be budgeted.

## DIAL RESOLVED — possibility (i) CONFIRMED by one corrected /api/params/lookup call (200). A2 fully dissolved.
   The plugin sets NO explicit User-Agent (postJSON sends Content-Type + Authorization only; transportHeaders empty in
   release), so it rides JUCE/CFNetwork's default signature. First lookup was Cloudflare-1010 UA-banned (Python-urllib);
   the corrected call used a CFNetwork/Darwin UA and reached the app.
   RESPONSE (200, deployment dpl_2jbLpKK4zxRP5beJAUxsHtQcGsiK):
     Millennia TCL-2      (ik AudioUnit|6e6e5232|1.11.1): exists=true, mapped_versions=["1.11.1"]  -> DIALABLE server-side
     SSL Native FlexVerb  (ik AudioUnit|61686c60|6.6.14): exists=true, mapped_versions=["6.6.14"]  -> DIALABLE server-side
   BOTH mapped_versions non-empty => both server-dialable. So the dialable-only toggle was CORRECT to offer them, and the
   model produced NO settings for them anyway. A1 THEREFORE COVERS ALL FOUR UNDIALLED SLOTS (Purple MC 77, AVOX WARM,
   Millennia TCL-2, SSL Native FlexVerb) - every one server-dialable, every one left empty by the model. It is a single
   defect: a prompt/model defect with the server map fully capable. SUBSTITUTION IS NOT THE FIX - there is nothing to
   substitute; the fix is entirely prompt/model. "only EchoJay EQ dials" and "map too thin" and "toggle broken" are all
   now dead - three refuted theories, one cause.
   LATENT (separate, not triggered here): the client's LOCAL param_maps cache lacks Millennia/FlexVerb (map=n in
   EJDialSummary) though the SERVER has them. If the model HAD dialled them, client apply would have had no local map to
   write with. Server-dialable and client-applicable can disagree; worth a fetch-on-offer guard later, not now.
   AMENDMENT 2 second consequence recorded: the dialable lookup runs ONCE PER SCAN and the result is cached; the 10:46
   build used the cached set, so no fresh per-turn call happened by design. A per-turn fail-open was never possible - any
   failure would be at scan time and persist until the next scan. Blocking is therefore rare and rescan-recoverable.

## LATCH DESIGN + CATCH C/D/E (15 Sep 2026) - V2 last-good meter latch, in the build (not yet compiled).
   The latch retains, per Link uid, the most recent frame whose SHORT-WINDOW fields were valid (momentary > -70 AND
   a bandRel above floor), captured in the 1 Hz recency poll; assembly serves cumulative from the live frame and
   short-window from the latch, so a build fired at the quiet moment after playback reports while-flowing values.
   CATCH C - THE WALL CLOCK WAS REMOVED DELIBERATELY. The old 5-min flow-recency Lapse answered "is this Link alive";
   the wrong question, and it fired on Sean's real workflow (play, listen, answer four brief questions, build) as a
   false "not heard recently". Re-derived from what actually invalidates a latched reading: different material or a
   changed Link chain BOTH re-latch the instant audio next flows (self-correcting); a reassigned channel is a
   different instanceUid (re-minted on collision) and drops the latch. So a wall clock guarded nothing. The latch is
   now SESSION-SCOPED (in-memory, dies with the instance / drop-on-disappear), the age is ALWAYS stated, and Lapse is
   reserved for NEVER-heard. DO NOT re-add a wall-clock Lapse thinking it was an oversight - it was removed on purpose.
   CATCH D - the printed level numbers come from two frames (input/peak cumulative, crest short-window), so the block
   STATES which are overall-since-playback and which are while-flowing, rather than let peak-minus-crest read as input.
   CATCH E - a latch existing is PROOF audio flowed, so "never heard" is only the no-latch case. When the live
   cumulative is invalid (Link restart floors integrated) but a latch is held, cumulative comes from the LATCH too
   (it carries integrated/true peak/LRA); pre-gain follows the integrated actually used (one source per branch), and
   the block says all three numbers are while-flowing. Four latch states to assert in the harness: healthy-live,
   decayed-live-with-latch, live-cumulative-invalid-with-latch (E), no-latch-ever. measurementAgeMs (renamed from
   ageMs, CATCH A) = age of the measurement that produced the printed numbers, never the age of the read.

## ⚠️ LANDMINE — buildChatRequestBody CONSUMES the staged turn state. NEVER rebuild a body to retry a turn.
   buildChatRequestBody (EchoJayAPI.cpp) CONSUMES AND CLEARS the staged per-turn state as it assembles:
     - nextChatMeters_   cleared at :1256   (the [METER SNAPSHOT] / measured block)
     - nextChatTurnType_ cleared at :1288
     - nextChatMapFps_   cleared at :1298   (the dialable-map fingerprints)
   Therefore ANY re-send that REBUILDS the body (e.g. calling sendChat again for the same turn) sends a
   DIFFERENT request under the SAME turnId - silently STRIPPED of the measured block, the turn type, and mapFps.
   The user would get a chain built on NO measurements with nothing indicating anything was missing: the exact
   defect this week was spent eliminating, reintroduced through a convenience path.
   RULE: any future retry, resend, or "just try it again on the other endpoint" MUST re-POST the CAPTURED body
   (the exact bytes already assembled), never rebuild it. If you cannot capture the body, do not auto-retry -
   surface a plain message and let the user resend by hand (one keystroke).
   This is the SECOND time this week a "small convenience" would have reintroduced a measured-data defect
   (cf. the pre-gain wrong-source bug). Treat body rebuild-to-retry as a defect by construction.

## FINDING 1 as SHIPPED (15 Sep 2026): suppression + plain message + full observation log. The turn does NOT self-complete - deliberate.
   On /api/chat-stream 403 code=chat_turn_not_streamed (a routing directive, not a chat reply): the raw `error`
   string is SUPPRESSED (logged only via "EJStream: REROUTE-OBSERVED origin=/api/chat-stream status=403
   code=... turnId=... serverError=..."), never rendered as a bubble or stored in history; a PLAIN message is
   shown ("sent to the streaming path... please send it again"); NO automatic re-send (see the landmine above).
   Fires ONLY on that code; every other non-2xx keeps the existing generic error path. Option A (bounded
   auto-re-send with an identical CAPTURED body) was considered and REJECTED: it needs a sendChat refactor
   days before a shoot to automate recovery from a condition whose only known trigger (a stale stream_chains
   flag) is already deleted - risk with no matching return.

## ⚠️ THIRD INSTANCE of "verified the wrong object" - the gain-path assertions (a)-(d) passed while the pre-gain is NOT applied.
   Sean's rack gain reads 0.0 dB; the reply stated +3.7 dB. The pre-gain is STATED but NOT APPLIED - exactly what Ruling 1
   was written to prevent. The harness gain-path assertions (a)-(d) PASSED anyway because they checked
   applied == stated == clamp(-18 - integrated) WITHIN THE SAME OBJECT (pendingLinkPreGain_ / the object the code wrote
   to). They NEVER checked that that object is the one in the AUDIO PATH. Same blind-spot class as (1) the unreachable
   call site emitting nothing while the harness passed, and (2) the permanently-healthy seeded frame making the
   required-set check unfalsifiable. THIRD time a guard verified the object the code touched instead of the object that
   matters. RULE (to be enforced before any fix): "applied" means READ BACK FROM THE OBJECT IN THE AUDIO PATH, never
   from the object the code wrote to - exactly the principle the EQ readback instrument already uses.

## DEFECT DIAGNOSED (15 Sep 2026): LINK PRE-GAIN IS NEVER ROUTED TO THE LINK. Stated in the reply, applied nowhere.
   Q1 WHERE applyPendingLinkPreGain WRITES: the MAIN plugin's ChainHost pre-gain -
   processorRef.getChainHost().setPreGainDb(pendingLinkPreGain_.db, false) (PluginEditor.cpp:29178). That is NEITHER
   the Link's rack preGainDb (LinkShm.h:1152 - the 0.0 dB knob Sean sees) NOR in the Link channel's audio path.
   Q2 DID IT RUN: NO. applyPendingLinkPreGain is called at PluginEditor.cpp:31086, which is INSIDE loadChainFromJson
   (starts 30904) - the OWN-CHANNEL build path. The router (2500-2505) sends a Link build to sendChainToLink and
   RETURNS; it never enters loadChainFromJson. Confirmed by the log: NO EJPreGain line for the build. pendingLinkPreGain_
   is computed at compose, stated in the reply, and left UNCONSUMED.
   Q3 SAME OBJECT BOTH DIRECTIONS: NO. measuredContextTargetLink stores pendingLinkPreGain_ keyed by targetLinkUid (the
   Link's reading). The apply (unreached) would write the MAIN's ChainHost ignoring the uid. And sendChainToLink's
   chain-cmd payload carries NO pre-gain field (LinkProcessor rack sidecar HAS preGainDb, but the build never sets it).
   So the Link's rack pre-gain is written by NO path.
   Q4 AUDIO PATH: the LINK's own instance processBlock carries the Link channel's audio; the MAIN's ChainHost carries
   the main channel's. The main's pre-gain, even if applied, is not in the Link channel's path.
   ROOT CAUSE: the pre-gain apply site sits on the own-channel path only; a Link build has no route to carry it to the
   Link's rack. THE FIX (not yet built) routes the computed pre-gain to the Link's rack preGainDb via the chain-cmd,
   not the main's ChainHost.

## SYMPTOM 2 (Link setting change does not change audio) - SEPARATE from the pre-gain; bypass suspicion KILLED by the log.
   The build's chain DID reach and apply on the Link: EJDialSummary slot 0 EchoJay EQ settings_structured=y requested=4
   applied=4 status=applied, on the Link process ("EchoJay on Mix Bus") - the settings are on the Link's rack (this also
   confirms the EQ 4/4 fix in-host). The v9 lease bypass RESTORES correctly: "RACK engaged, 6 slot(s) bypassed, streaming
   dry" -> "RACK released/expired - 6 slot bypass state(s) restored". So the reviewer's #3 (built chain left bypassed) is
   NOT the cause on this evidence. Symptom 2's mechanism is NOT the same as the pre-gain's and is NOT yet nailed - needs
   a hand-edit (ctrl-cmd) apply trace and the Link's actual processing/monitoring routing. TWO DEFECTS, not one.

## BYPASS re-examined on the RIGHT evidence (15 Sep) - the sidecar publishes the EFFECTIVE bypass; the rack IS active.
   Caught: "restored to bypassed IS still bypassed" - correct in principle. But the Link rack sidecar
   (rack-7f3595d7d6.json) RIGHT NOW: all 6 slots bypassed=False; preGainDb=0.0. publishRackSidecar serializes
   s.bypassed from chainHost.getAllSlotInfos() (LinkProcessor.cpp:347) = the EFFECTIVE processing bypass (s.bypassed =
   info.bypassed, :1953), NOT the intended. A newly built slot's intended bypass is FALSE/active unless the plan says
   bypass (:2038), and the lease restores each slot to its intendedBypassed (:1994). So: built slots are active, the
   lease restored them to active, and the sidecar's EFFECTIVE state confirms active. THE RACK IS PROCESSING. The bypass
   hypothesis is killed - this time on the effective published state, not on "the mechanism ran". Q4: if Sean's screen
   shows a slot's B engaged while the sidecar says bypassed=False, that is a UI-vs-published mismatch worth naming, but
   the PUBLISHED effective state is active for all six.
   SO SYMPTOM 2 IS NOT BYPASS AND NOT THE PRE-GAIN. With the rack active + the build's settings applied (EJDialSummary
   applied=4), a hand-EDIT of a slot parameter not changing audio points at the EDIT TRANSPORT: the ctrl-cmd vocabulary
   the shipping Link accepts is active/gainDb/placement/preGainDb/mute/solo/preGainReset/pullSlotState - NO per-slot
   PARAM-SET. A hand-dial of a Link slot's parameter has no ctrl-cmd to carry it; whether it reaches the Link at all is
   the next thread (not yet traced). Distinct from symptoms 1 and the bypass.

## PRE-GAIN FIX IS V2-ONLY - NO LINK TRIP. The shipping Link ALREADY understands a preGainDb ctrl-cmd.
   LinkProcessor.cpp:76-84: on ctrl-cmd with "preGainDb", the Link calls chainHost.setPreGainDb(g, userSet) on ITS OWN
   ChainHost - the pre-gain in the Link's audio path, the knob Sean sees (added 18 Aug 2026 for the mixer's Pre mode).
   So the fix: on a Link build, instead of applyPendingLinkPreGain writing the MAIN's ChainHost, send a ctrl-cmd
   {preGainDb: pendingLinkPreGain_.db, preGainUserSet: false} to ctrl-cmd-<uid>.json. V2-only; the currently-shipping
   Link consumes it unchanged. No Link rebuild, no re-sign.

## PRE-GAIN FIX WRITTEN + VERIFIED (16 Sep 2026), V2-only. Send half RED->GREEN on the bench; end-to-end is in-host.
   applyPendingLinkPreGain now sends ctrl-cmd {preGainDb: <the one clamped value>, preGainUserSet: false} to
   ctrl-cmd-<uid>.json for the SAME uid the reading came from (the shipping Link consumes it into ITS OWN ChainHost,
   LinkProcessor.cpp:76-84 - the audio path, Sean's knob). Called at the Build site right after sendChainToLink, only
   when pendingLinkPreGain_.uid == the build uid. The wrong main-ChainHost write is REMOVED from applyPendingLinkPreGain
   AND its fallback removed from loadChainFromJson (own channel keeps computePreGainAtBuild untouched). Ruling 1 held:
   one computation, one clamp, one number - the value sent == the printed value.
   VERIFICATION: tools/pregain_readback (SEND half, main lib) - GREEN: ctrl-cmd written with preGainDb == stated (+2.0),
   preGainUserSet false, the main's OWN ChainHost pre-gain UNTOUCHED (0.0), consumed once. RED on the old body (revert):
   ctrl-cmd not written, main ChainHost = 2.0 (the pre-gain went to the wrong object) - 4 assertions fail. Both shown.
   LIMIT, STATED: the end-to-end sidecar readback is NOT bench-reachable - LinkProcessor is the EchoJayLink target only,
   and main+Link are separate plugins with overlapping shared code (one binary cannot host both real instances). The
   Link's preGainDb CONSUME is shipping/unchanged (mixer Pre mode). So the send half is bench-proven; the sidecar
   readback is IN-HOST (Sean sees the knob move to +N and the level hold). Plugin compiles clean with the fix.

## SYMPTOM 2 (Link setting change does not change audio) - TRACE, cause NOT yet confirmed; needs an in-host knob trace.
   Editing a Link rack on the main is a BORROW: sendChainToLink lands a build/edit in the BORROWED HOST (a local
   session copy in the MAIN's process), NOT on the Link, while a borrow is active - "the Link refuses chain-cmds while
   leased" (PluginEditor.cpp:28356). During the borrow the Link streams DRY (its rack bypassed, EJLease). Edits reach
   the Link's audio only on COMMIT/deselect (state pushed back, lease released, rack un-bypassed). The build DID reach
   the Link (EJDialSummary applied=4, sidecar active) - so on this run the commit worked. LinkProcessor::processBlock
   runs the chainHost rack unless empty/all-bypassed (:1660), and the sidecar shows all 6 active, so the rack IS in
   circuit. The ctrl-cmd vocabulary has NO per-slot live PARAM verb - there is no live-edit transport; edits ride the
   borrow-commit cycle. WHAT IS NOT CONFIRMED: whether a knob turned DURING a borrow (a) is simply dry until deselect
   (expected), or (b) is lost on commit, or (c) the commit does not push back. That needs Sean's in-host trace: turn a
   knob, deselect, does the audio change? is a chain-cmd/ctrl-cmd written? does the Link log receiving it? I did NOT
   propose a fix. OPTIONS if it is a transport gap: re-send the full chain via chain-cmd on commit (V2-only, existing
   verb, but blocked while leased so only on release) vs. add a per-slot live PARAM ctrl-cmd verb to the Link (a Link
   change = two-plugin signing trip). Cost-out favours the V2-only commit path IF the failure is in the commit.

## BUILD NOTE (16 Sep 2026) - Link pre-gain verification boundary. The SEND is bench-proven; the sidecar LANDING is in-host only.
   The object in the audio path (the Link's rack preGainDb) lives in the LINK's process. One harness binary cannot host
   both a real main and a real Link (LinkProcessor is the EchoJayLink target; the two plugins share overlapping code and
   would double-define). So the bench proves the main SENDS the correct ctrl-cmd (tools/pregain_readback, RED->GREEN);
   the Link CONSUMING it into its sidecar/audio is verified in-host (Sean sees the knob move and the level hold) and
   rests on the shipping, unchanged Link preGainDb path (mixer Pre mode). State this wherever the pre-gain fix is described.

## SYMPTOM 2 REOPENED - OPEN / INTERMITTENT (16 Sep 2026). Mechanism NOT established. Borrow/lease is a CANDIDATE, not the cause.
   Observation A (~earlier, approx 10:0x-10:4x per the build logs): edited a Link rack, settings applied (EJDialSummary
   applied=4), NO audible change.
   Observation B (~16 Sep, ~20 min after A per Sean): edited the SAME Link rack, SAME build/session, heard it change
   LIVE while editing.
   Same build, same rack, same session -> INTERMITTENT. Intermittent is worse than a consistent fault: it can appear
   on camera and cannot be reproduced on demand. DO NOT write the borrow-streams-dry explanation as the cause; it is
   ONE candidate. Candidates on the table: (1) lease window - A was inside a build's lease (Link streaming dry), B was
   after expiry; if so the real behaviour is "for a window after a build, rack edits are inaudible" - exactly the
   build-then-tweak sequence Sean does on camera. (2) pre-ready interactivity - an edit made before the rack goes
   orange/ready lands on an object the borrow then overwrites (Sean's ITEM 1). (3) per-slot mix knobs inert on a Link
   rack (Sean's ITEM 2). To confirm/kill from the LOG, not by reasoning: lease acquire/release timestamps today, lease
   duration + what releases it, and whether A falls inside a lease window and B outside. If A was NOT inside a lease,
   the lease candidate is dead and it is unexplained intermittency - to be said plainly, not patched with a 2nd theory.

## SYMPTOM 2 INVESTIGATION (16 Sep) - lease timeline + ITEM 1/2 traced. Cause STILL NOT established; multiple live candidates.
   LEASE/BORROW timeline (log, today): 10:54:30 RackLock acquired uid=7f3595d7d6; 10:54:33 Borrow engaged (ringSlot 37);
   10:55:03 switch to uid=a690308f4f (6 parked released, engaged); 10:55:07 back to 7f3595d7d6; 10:55:10 engaged. Rapid
   rack switching, each a borrow-engage. A borrow lasts until DESELECT/switch; the lock renews every kLeaseRenewMs=1000ms
   (LinkShm.h:1643) and releases on deselect/close. DURING a borrow the Link streams DRY. I do NOT have Sean's two
   observation timestamps, so I CANNOT place A inside a lease and B outside - the lease-window candidate is PLAUSIBLE but
   UNCONFIRMED. Note: Observation B was "live WHILE editing"; if editing == borrow active == streaming dry, B should have
   been dry too - which points at a MONITORING/solo factor (LISTEN/in-context) for B, or B being outside a borrow. Not resolved.
   ITEM 1 (interactive before ready/orange): the chain panel is writeLocked/greyed while rackLockState != Held (Idle=
   acquiring, HeldByOther, WaitRecency) - PluginEditor.cpp:7591-7608; it goes writable (ready/orange) only once WE hold
   the lock. But that gate is on the CHAIN PANEL's slot writes, not necessarily on opening the plugin's own window.
   a) a pre-ready edit's destination is NOT fully established from code - needs the openSlot/pull order + an in-host repro.
   b) PLAUSIBLE for Observation A (edit before ready -> pull overwrites it -> applied, no audio) but UNCONFIRMED; do not
   assert it. c) size: gate interactivity on rackLockState==Held (the writeLocked machinery already exists for the panel;
   extend to plugin-window open + slot knobs) - V2-only, likely small, low risk. Number pending a closer read.
   ITEM 2 (per-slot wet knob on a Link rack): handler EXISTS (PluginEditor.cpp:2090) - borrowed rack -> bh->setSlotWet
   (writes to the borrowed host); a NON-borrowed remote Link -> return (knob HIDDEN "in remote mode"); own rack ->
   getChainHost().setSlotWet immediately (audible). a) so it is NOT inert; it is hidden/blocked in the remote/pre-ready
   phase and, once borrowed, writes to bh but is INAUDIBLE during the borrow (streaming dry). b) per-slot wet already
   travels to the Link via chain-cmd on COMMIT (the chain array carries per-slot wet, :25636) - V2-only, no Link change;
   masterWet(sidecar)/gainDb(ctrl-cmd) exist but a LIVE per-slot mix verb does not. c) OWN rack works immediately, Link
   rack is deferred(bh)/hidden(remote) - that difference IS the diagnosis (the borrow model), same root as symptom 2.
   NO FIX WRITTEN for either. Sizes pending the reviewer's decision; both are V2-only paths on the evidence so far.
