- **Item 1 finding (the map-builder step): the maps' named controls are NOT built in the plugin.** The client's ejextract writes the extractor sample (params with `discrete` and `num_steps` and the per-step texts — CLA-76 Analog and the Manley knobs already carry them); the served map's `controls` section (kind anchored/mode, `trust: setread`) comes from the **ejmap tool** (/Applications/ejmap.app, its own repo with docs/SERVER_CONTRACT.md — not on this Mac as source) through `POST /api/params/ejmap`, and the server's `ingestControls` (lib/params-lib.js:1953) accepts ONLY `mode` (labels) and `anchored` (anchors) — a `position` kind is rejected as "unknown control kind". So the step has two halves: (i) the ejmap tool emits `kind "position", steps, positions (panel texts), unit "position"` for every discrete control with 2–23 steps and leaves continuous anchored controls alone; (ii) the server ingest accepts and stores that kind (lookup already forwards it on server/2026-09-21-positions). The "guard RED on current tree" belongs to whichever repo holds the builder; the ingest half can be built and legged here. Decision needed: build the ingest half now (server), and where the emitting half is built (ejmap repo).
- **Item 6 (Manley, observation):** the client's log carries only the summary — `EJDialSummary: slot 3 ("UAD Manley Massive Passive MST") … keys=[controls{Ch1HiMidGain, Ch1HiMidFreq, Ch1HiMidBW, Ch1HiGain, Ch1HiFreq, Ch1HiBW, Ch1LoEnable, Ch1GAIN}] requested=8 applied=5 manual=3 status=partial` (10:10:57) and three events.jsonl rows `reason "partial", manual ["Ch1HiMidGain"] / ["Ch1HiMidBW"] / ["Ch1HiGain"]`; the asked values and the per-control notes are on the chain card only (not logged; the 10:10 server rows are outside the searchable log window). Map entries: Ch1HiMidGain index 12 anchored [0, 11] dB, Ch1HiMidBW index 13 anchored [1, 3] no unit, Ch1HiGain index 17 anchored [0, 11] dB — all 16 anchors, `trust setread`; the extractor sample says all three are **discrete, 16 steps** with texts "0.00 dB … 11.00 dB" / "1.00 … 3.00" (Ch1HiMidFreq, which applied, is 11 steps 220–10000 Hz). The live names match the map exactly (probe). Branch that produces "manual" (by elimination, not observed): the anchored write interpolates a norm between two of the 16 detents, the settled read-back on a discrete parameter snaps to a detent whose text differs from the asked value, and `settleVerify` restores the pre-write value with the note "write did not stick (immediate read …, settled read …), value restored" (EchoJayParamApply.h:1070) — the control lands in the hand-dial list (ChainHost.cpp:1664 renders "needs hand-dialing"). Ch1HiMidFreq applied because its ask sat on a detent. **Proposed fix (one line):** item 1's rule — a discrete 2–23-step control becomes `kind position` with its panel texts, and the client lands the nearest detent by text/number (EchoJayParamApply.h's position kind) — which covers these three and CLA-76 Analog alike; no map edit by hand.

## 22 Sep 2026 — 21m OBSERVATIONS (no code)
- **1. Links off by default.** What V2's per-Link on/off means: `snap.active` gates ONLY the audio ring (capture/meter feed) — PluginProcessor.cpp:5273–5301 connects/disconnects the ring on it; chains, remote control, the roster row and the links body (buildClassifyLinks filters on uid/name only) do not depend on it. Default for a Link V2 has not seen before: the registry row itself — `LinkShm::claimSlot` sets `activeFlag = 1` on every fresh claim (LinkShm.h:937 "default Active; setSlotActive() refines"), V2's `LinkDisplayEntry.active` defaults true (LinkShm.h:1009) and reads the row's flag (LinkShm.h:1026 → PluginProcessor.cpp:5303). The Link's own flag: `LinkProcessor::linkOn` (LinkProcessor.h:60, default true), PERSISTED in the Link's state ("linkOn", getState 2743 / setState 2808), published to the registry by `updateShmState()` (LinkProcessor.cpp:1572 `setSlotActive(regMap, regSlotIdx, on)`), flipped by V2's remote command (LinkProcessor.cpp:806–813, which logs "remote set active" and republishes only when the value CHANGES). So V2 does read the Link's flag, through the registry row. Why they disagree on first sight (inference — the diag lines that would prove it, `EJLinkState: post-init/setState linkOn=…`, print only with ECHOJAY_LINK_STATE_DIAG and none appear in today's log before 11:00; the 10:52 lines are saves, all linkOn=1): the roster tick is drawn from BOTH `connected` and `active` (`drawActiveTick(g, …, connected, active, …)`, PluginEditor.cpp:9947–9950) and `connected = snap.active && the ring is mapped` — a Link whose ring V2 has not bound yet (no audio published, or before V2's poll binds it) paints as off with the tooltip "not connected", while the Link's editor tick reads `linkOn` = ticked; the first click sends `!active`, i.e. turns an active Link OFF, and the second turns it on again — "had to click each on". A second candidate: a Link whose `setStateInformation` restored `linkOn` before its registry slot existed publishes nothing until the next change (`setSlotActive` needs `regSlotIdx ≥ 0`); the claim then sets the row to 1 anyway, so this one reads on. **For the ruling (Sean's rule, what it needs):** the authority already IS the Link's `linkOn` and V2 already mirrors it, so the rule needs (a) the roster tick to paint `active` alone (a separate lamp for "audio flowing"), (b) a Link reporting active to be on in V2 with no click — true today once the row is read; (c) the click writes over the chain-command transport — true today (`sendLinkActiveCommand`, PluginEditor.cpp:29325 → the Link's remote handler). Plus ECHOJAY_LINK_STATE_DIAG on in 21m with V2-side poll lines (uid, snap.active, connected) so the next report is observed, not inferred.
- **4. Gain staging.** Tallies today: `chainInTally_` (raw chain input, before the pre-gain), `chainOutTally_` (chain output, post master wet, pre bus trim) — ChainHost.h:2186–2187; and per ACTIVE slot, inside its `SlotWetBlend` graph node (ChainHost.cpp:643): `inTally_` on the dry tap (graph inputs 2/3) and `outTally_` on the plugin's output (inputs 0/1), measured BEFORE the wet/dry blend and before the fully-wet early-out, exposed as `ChainHost::getSlotLevels(i)` → `{in, out, measured}` (ChainHost.h:1166/1174; a bypassed slot has no node and is not measured). Window (EchoJayLevelTally.h): K-weighted, 100 ms hop, 400 ms momentary (4 hops), short-term = the BS.1770 3 s window (30 hops, no gate) with `maxShortTermDb` held since `resetShortTermMax()`, integrated gated −70 abs / −10 rel, `known` after the heard floor. Per-slot output trim: NONE exists — the only gains are the chain pre-gain (ChainHost.h:1176–1192, before slot 1, user-owned, session-persisted) and the Level slot; nothing per slot. **What the rule needs:** in ChainHost a per-slot `std::atomic<float> trimDb` beside the slot's `wet` atomic (the SlotWetBlend already takes `wet` by shared_ptr), applied inside `SlotWetBlend::processBlock` to channels 0/1 (the plugin's output) BEFORE `outTally_.push` and before the blend — so the out tally, the wet blend and every downstream slot see the trimmed level (the rule's "slot wet blends measured after the trim"); a per-slot `keepLevel` flag; at Listen/Check the loop (or ChainHost on the loop's request) computes `out.shortTermDb − in.shortTermDb` per measured slot except the Level slot and the last limiter, sets `trimDb = −(out−in)` clamped ±12 unless kept, re-measured every Listen/Check with the tallies reset for the window; tile text "−2.3 dB match" and the chain card line from the same atomic; persisted with the session like the pre-gain (not carried by a shared chain); the loop's opening gain then assumes a unity chain (the arm estimate uses chain IN, no per-slot compensation). Cost: one multiply per sample per slot on the wet leg.
- **6. PTSL.** SDK presence: NOT on this Mac — no ptsl.proto / gRPC stubs anywhere (mdfind); only the AAX SDK 2.9.0 docs mention it. The server side IS here: Pro Tools 25.12.1.133 ships `Contents/Frameworks/libPTSL.dylib` (24.4 MB, 23 Jan 2026) with 294 command ids. Plugin process: Avid's own AAX guide (aax-sdk-2-9-0 AAX_Pro_Tools_Guide.doxygen §"Using the Pro Tools Scripting SDK with AAX") says an AAX plug-in MAY open a PTSL connection, on a non-main thread — the AAX runs inside the Pro Tools process, so it is a loopback gRPC client. Version: PTSL exists since Pro Tools 2022.12; 25.12.1 is far above the minimum. Licence / prompt: this build carries `RegisterConnection` and `AuthorizeConnection` (an authorisation step beyond registration); what it asks for (a developer key, a user consent dialog) cannot be verified without the SDK. Capability by point: (a) create an aux track — `CreateNewTracks` with `TrackType TT_Aux` exists in this build: YES; (b) route the members' outputs or sends to a new bus — NO track-output / send / routing command id exists in this build (the only path-shaped ids are `CreateSignalPath`, `GetMonitorOutputPath`, `GetSessionPath`; no SetTrackOutputPath / send commands); (c) insert EchoJay Link on it — NO insert-plug-in command exists (the only "PlugIn" id is `EnableCueProVideoPlugIn`). So PTSL in 25.12.1 gets the aux, not the routing or the insert; those stay manual (or a Link the user drops on the aux).
- **Queued for 21m, no observation needed (acknowledged):** rename alias (roster right-click, alias in V2 session state, shown everywhere V2 names the Link, "Reset name"); per-rack Undo/Redo (20 deep, Cmd-Z + button; loop Undo unchanged; borrowed-rack undo through the transport); EchoJay Limiter Threshold READOUT = ceiling − input gain (display only). **Contract (both sides build to it):** `CONTRACT_GROUPS_2026-09-22.md`. Request bodies (chat, chat-stream, classify) gain `groups[] {id, name, members: [instanceId], bus: instanceId|null}`; `links[]` gains `channelType`, and `instanceId` is required on a groups request. Link identity is the instanceId everywhere; the name is display only. Chain block gains `target {groupId, mode: "bus", linkId} | {groupId, mode: "each", linkIds}`. Gain block stays absolute per Link, `linkId` = instanceId on a groups request (+ `name` for display). `[GROUPS - N] … [END GROUPS]` inside LINK LEVELS; `GROUPS:` facts line after `LINKS:`; mixed-with-no-bus = ONE question, no block; empty group = decline, no block; caps 12 groups / 16 members. Everything gated on `groups` being present: a request without it is byte-identical.

**Server half (this branch):**
- `api/_groups.js` (new): ingest + guards (name collision refused `[group-name-collision]`, duplicates, unknown members `[group-member-unknown]`, unknown bus, caps `[group-cap]`, empty `[group-empty]`); presence from the capture-noise absent/silent names; a Link's type = `channelType` else its name through the alias table; group type (bus → the bus Link's; else members' shared type; else mixed); resolver group > Link (> channel alias, the caller's tier); the request a target is read from (answer / tap / bare "build" turns inherit the request, `[group-resolve-inherited]`); level words + delta (number wins; direction 2 dB; a bit 1; way 4); the offset maths with the clamp rule (largest delta that keeps every member in -24..+12, the limiting member named, applied 0 = no block); `[GROUPS]` render + rules paragraph; `factsLine`; the reply passes (chain block `target` + result prefix "Built on each of the N X Links." / "Built on <bus>."; the server-written gain block on a chat level turn, replacing any model block; a model block's name-keyed `linkId` rewritten to the instanceId when unique, dropped otherwise `[gain-linkid-normalised]`; a block that slips through on mixed/empty removed); the mixed / empty notes.
- `api/_links.js`: parseLinks keeps `channelType`; buildLinksContext takes the groups block (byte-identical without it).
- `api/_chain-briefs.js`: resolveChannel takes `override` (a named group's / Link's type wins over the typed alias and the surface; source "group" / "link").
- `api/_chat-assembly.js`: deriveTurnContext parses groups (v2 + `groups` present); assemblePrompt resolves the turn, renders the block, briefs on the group's type, injects the one-question / not-live note (briefAskTurn true, brief skipped) on mixed / empty, returns `groupTurn`.
- `api/classify.js` + `api/_classify-prompt.js`: `GROUPS:` fact (only when sent); CHANNEL fact = the group's type on a build verb (`[group-channel-fact]`) so channel_mismatch is judged against it; the level clamp to chat (`[group-level-clamp]`); response keys `targetGroup` / `groupLevel` / `groupLevelClamped` only when the request carried groups; prompt v24 = one GROUPS rule; the loop-verb return still precedes everything.
- `api/chat.js`, `api/chat-stream.js`: `applyGroupReplyPasses` after the chain post-passes on both; chat-stream's runStream takes `groupTurn`.
- `api/_scrub.js`: `[GROUPS - …] … [END GROUPS]` scrubbed from replies.

## 22 Sep 2026 — 21m round, OBSERVATION 4: the CLA-76 slot wet after "add a CLA-76, ratio 8, analog off"
- **Sources of a slot's wet, in order (code):** (1) the op's own `wet_pct` — ChainHost.cpp:1551 parses it onto the op, and the add/replace completion applies it at ChainHost.cpp:2187–2190 ("wet_pct riding an add/replace: EchoJay's own blend on the slot that just loaded; absent leaves the default (1.0)", `setSlotWet(slotIdx, theOp.wetPct / 100.0f, WetSource::Assistant)`); (2) the client default — `ChainSlot::wet = 1.0f` (ChainHost.h:57 and 1864): a slot with no `wet_pct` on its op is 100 % wet; (3) a later `set_wet` op (ChainHost.cpp:2012); (4) the server's degree pass (`[degree-wet]`, rule degree_slot_wet) which only FILLS a value when a degree word was typed.
- **This turn (09:21:48, chain_edit):** the server logged `[degree-wet] {"degree":null,"kind":"unqualified","band":[60,80],"action":"CHECKED","filled":[],"clamped":[]}` — no degree word, nothing filled or clamped — and `[slot-wet] {"rule":"reverb_delay_slot_wet","action":"CHECKED","slots":0}`; `[chain-settings-edit]` emitted only the five band controls. So the server wrote no wet. The client's edit lines (`EJEdit: … wet=`) are not in the unified log for that minute (V2's edit logging is not captured there; only EJDial/EJDialSummary/EJPane lines are), and the raw model reply is not captured. **By elimination the value below 100 came from the op itself: the model wrote a `wet_pct` on the add op (the degree pass leaves an unqualified turn's value as written), and the client applied it at ChainHost.cpp:2187–2190.** Not observed directly — the raw reply would settle it.
- **Rule for the ruling (Sean's):** a compressor or EQ added with no degree word is 100 % wet. Where it would live: the server's degree pass (strip/clamp a model-written `wet_pct` on a compressor/eq add when the turn is unqualified) and, belt-and-braces, the client (ignore `wet_pct` on an add of a compressor/eq slot when the op carries no degree marker).

### Ruling 2 — gain staging (860ff9e)
- Per-slot trim atomic beside the wet atomic; SlotWetBlend applies it to the plugin's output BEFORE the out
  tally and the blend. `ChainHost::measureUnityTrims` at every Listen/Check window close: every slot except the
  Level slot and the last limiter (and bypassed / kept slots) → trim = −(out − in) over the short-term window,
  clamped ±12 dB. The proposal is computed from the unity chain (window measured + the trims just applied).
- Tile text "−2.3 dB match" / "level kept" and the card line read the same atomic (SlotInfo.trimText).
- Persisted like the pre-gain: trimDb / keepLevel in buildChainSlotsVar, applied on restore beside the saved wet.
- Keep flag: right-click on a slot tile → "Keep this plugin's level". LOCAL RACK ONLY — there is no keep-level
  op over the chain-command transport; on a borrowed or held remote rack the toggle does nothing. FLAGGED:
  the ruling said "ChainHost → both bundles" (done: the trim runs inside whichever ChainHost hosts the rack)
  but did not say whether the keep flag needs a transport op; needs a ruling.
- Found while wiring: the graph rebuild created a FRESH trim atomic on every rebuild, so the blend node would
  have read the old one. Fixed (one atomic per slot for the slot's life) before any guard ran.
- Guard (loudness_loop_guard P legs, GREEN, scribble GREEN, green21m_loud11.log): P0 +4 dB Gain between Level
  and limiter, no trim before Listen; P1 after Listen trim −4.00, slot ends −0.00 (out−in), tile/card
  "−4.0 dB match" from the same atomic; P2 Level and limiter exempt (0.00 / 0.00), log line carried; P3 keep
  flag holds ("level kept", slot still +4.00, log "kept (4.0 dB)"); P4 trim AND keep persist across
  save/reopen (buildChainSlotsVar → restoreSavedChain); P5 proposal computed from the unity chain:
  "measured −18.0 LUFS … needed +9.0 dB" (as it stood: −14.0 / +5.0); after Go the Level moves +6.00 (the
  existing per-pass clamp), chain out −12.0 printed, not asserted.

### Undo/Redo (9b3b006)
- ChainHost stack per rack, 20 deep; snapshot = the session-save pair (slots var + state var + params var);
  pushed BEFORE add / remove / move / bypass / wet (one step per knob gesture, 1.5 s coalesce) / keep-level and
  ONE step per applyChainEdits batch; undo/redo restore through restoreSavedChain; a restore pushes nothing.
- Transport: a single {"op":"undo"|"redo"} is answered by the RECEIVING rack's own stack in applyChainEdits
  before the base-slot guards. Borrowed rack: local copy undoes + pushes the op (borrowPushStructuralEdit;
  the borrow bookkeeping follows the new slot count). Held remote rack: sendRackUndo (same lock/connected
  refusals as sendRackEdit). Editor: Cmd-Z / Cmd-Shift-Z on the Chain tab, ↶ ↷ buttons after the add block.
  Loop Undo pill untouched.
- Guard (level_slot_guard U0–U8, GREEN, scribble GREEN, green21m_level3.log): depth 20; two adds = two
  steps; bypass a step; undo restores the un-bypassed rack (2 slots, undo=2 redo=1); second undo removes the
  Gain; redo puts it back; a new edit clears redo; a batch of two ops is ONE step and one undo reverts both;
  {"op":"undo"} through applyChainEdits answered "undo: bypass EchoJay Level" depth 4→3; empty stack
  answers "nothing to redo" as a refused op; three wet writes in 1.5 s = one step, undo returns the
  pre-gesture wet; a restore pushes no step; 25 toggles → depth 20.
- FLAGGED: the harness's second EchoJayProcessor on main's stack overflowed it (SIGSEGV in chkstk); the extra
  processors in the U and P legs are heap-allocated. Not a product defect.

## 22 Sep 2026 — 21n OBSERVATION 1d: why there is no "PuigChild 660 (s)" (no code; scanner cache + AU registry, read-only)
**Verdict: NOT a scanner bug, and not a general absence of Waves stereo variants on this Mac.**

  `PuigTec MEQ5 (m)` PM5M · `PuigTec MEQ5 (s)` PM5S. So the 660 registers ONE build (mono) and the 670 registers ONE

  The EQP1A and MEQ5 beside them register BOTH, which is what a missing-variant bug would have to explain away.

  IR-L, IR1, LFE360, PRS Archon, PRS Dallas, PRS V9, **PuigChild 660**, Q-Capture, UltraPitch 3 Voices, UltraPitch 6

## 22 Sep 2026 — 21n round BUILT (commits c4c99f3 0e4ad50 0377519 7951bf8 9fc06f7 d66efd5 bf684c0), suite 38/38, ship_2026-09-21n packaged unsigned
Order as ruled: item 2 first, then 1a / 1b / 1c, then item 3, then item 4.

### Item 2 — alias mirror (c4c99f3)
V2's alias is pushed to the Link over the ctrl-cmd transport as an additive "alias" field ("" clears); the Link stores it
in `displayAlias`, shows "aka <alias>" beside its name field, echoes it in the ctrl-ack and saves it in its own state.
`effectiveDisplayName()` - the registry row, the identity every surface keys on - never reads it. Guard
alias_mirror_guard: two processes (a real LinkProcessor + LinkEditor against the Link archive, a real EchoJayProcessor
against the V2 archive), GREEN. RED on the pre-21n tree: link side compile refusal, V2 side BEHAVIOURAL (setLinkAlias
wrote no ctrl-cmd). KNOWN LIMIT, stated: the one-file ctrl-cmd transport means an alias push in the same second as a
mixer Active/gain command overwrites it (the ctrl seq is epoch seconds).

### Ruling 1a — one sender (0e4ad50)
`writeChainEditCommand` resolves the lease id itself from the borrow session or the edit session when the caller passes
none. lease_id_guard (two processes: V2 leases the rack, then sends the chat-card's exact call with NO lease id) GREEN;
BEHAVIOURAL RED on the pre-21n tree - ack "failed", 6 slots unchanged.

### Ruling 1b — channelWidth per target (0377519)
`LinkSlotInfo.channels` from the registry row; `chatTargetChannelWidth()` = the target Link's width, V2's own only on the
local rack, 0 (unknown) leaves the field off. ui_guard (11) GREEN, compile-refusal RED. OBSERVED: the Kick bus Link's
ring header reads numChannels 2, so the 18:46 body's "2" was V2's own width and coincidentally right.

### Ruling 1c — registration names in the feed (7951bf8)
A family is never collapsed onto a bare stem; sibling variants each get their own row. substitute_guard F1-F3 GREEN,
BEHAVIOURAL RED (the pre-21n tree offers "PuigChild 660" and "CLA-76").

### Item 3 — one plugin-wide undo history (9fc06f7)
50 deep, entries as data, targets resolved by identity at apply time, skipped-with-a-line when absent. Kinds: chain,
wet / trim / keep, dial, gesture (300 ms coalesce, from a lock-free ring the parameter listener fills), linkActive /
linkGain, alias, loop. Not recorded: chat, captures, session load. Cmd-Z / Shift-Cmd-Z anywhere outside a text field,
plus header buttons; the chain panel's buttons follow the same history. A held remote rack still answers {"op":"undo"}
from its own stack. global_undo_guard GREEN (11 classes), compile-refusal RED.
FOUND while building: a dial entry that only knew JUCE parameters recorded nothing for an EchoJay built-in, whose
controls live in its ParamSchema; the snapshot now carries both vocabularies ("p:<id>" for a built-in).

### Item 4 — link groups, client half (d66efd5)
Groups persist in the V2 state; groups[] + links[] ride every chat / chat-stream / classify body while groups exist and
NEITHER is sent when there are none; channelWidth now goes as the contract's string ("mono" | "stereo"). A roster group
row carries the level offset: each present member's trim moves by the largest |delta| that keeps every member inside
-24..+12 (the bus alone when set), and the row names the limiting member. A chain block's target {bus} / {each} builds
accordingly, card line "Built on each of the N Links.", skipped members named. groups_guard G0-G5 GREEN,
compile-refusal RED.

## 23 Sep 2026 — PROPOSAL (not built): a SEQUENCED control transport, to close the alias-vs-gain overwrite
Today every control command to a Link is ONE file, `ctrl-cmd-<uid>.json`, written with replaceWithText and stamped with
a seq that is epoch SECONDS; the Link polls at 30 Hz, applies, deletes it and acks. Two commands written before that
poll - an alias push and a mixer gain, say - leave only the second, and two in the same second additionally collide on
the "duplicate seq, already applied" refusal. The same shape cost the 20 Sep round a delete (link_state_guard L1b).

1. Replace the single file with a per-Link RING, `ctrl-ring-<uid>.bin`: a small header plus 32 fixed 1 KB slots, mapped
   exactly like the meter ring, with the JSON PAYLOAD unchanged inside each slot - the envelope changes, not the schema.
2. Producer: claim the next write index, fill the slot, publish with the even/odd seqlock LinkMeterFrame already uses,
   so a half-written slot is never visible.
3. seq becomes a MONOTONIC counter in the ring header instead of epoch seconds, so two commands in one second get
   distinct ids and the duplicate-seq refusal stops firing on honest traffic.
4. Consumer: each poll drains EVERY unread slot in order and applies them in that order, so an alias and a gain issued
   a millisecond apart both land, in the order sent.
5. The ack keeps its present shape and names the HIGHEST applied seq, so nothing on the reading side has to change.
6. A FULL ring refuses the write and says so to the caller - the one thing the current path cannot do is tell anyone it
   dropped a command.
7. Idempotence rides along: each entry carries the cmdId the chain commands already use, and the Link's existing
   last-64-applied list skips a replayed entry.
8. Chain commands (`chain-cmd-<uid>.json`) move into the same ring with a class tag, which closes the paused-Link
   overwrite by construction rather than by the re-send at deselect.
9. Back-compat: V2 writes the ring only when the Link's registry row advertises `ringCtrl=1` (one spare bit, the same
   shape as dialCapable), and writes the old single file otherwise, so a 21n-or-older Link keeps working untouched.
10. Nothing about placement, gain, alias, slotWet or pre-gain semantics changes; this is purely how the message gets
    there, and it is the prerequisite the keep-level transport op should be built ON, not beside.

**The guard it needs** (two processes, the alias_mirror_guard / lease_id_guard pattern - a real LinkProcessor against
the Link archive, a real EchoJayProcessor against the V2 archive): with the Link's timer PAUSED, V2 writes four
commands back to back inside one second - alias "Lead Vox", gain -6, active off, alias "BV 1" - then the Link resumes.
GREEN requires all four applied IN ORDER (displayAlias "BV 1", gainDb -6, linkOn false), the ack naming the highest
seq, and a 33rd command onto a full ring REFUSED with a stated reason. RED on the current tree is BEHAVIOURAL and is
exactly Sean's bug: only the last write survives, so displayAlias is empty and the gain never arrives.

## 23 Sep 2026 — OBSERVATION (no code): why the server cannot dial Auto-Tune Pro's Scale to Minor
**The probe, verbatim.** `EchoJayProbe "Auto-Tune Pro" "AudioUnit:Effects/aumf,AT81,VST " 76720177 --list-params`
(signed probe, `arch -x86_64`; the scan holds the AU at v10.5.0, the registry map at v11.5.1): **112 parameters**, and
the three asked for read:
```
1	Scale		2147483647	0
2	Key		2147483647	0
3	Detune		2147483647	0
```
(index, name, label, numSteps, isDiscrete). **Every one of the 112 is continuous** - Antares publishes no step count
for anything in this plugin.
`--list-steps 1`, `--list-steps 2`, `--list-steps 3` each answer, verbatim:
```
refused not a stepped control (numSteps 2147483647, discrete 0)
```
**So there are no step indices to print: the plugin does not expose these as AU steps at all.** The names are not
missing from the map by oversight; they are unreachable under the current probe contract.

**What the server serves today** (read-only /api/params/lookup on `AudioUnit|76720177|11.5.1`: tier same_count, match
product_version_ignored, human map v11.5.1, 114 controls):
- `Scale`: kind **anchored**, range **[19, 31]**, anchors `[[19,0.7125],[24,0.75],[31,0.775]]` - three numbers, no names.
- `Key`: kind **mode**, labels `{C:0, C#:0.1, D:0.2, D#:0.3, E:0.4, F#:0.5, G:0.6, G#:0.7, A:0.8, A#:0.9, B:1}` -
  **eleven** labels for twelve semitones: **F is missing** between E (0.4) and F# (0.5).
- `Detune`: kind anchored, range [-100, 100], 21 even anchors.
- **0 of the 114 controls carry `positions` / `steps` / `choices`.** No local map exists for this plugin
  (param_maps.json holds none), so the turn used the registry's.

**Ten lines: what the client's map builder would need to carry those names.**
1. The probe's step mode is gated on `numSteps != INT_MAX && isDiscrete`; Antares reports neither, so 21k's
   stepped-positions path never fires for Scale, Key or Detune. That gate is the whole blocker.
2. Add a SAMPLED mode beside it - `--list-text <index> [n]` - walking n normalised positions (129 by default) and
   printing `text<TAB>i<TAB>norm<TAB>panelText`, read after the AU settles on the run loop, exactly as step mode does.
3. Runs of identical panel text collapse to ONE entry whose norm is the CENTRE of the run: that is the detent, and
   the run's width is the evidence the control is stepped even though the AU denies it.
4. Declare a control stepped-by-text only when the sample yields >=2 and <=128 distinct texts and no run is narrower
   than 1/256; otherwise leave it continuous and change nothing.
5. `buildMapFpsJson` itself does not change - it is name -> fingerprint only. The names ride the existing ejmap upload
   (POST /api/params/ejmap), whose ingest already accepts `kind: position`.
6. The builder emits the runs as `positions: [[text, normCentre], ...]` beside the existing anchors, and sets
   `kind: "position"` when every value is a name rather than a number.
7. The server can then resolve "Minor" by NAME, and the client's nearest-detent snap (21k, EchoJayParamApply) lands on
   a run centre instead of the nearest of three anchors.
8. Cost: one extra out-of-process run per unmapped stepped-looking control, on the same 30 s bound, never on the
   message thread - the same shape as the list run that already exists.
9. Trust: a sampled position is `setread` only if the read-back after a write returns the same text; otherwise it is
   written as `asked` and the model is told, exactly as the dial tiering does today.
10. Additive by construction: a control that already carries anchors keeps them, and a map without `positions` behaves
    as it does now.

**The guard it would need** (stepped_position_guard, two directions on mock AUs, no network): a mock that reports
numSteps INT_MAX / isDiscrete false while its value text returns "Chromatic" / "Major" / "Minor" over three equal runs
must yield exactly three positions with centres 1/6, 1/2, 5/6 (+-1/256) and `kind: "position"`; a second mock whose
text is a continuous dB read-out must yield NO positions and stay anchored. RED on the current tree is behavioural and
is this observation: the probe answers "refused not a stepped control" and the map carries a bare numeric range.

## ⚖️ BINDING PRINCIPLE (Sean, 23 Sep 2026): SUBTLE AT EVERY STAGE, LOUD ONLY AT THE END
Before the Level and Limiter slots, **no plugin clips, overcooks or works hard**:
- dynamics stages hold **2–3 dB GR by default**;
- saturation and tape act through **wet, not drive**;
- **no stage adds make-up the next must absorb**;
- **no slot input exceeds −3 dBTP**;
- each slot **passes level on at roughly unity** — that is what the per-slot trims exist for.
The **Level + Limiter pair alone** brings the mix to target at −0.1 dBTP.
The loop **never acts on an invalid reading**: a −245 LUFS-S read is SILENCE, not a level — it stops and says
"no reading", it does not compute a trim from it.
After Listen the **per-slot in / out / GR picture must be available**, and anything over the line is **flagged**.
This is the rule the 23 Sep 12:00 distortion fix is built under, once its observation is filed.

## 23 Sep 2026 — OBSERVATION (no fix): the ~12:00 Mix Bus chain, quiet and distorted
**First, the gap, stated plainly.** The loop and ChainHost lines for that session are NOT recoverable. EchoJay logs
through os_log, and the unified store holds **no EchoJay line from the current Pro Tools instance (pid 36612)** - 91
Pro Tools lines in the last 80 minutes, all XPC/Waves chatter. The EchoJay lines that do survive (11:21-11:24) are
from the PREVIOUS Pro Tools instance and from my own guard binaries, which wrote ~1 100 lines into the same window
and are the likely cause of the drop. So I cannot print the per-slot trim lines, the loop's readings, the gains it
wrote, or the GR figures for that build: **they were never captured, and I will not reconstruct them.**

**What IS on disk for that session:**
- `events.jsonl` 12:26:08 - `dial_miss "Abbey Road J37 Tape (s)" reason=readback_mismatch requested=9 manual=["Bias"]`.
  Nine controls were asked of the J37 and the read-back did not agree; Bias could not be verified. **No event for the
  PuigChild 670** (dial_miss only fires on a miss, so its dial reported clean).
- The 12:25 turn's [CHAIN LEVELS], measured on the live input before the build: **input -15.7 LUFS (p10 -18.5,
  p90 -14.5, loudest 3 s -14.2), peak -1.5 dBFS, crest 14.0 dB, heard 1m32s** - a healthy input, not a quiet one.
- Sean's panel reading: IN -245.5 LUFS-S / -12.7 dBTP, OUT -241.3 LUFS-S / -8.5 dBTP, GR 0.0, gain +4.2 dB.
  OUT - IN = **+4.2 dB**, exactly the Level slot's own gain: the panel is self-consistent with **a stopped transport**
  (the tally floors at -200 dB and the K-weighted offset puts silence near -245). Those two numbers are the idle
  floor, not a measurement.

**What the evidence does support, and what it does not.**
1. **The trims ran and moved the chain by exactly -3.0 dB.** The plan said +7.2 dB and the loop wrote +4.2 dB. Since
   21m the proposal is computed from `measured + trimDeltaDb_` - the sum of the unity trims written at that window -
   so a 3.0 dB shortfall IS the trim sum. That is arithmetic from the shipped code, not an inference.
2. **A trim cannot undo distortion, only make it quieter.** The trim is applied inside the blend node to the
   plugin's OUTPUT. A stage driven hard internally (tape drive, a compressor's make-up) keeps every bit of its
   distortion; the trim removes the level that came with it. A chain that is BOTH quiet and distorted, with the
   limiter reading GR 0.0, is exactly that shape: the loudness was taken away downstream, the distortion was not.
3. **The silence hole is real and is mine.** `ChainHost::measureUnityTrims` gates only on `lv.measured` and on the
   readings being finite - **there is no level floor**. The loop's own window refuses quiet audio (a -40 LUFS count
   floor, kCountFloorLufs), but the trim pass at the same window close does not apply it, so a slot sitting at the
   silence floor still yields `out - in` and a trim of up to +-12 dB. That is why a -245 LUFS-S reading is allowed to
   feed a decision at all: nothing in that function asks whether there was any audio to read. The binding principle
   filed today forbids exactly this.
4. **The J37 is the one stage with a measured dialling fault** (read-back mismatch on 9 controls, Bias unverified),
   and Bias/drive is the control that distorts. Nothing measured points at the 670.

**So, of the three: it is "something else", with (a) unproven and (b) partly supported.** The chain's loudness was
removed downstream by the unity trims (-3.0 dB, provable) while a stage upstream was left driven hard (the J37's
dial did not verify), so the result is quiet AND distorted with the limiter idle. Whether the trims were themselves
computed from a silence reading cannot be decided from what survives - but the code permits it, and that alone is a
defect to fix under the new rule.

**What must exist before the next one of these.** The per-slot in/out/GR picture at each Listen has to be written
somewhere that survives (events.jsonl, as the dial events are), not only to os_log - which is the principle's last
clause. Without it this question can only ever be answered by arithmetic and code reading, as it was here.

## 23 Sep 2026 — CORRECTION to the two 660 observations: the reply DID carry an op
My 22 Sep and 23 Sep entries both said the turn "carried no op at all", reading this pair of lines as the whole story:
```
EJStream: done resolvedTurnType=chain_edit chainBlock= stopReason=end_turn
EJChat: CHAIN_EDIT block received
EJChain: extracted block -- 0 ch, 0 slot(s), 0 with settings_structured
```
**That third line reports the BUILD block only.** Its counter walks `chainJson`'s `"chain"` array
(PluginEditor.cpp:27703 and the block above it), and a chain_EDIT turn does not carry one - its payload is the EDIT
block, which the line above it says arrived ("CHAIN_EDIT block received"). So "0 slot(s)" means "no build block", not
"no op", and the correct reading of both turns is: **an edit block arrived and the client rendered its card.**
- **23 Sep 10:48, chat 1790156887774 "add the puigchild 660 with fast compressor settings" (Mix Bus, stereo):** this
  is the turn behind Sean's screenshot - the card reading *"add PuigChild 660 (m) first - sets Threshold 1, Left
  Threshold 0 0 ..."* with an **Apply** button. Only this turn asked for the 660 today (the other four dumps carry the
  name in the feed alone).
- **22 Sep 18:46, chat 1789742419175 (Kick bus):** the same shape, and the op there was the "suggest" one Sean
  reported, refused at Apply for the missing lease id (fixed in 21n as ruling 1a).
**What the corrected reading adds:** the op dialled `Left Threshold 0` - an LED segment of the threshold ladder, not
a control - beside the real `Threshold`. That is precisely the hazard item 6 addresses: until the read-outs are
marked, the map offers seven plausible "Left Threshold n" entries next to the one real Threshold, and the model used
one. The earlier claim that the turn produced nothing was wrong and is withdrawn.
